Conclusion: The proposed improvements significantly enhance the capabilities of Casimir force setups for dark matter research.

Abstract: We propose the use of sphere-and-plate Casimir force measurement setups to detect ultralight vector dark matter. We demonstrate that sub-picometer signals can be parametrically amplified to be detectable. We present a novel improvement that allows the continuous tuning of the natural frequency of the system, opening new research opportunities at the submicron scale. We show that the improved setup can determine the dark matter mass with high accuracy, significantly expand the detectable mass range, and achieve the best sensitivity to date in the $8\times10^{-14}\sim1\times10^{-12}{\rm~ eV}$ mass range.

</details>


### [62] [Macroscopic dark matter constraints for extended mass functions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05626)
*Zachary S. C. Picker,Andrew Buchanan,Melissa Diamond,Joseph Bramante*

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how different mass distributions of macroscopic dark matter impact existing constraints on their properties.

Method: The authors recalibrate constraints by considering the fraction of dark matter in macroscopic forms and analyze varied mass functions to derive implications from their distributions.

Result: Wider mass functions of macroscopic dark matter are found to face significant constraints, particularly from the tail ends of the mass distribution.

Conclusion: The study provides vital computational tools and datasets for further exploration in the characterization of macroscopic dark matter constraints.

Abstract: We compile and recompute a subset of the landscape of constraints for macroscopic dark matter (macros), explicitly including the dependence on the fraction of dark matter in macros $f_{\rm dm}$. This allows us to map these constraints to scenarios with extended mass functions for the macros, which is a generic outcome for many of the assembly processes relevant to macro formation. We compute a number of examples of constraints for extended mass functions, finding that in many cases, wider mass functions can be severely constrained by just the interactions of the tails of the distributions. Finally, we present the code and data required for computing these limits and generating these plots in a publicly accessible repository.

</details>

*Higinio Valle,Cristina Oropeza Barrera,Oscar Ochoa-Valeriano*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The study assesses the potential for discovering a vector-like top partner at a 3 TeV $e^{+}e^{-}$ collider, focusing on event selection using recoil mass. It evaluates discovery potential across various conditions, demonstrating that significant significance can be achieved for lower masses at higher coupling strengths.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the discovery potential of a vector-like top partner at high-energy colliders, particularly in the context of a $3$ TeV $e^{+}e^{-}$ accelerator.

Method: An event selection strategy based on recoil mass is utilized, validated under baseline conditions and analyzed under more realistic parameters like beam polarization and ISR. Fully simulated sample data is employed.

Result: The analysis indicates a statistical significance above $3σ$ for low benchmark masses when the coupling strength $κ_{T}$ exceeds approximately 0.3, highlighting the collider's potential in discovering the top partner.

Conclusion: The study establishes that even in challenging conditions, significant discoveries of the vector-like top partner may be achievable, guiding future experimental efforts and theoretical interpretations.

Abstract: A study of the single production of a vector-like top partner $T$ at a $\sqrt{s}=3$ TeV $e^{+}e^{-}$ collider is presented, using a decay-mode and mass-agnostic event selection built around the recoil mass observable. The analysis relies on fully simulated samples processed through a common reconstruction and event selection chain. The baseline selection is validated without beam polarization or initial-state radiation (ISR). The impact of more realistic running conditions, including ISR together with an $e^{-}$ beam polarization of $-80\%$, is then evaluated and the discovery reach is mapped as a function of the top partner mass $m_{T}$ and coupling strength $κ_{T}$. In the baseline scan, the estimated statistical significance rises above $3σ$ for the lowest benchmark masses once $κ_{T}$ exceeds approximately 0.3.

</details>


### [64] [Fermion mass hierarchy and flavor signals from a $D_4$ dihedral symmetry within a 2HDM-Tx with four-zero textures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05773)
*M. A. Arroyo-Ureña,E. Díaz,J. L. Díaz-Cruz,H. Hernández-Arellano*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: An extended Two-Higgs Doublet Model with $D_4$ symmetry predicts a four-zero texture in fermion mass matrices, suppresses flavor-changing neutral currents, and includes a dark matter candidate. The model fits recent Higgs data and lepton flavor violation constraints, predicting the lepton-flavor violating process $h \to τμ$ at the High-Luminosity LHC.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore a new framework for the fermion mass hierarchy that also accommodates dark matter and satisfies flavor constraints.

Method: Introduce non-equivalent Yukawa matrices to achieve Hermitian four-zero mass texture and analyze phenomenological implications.

Result: Successfully explains neutral resonance excesses at $95$ GeV and $152$ GeV while adhering to current Higgs data and lepton flavor violation limits.

Conclusion: The proposed 2HDM-Tx framework offers a compelling approach to understanding fermion masses and predicts observable lepton-flavor violation at future colliders.

Abstract: We propose an extended Texturized Two-Higgs Doublet Model (2HDM-Tx) where a discrete $D_4$ symmetry predicts a four-zero texture in the fermion mass matrices. In contrast to the standard ansatz where the underlying Yukawa matrices are assumed to be structurally identical, we introduce a novel configuration of non-equivalent matrices that complementarily generate a specific Hermitian four-zero mass structure. This framework naturally suppresses flavor-changing neutral currents and provides a structural rationale for the fermion mass hierarchy, while in addition including a dark matter candidate. As a concrete phenomenological application, we show that the model simultaneously accommodates the persistent neutral resonance excesses at $95\text{ GeV}$ and $152\text{ GeV}$, satisfies current \text{LHC} Higgs data, and is consistent with the stringent upper bounds from lepton flavor violation processes. These combined constraints severely restrict the parameter space of the scalar phenomenology. Finally, we study the prospects for the discovery of the lepton-flavor violating process $h \to τμ$ at the High-Luminosity LHC.

</details>


### [65] [Deconfining Phase Transition under Real Rotation: A Matrix Model Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05865)
*Qianqian Du,Jing He,Yun Guo,Mei Huang,Enke Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a matrix model analyzing the deconfining phase transition in a rotating gluon plasma confined in a cylinder, revealing how rotation affects the deconfining temperature.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the effects of rotation on the deconfining phase transition in gluon plasma, with a focus on how angular velocity and effective mass impact the system.

Method: A matrix model is developed incorporating both perturbative and non-perturbative contributions to analyze the phase transition under conditions of rotation.

Result: Results indicate that the deconfining temperature decreases with increasing angular velocity for a constant effective mass, while a non-monotonic behavior appears with a mass dependent on angular velocity, affecting the temperature profile.

Conclusion: The findings highlight discrepancies between theoretical predictions and lattice simulations regarding the impact of rotation on the deconfining phase transition, indicating a need for further research.

Abstract: We construct a matrix model to study the deconfining phase transition for a pure gluon plasma that is confined in a cylinder of radius ${\cal R}$ and rotating rigidly at a real-valued angular velocity $Ω$, satisfying $\mathcal{R} Ω<1$. The deconfining phase transition arises due to the competition between two terms that constitute the matrix model. The perturbative term comes from the one-loop effective potential computed in the presence of a background field, while the non-perturbative term represents a correction to the perturbative contribution which is brought about by taking into account an effective mass of the gauge fields. Our results show that real rotation induces a radial inhomogeneity of the system and the deconfining temperature $T_c$ drops away from the rotation axis which is consistent with the Tolman-Ehrenfest law. As for the $Ω$-dependence of $T_c$, it relies on our assumptions of the gluon effective mass. For a constant mass, $T_c$ is found to always decrease with increasing $Ω$. A non-monotonic behavior of $T_c$ shows up when a $Ω$-dependent mass is considered, leading to a qualitative change in the region of small angular velocity. In addition, by setting $Ω=0$ to eliminate rotational effects, we also demonstrate that the finite-volume effect reduces the deconfining temperature relative to the infinite-volume limit. Comparisons between our results and those from various lattice simulations and phenomenological models suggest that controversy remains over how the deconfining phase transition is modified by real rotation and further work is required to reach a definite conclusion.

</details>


### [66] [Decipher the nature of glueball candidate $X(2370)$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05908)
*Sheng-Qi Zhang,Bing-Dong Wan,Cong-Feng Qiao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper suggests that $X(2370)$ may be a flavor-singlet hybrid or trigluon glueball based on decay ratio calculations, emphasizing two key resonance ratios for experimental testing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the constituent structure of the $X(2370)$ particle, which is a candidate for a pseudoscalar glueball.

Method: The authors calculate three-pseudoscalar decay ratios and identify resonance-resolved ratios to distinguish between potential structures of $X(2370)$.

Result: Current data favors a flavor-singlet hybrid scenario while allowing for a trigluon glueball, supported by specific decay ratios.

Conclusion: Two resonance-resolved ratios provide a method to experimentally differentiate between compact-tetraquark, hybrid, and trigluon glueball models for $X(2370)$. This addresses the constituent ambiguity effectively.

Abstract: Recent evidence for an approximately flavor-singlet assignment has strengthened the case for $X(2370)$ as a pseudoscalar glueball candidate. However, flavor singletness alone does not uniquely determine its constituent structure. Using calculated three-pseudoscalar decay ratios, we find that current data favor a flavor-singlet hybrid while remaining compatible with a trigluon glueball. More importantly, we identify two resonance-resolved ratios, $a_0(1450)π/[K_0^*(1430)\bar K+\mathrm{c.c.}]$ and $φφ/[b_1(1235)ρ]$, whose predicted hierarchies place the compact-tetraquark, hybrid, and trigluon-glueball realizations in parametrically separated regions. These observables convert the constituent ambiguity of $X(2370)$ into a directly testable experimental question.

</details>


### [67] [Mass Generation for Axion Like Particles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06030)
*Luca Merlo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper reviews the QCD axion and its parameter space, discussing recent exotic axion models that broaden this space and investigating the origin of axion masses and their links to other Standard Model issues.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the limitations of the QCD axion in solving the Strong CP problem and explore new exotic axion models that could expand theoretical possibilities.

Method: A review of current axion research and analysis of mass origin challenges, alongside connections to other Standard Model issues.

Result: Identification of extended parameters for exotic axion models and insights into the mass origins and their implications within the Standard Model framework.

Conclusion: Broadening the understanding of axions could lead to new models and solutions for outstanding problems in particle physics.

Abstract: The most widely accepted solution to the Strong CP problem is the QCD axion, whose traditional parameter space is relatively constrained. However, recent studies on exotic axion models have shown that this space can be significantly extended, opening up a wide range of previously unexplored possibilities for model building. I first review the current status of axions and axion-like particles, then focus on the challenges related to the origin of their masses and explore potential connections to other unresolved issues within the Standard Model.

</details>


### [68] [Improved analyses of the electroweak phase transition and its phenomenology in the Georgi-Machacek model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06139)
*Cheng-Wei Chiang,Kazuki Enomoto*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper explores the electroweak phase transition in the Georgi-Machacek model, demonstrating its potential to alter the phase transition compared to the Standard Model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate the electroweak phase transition differences introduced by the Georgi-Machacek model with isospin-triplet scalar fields.

Method: Utilizes the thermally resummed one-loop effective potential, incorporates custodial symmetry-breaking effects via renormalization group equations, and applies Bayesian analysis for parameter identification.

Result: Identifies viable parameter regions and explores electroweak phase transition implications, including Higgs boson decay modes and detectable signatures in future experiments.

Conclusion: Strong first-order electroweak phase transition scenarios in this model can be tested through observables in upcoming high-energy collider experiments.

Abstract: The Georgi-Machacek model is a promising new physics scenario with isospin-triplet scalar fields, whose effects can qualitatively change the nature of the electroweak phase transition compared with the Standard Model. We examine the electroweak phase transition in this model by using the thermally resummed one-loop effective potential. We also incorporate effects of custodial symmetry breaking due to radiative corrections by evolving the couplings and classical fields according to the renormalization group equations. We identify viable parameter regions by Bayesian analysis using theoretical requirements and the latest experimental bounds. We then investigate the electroweak phase transition in the resulting parameter regions and discuss its phenomenological implications, such as Higgs boson decays into neutral gauge bosons, di-Higgs production at high-energy colliders, and stochastic gravitational waves associated with a first-order electroweak phase transition. We find that parameter points for the strong first-order electroweak phase transition can be verified by these observables at next-generation experiments.

</details>


### [69] [Exothermic dark matter and the 248 keV nuclear recoil in LUX-ZEPLIN](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06153)
*Howard Baer,Vernon Barger*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The research explores a single nuclear-recoil event reported by the LZ experiment, attributing it to dark matter interactions involving a pseudo-Dirac pair. It examines the implications of kinematics on event counts under different halo escape speeds and discusses theoretical constraints on couplings and event rates, proposing argon as a decisive medium for further testing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a viable dark matter interpretation for the observed nuclear-recoil candidate, accounting for the peculiar properties of dark matter interactions and their kinematic implications on detection rates in current experiments.

Method: The study employs a theoretical approach involving coupled Boltzmann equations and eigenchannel methods to analyze potential dark matter interactions, specifically focusing on downscattering processes and their kinematics in relation to existing experimental data.

Result: The analysis predicts that the event count can vary significantly based on the escape speed of the halo, with a ratio of counts below and above a specific energy threshold, and it derives conditions on the possible dark matter parameters, including coupling strengths and mass constraints.

Conclusion: The findings suggest that previous interpretations may be limited and that argon detectors could provide critical tests for this dark matter model, emphasizing the importance of refining the detection framework for endothermic processes.

Abstract: The single 248-keV nuclear-recoil candidate reported by LZ is reproduced by dark matter that downscatters, with the relic in the excited state of a pseudo-Dirac pair and a splitting δ\simeq-350\keV. Every interpretation so far advanced is endothermic and places the splitting within a few percent of the xenon kinematic ceiling, so that the predicted count changes from zero to 143 times its central value as the halo escape speed runs from 500 to 600 km/s. The downscattering branch has no threshold speed; the same variation changes its count by 0.3 %. The ratio of counts below 70 keV to those above is 0.011, so the null standard search follows from the kinematics. A weak-strength coupling overproduces the event by 8.9\times10^{5}, so the mediator is a dark-sector one, and longevity of the relic excited state requires |δ|<2m_e. Solving the coupled Boltzmann system removes the one free normalization. Rotating to the eigenchannels of the off-diagonal potential gives σ_0=(π/k^2)\sin^2(δ_+-δ_-), whose phase difference is 0.03 to 1.3 rad, so the Born rate exceeds the s-wave unitarity bound where it is used. One event then requires σ_p\simeq2\times10^{-42} cm^2 and a kinetic mixing ε\simeq1\times10^{-6} at m_{A'}=1\GeV, with beam-dump coverage below 0.6\GeV pushing the mediator above that value. The ground state upscatters endothermically through the same operator, which excludes |δ|\lesssim300 keV and makes the published endothermic reading the small-splitting limit of one model. The decisive test is argon, whose first form-factor zero lies at 695 keV against 94.6 keV for xenon; argon yields 4 to 6 events per tonne-year near 250 keV, where the endothermic reading is forbidden at any exposure.

</details>


### [70] [Inelastic dark matter and baryon flavor symmetry in light of LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) experiment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06171)
*Hyun Min Lee*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a novel inelastic dark matter model within a $U(1)'$ baryon flavor symmetry extension of the Standard Model, capable of explaining observed phenomena like the LZ nuclear recoil event and maintaining compatibility with existing detection limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore dark matter models beyond the Standard Model that can simultaneously explain observed events and adhere to existing experimental constraints.

Method: The authors propose a framework utilizing a local baryon flavor $U(1)'$ symmetry, integrating extra vector-like quarks and a Dirac singlet as dark matter, with a focus on how spontaneous symmetry breaking induces inelastic dark matter properties.

Result: The model offers a viable parameter space accommodating a $1 TeV$ dark matter candidate that aligns with the LZ nuclear recoil event while adhering to relic density and collider constraints, predicting a light $Z'$ boson with mass between $10-20$ GeV.

Conclusion: The framework suggests potential avenues for collider searches for the predicted $Z'$ boson, indicating that the model could be testable in upcoming experiments.

Abstract: We present a novel model for inelastic dark matter in a simple extension of the Standard Model (SM) with a local $U(1)'$ baryon flavor symmetry, such as $U(1)_{B_i-B_j}$ with $i\neq j$. In this framework, we realize a realistic flavor structure for the mixing Yukawa couplings due to higher dimensional operators or renormalizable couplings between the SM quarks and extra vector-like quarks that are anomaly-free by themselves. We regard a Dirac singlet fermion as being dark matter and obtaining a small mass splitting for inelastic dark matter, only after the $U(1)'$ symmetry is broken spontaneously. In particular, in the scenarios of heavy dark matter of $1\,{\rm TeV}$ mass, we show that there is a consistent parameter space for explaining both the LZ nuclear recoil event and the correct relic density for dark matter and the existing collider and indirect detection bounds are satisfied at the same time. In this case, we predict a relatively light $Z'$ boson with $10-20\,{\rm GeV}$ mass and a weak gauge coupling, suggesting a dedicated search at colliders.

</details>


### [71] [Planck-Scale Signatures in Vacuum Neutrino Oscillations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06203)
*Bipin Singh Koranga,Imran Khan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a new framework for analyzing two-flavour vacuum neutrino oscillations affected by a weak, flavour-blind Planck-scale mass correction, using a perturbative approach akin to weak Earth-matter effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the impact of a dimension-five Weinberg operator on neutrino oscillations and to distinguish quantum-gravity contributions from traditional matter effects.

Method: A closed-form perturbative framework is developed, allowing for analytical expressions of oscillation parameters under the influence of Planck-scale corrections, independent of baseline lengths.

Result: The Planck-scale correction affects the oscillation phase and conversion probability, with a unique linear increase in phase mismatch with propagation distance, providing sensitivity projections for future neutrino experiments.

Conclusion: The findings serve as a valuable tool for probing Planck-scale mass corrections and distinguishing their effects from traditional matter-induced variations in neutrino oscillations.

Abstract: We present a closed-form perturbative framework for two-flavour vacuum neutrino oscillations in the presence of a weak, flavour-blind Planck-scale mass correction generated by the dimension-five Weinberg operator of the Standard Model Effective Field Theory. The perturbative treatment is developed in direct structural analogy with approaches used to describe weak Earth-matter effects. We derive simple analytical expressions for the modification of the oscillation length, the oscillation phase, and the flavour-conversion probability, valid for arbitrary neutrino mixing and baseline lengths. Unlike matter-induced perturbations, the Planck-scale correction is constant along the neutrino trajectory. Consequently, its effect on the transition probability remains bounded and independent of the baseline at the amplitude level, while the associated modification of the effective mass-squared splitting produces a phase mismatch that increases linearly with propagation distance. We further present illustrative order-of-magnitude sensitivity projections for next-generation long-baseline and reactor neutrino experiments, including DUNE, Hyper-Kamiokande, and JUNO. Our results indicate that the accumulated phase drift provides a particularly promising observable for probing Planck-scale mass corrections and for distinguishing a possible quantum-gravity contribution from conventional matter-induced effects.

</details>


### [72] [Nodal filtering in open-charm decays of the $ψ(4040)$--$ψ(4160)$ system](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06377)
*Bing-Dong Wan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The study explores hadronic decay channels in the $ψ(4040)$--$ψ(4160)$ system using a Bethe-Salpeter framework, revealing insights into the momentum-space structure of quark systems through decay patterns and amplitude behaviors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate how hadronic decay channels can provide insights into the momentum-space structure of confined quark systems, particularly in the $ψ(4040)$--$ψ(4160)$ complex.

Method: An instantaneous Bethe-Salpeter framework is combined with a relativistic $^3P_0$ decay model to analyze the decay channels and corresponding amplitudes in the open-charm sector.

Result: Findings include a two-state level-repulsion description, channel-dependent overlap kernels acting as momentum-space filters, and an observed reversal in width ratios in nonstrange and strange sectors, demonstrating distinct amplitude behaviors across channels.

Conclusion: These results indicate that open-charm decays serve as an effective probe for understanding momentum-space wave-function structures in heavy quarkonium.

Abstract: Hadronic decay channels can provide direct information on the momentum-space structure of confined quark systems. We investigate this possibility in the $ψ(4040)$--$ψ(4160)$ system using an instantaneous Bethe--Salpeter (BS) framework combined with a relativistic $^3P_0$ decay model. The BS solutions yield nearby bare $3\,^3S_1$ and $2\,^3D_1$ states at 4051 and 4110 MeV, respectively, whose opposite displacements from the physical vector states motivate an effective two-state level-repulsion description. By comparing the matched open-charm channels $D\bar D$, $D\bar D^*+{\rm c.c.}$, $D_s\bar D_s$, and $D_s\bar D_s^*+{\rm c.c.}$ with the same leading $P$-wave threshold behavior, we show that channel-dependent overlap kernels act as momentum-space nodal filters. At $M=4146$ MeV, the $PV/PP$ width ratio is 0.253 in the nonstrange sector but 1.63 in the strange sector, reversing the ordering expected from phase space alone. The momentum-resolved amplitudes reveal that different channels weight opposite sides of the same $2D$ nodal region differently, leading to distinct cancellation patterns and a common recoil condition, $P_f\simeq0.775$ GeV, for charge-resolved $D\bar D^*$ amplitude zeros. These results establish open-charm decays as a probe of momentum-space wave-function structures in heavy quarkonium.

</details>


### [73] [A Mean-Field Approach to the Dielectric Response of Bulk Superconductors for Light Dark Matter Direct Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06423)
*Chengyao Gan,Hui Li,Wenjing Li,Zheng-Liang Liang,Lin Zhang,Fawei Zheng*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives the dielectric function for bulk superconductors to assess the validity of the Lindhard function in dark matter detection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the appropriateness of the free-electron Lindhard dielectric function in superconductors for dark matter detection.

Method: The study employs the Bardeen-Cooper-Schrieffer (BCS) framework to derive the dielectric function while considering Bogoliubov quasiparticle coherence factors within the random-phase approximation.

Result: A systematic comparison indicates that the Lindhard function aligns well with the derived dielectric function for energy depositions above a threshold of $ω	extgreater5Δ$ in aluminum and tungsten silicide (WSi).

Conclusion: The Lindhard function is established as a robust approximation for superconducting dark matter detectors in the specified energy regime.

Abstract: The dielectric function is central to describing many-body screening effects in dark matter (DM) direct detection with condensed matter targets. Current superconducting detector analyses employ the free-electron Lindhard dielectric function to model in-medium effects, an approximation whose validity in the superconducting state remains untested. We derive the electronic dielectric function for bulk superconductors within the Bardeen-Cooper-Schrieffer (BCS) framework, incorporating the full Bogoliubov quasiparticle coherence factors in the random-phase approximation. A systematic comparison with the Lindhard function for aluminum and tungsten silicide (WSi) reveals good agreement for energy depositions $ω\gtrsim5Δ$, establishing the Lindhard function as a robust approximation for superconducting DM detectors operating in this regime.

</details>


### [74] [Radiative double inverse seesaw and dark matter in an alternative gauged $U(1)_{B-L}$ model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06494)
*Hiroshi Okada,Labh Singh*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper presents a framework for generating tiny neutrino masses using a double inverse seesaw mechanism in a gauged $U(1)_{B-L}$ model, introducing dark matter candidates and analyzing their detection constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore a theoretical framework that explains tiny neutrino masses and provides candidates for dark matter.

Method: The model involves right-handed neutrinos with non-universal $B-L$ charges, introducing vector-like fermions and inert singlet scalars in loop processes. A detailed examination of both fermionic and bosonic dark matter scenarios is conducted, focusing on interactions mediated by the $B-L$ gauge boson.

Result: The analysis indicates that fermionic dark matter is strongly favored; meanwhile, scenarios involving bosonic dark matter via the $Z'$ portal are largely ruled out by recent detection experiments.

Conclusion: The proposed framework is viable for explaining neutrino masses while identifying dark matter candidates, with a clear preference for fermionic dark matter in current experimental contexts.

Abstract: We propose a concrete theoretical framework for the double inverse seesaw mechanism within an alternative gauged $U(1)_{B-L}$ model, where the tiny neutrino masses emerge radiatively at the loop level in accordance with the 't Hooft naturalness criterion. By assigning non-universal $B-L$ charges of $(-4, -4, 5)$ to the right-handed neutrinos, we introduce vector-like fermions and two inert singlet scalars that circulate in the loop to generate the required mass terms. The spontaneous breaking of the $U(1)_{B-L}$ symmetry leaves a remnant $Z_2$ symmetry, which naturally stabilizes these new particles as dark matter (DM) candidates. We systematically investigate both fermionic and bosonic DM scenarios, assuming their interactions are predominantly mediated by the $B-L$ gauge boson ($Z'$). Our comprehensive analysis of the relic density, direct detection, and collider bounds reveals that the fermionic DM scenario is strongly favored. In contrast, the bosonic DM via the $Z'$ portal is severely constrained and largely ruled out by the latest direct detection experiments such as LZ, PandaX-4T, and XENONnT.

</details>


### [75] [The Inert Doublet Model of Dark Matter and the LUX-ZEPLIN High-Recoil Event](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06571)
*Lei Wang,Yang Xiao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The LZ search reported a nuclear-recoil event interpreted as inelastic dark matter scattering in the IDM model, identifying a viable high-mass region with specific mass parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To interpret the recent LZ-nuclear recoil event in terms of dark matter scattering, specifically within the inert doublet model.

Method: A systematic examination and profile-likelihood analysis were conducted to identify viable masses and parameters in the inert doublet model that match the LZ event.

Result: A high-mass IDM region was identified with a profile best fit at $m_H=1080~{m GeV}$ and $m_A-m_H=369~{m keV}$.

Conclusion: The IDM can consistently explain the LZ nuclear-recoil event under current theoretical and experimental constraints.

Abstract: The recent LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) search reported a nuclear-recoil event near $248~{\rm keV}$. Such a high-recoil event can be interpreted in terms of endothermic inelastic dark matter scattering, which typically requires a mass splitting of order a few hundred keV between the initial and final dark states. The inert doublet model (IDM) provides a natural realization of this scenario through the $Z$-mediated transition $H+N\to A+N$. In this work, we systematically examine whether this interpretation can be consistently realized in the IDM under the relevant theoretical constraints and existing experimental bounds. We perform a detailed profile-likelihood analysis of the LZ event, and find a viable high-mass IDM region with a profile best fit at $m_H=1080~{\rm GeV}$ and $m_A-m_H=369~{\rm keV}$.

</details>


### [76] [Inelastic Self-interacting Dark Matter and LUX-ZEPLIN 248 keV Event in a Dirac Modular Inverse Seesaw](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06825)
*Pritam Das,Biswajit Karmakar,Satyabrata Mahapatra,Partha Kumar Paul*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper presents a new framework linking Dirac neutrino masses and self-interacting dark matter (SIDM) using $A_{4}$ modular symmetry, proposing significant implications for neutrino mass, dark matter properties, and cosmology.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To connect the origins of Dirac neutrino masses with self-interacting dark matter, addressing both phenomena simultaneously within a coherent theoretical framework.

Method: Utilizes an $A_{4}$ modular symmetry to enforce Dirac neutrinos through an Inverse Seesaw mechanism, and couples a singlet scalar to a vector-like fermion DM candidate to introduce inelastic scattering.

Result: The proposed model fits recent experimental signals from the LUX-ZEPLIN experiment while addressing small-scale structure issues in Cold DM, and predicts cosmological implications including gravitational waves from domain wall annihilation.

Conclusion: The framework successfully correlates neutrino properties with dark matter characteristics and cosmological phenomena, while minimizing free parameters and predicting observable gravitational wave signatures.

Abstract: We propose a novel framework that simultaneously addresses the origin of Dirac neutrino masses and the nature of self-interacting dark matter (SIDM). The model is based on an $A_{4}$ modular symmetry to ensure the Diracness of neutrinos as well as the stability of the DM. The neutrino sector realizes a Dirac Inverse Seesaw mechanism where the smallness of the neutrino mass is governed by the vacuum expectation value (VEV) of a singlet scalar $φ$. This same scalar couples to a vector-like fermion DM candidate, inducing a tiny Majorana mass splitting that renders the DM pseudo-Dirac and inelastic. Crucially, the scalar also acts as a light mediator for DM self-interactions, potentially solving the small-scale structure problems of Cold DM. In light of the recent 248 keV nuclear-recoil event, LZ230616, observed by LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) DM direct detection experiment, we demonstrate that our inelastic SIDM parameter space naturally accommodates this signal via endothermic scattering kinematics. Furthermore, the spontaneous breaking of the dark parity required for this inelasticity produces a network of cosmological domain walls. We show that the explicit symmetry breaking needed to safely annihilate these walls generates a stochastic gravitational wave background. The non-holomorphic modular symmetry reduces the free parameters, correlating neutrino observables, addressing DM phenomenology, $ΔN_{\rm eff}$, and gravitational-wave signatures.

</details>


### [77] [Multi-Messenger and Paleo-Detector Probes of the LZ Dark Matter Signal](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06640)
*Meiwen Yang,Quan-feng Wu,Yue-Lin Sming Tsai,Yi-Zhong Fan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The LUX-ZEPLIN collaboration reported an excess in nuclear recoil energy, which can be explained by a leptophobic inelastic dark matter model that adheres to detection constraints while predicting observable effects in paleo-detectors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the reported excess in nuclear recoil energy from LUX-ZEPLIN and to propose a viable dark matter model that avoids existing detection constraints.

Method: The study introduces a leptophobic inelastic dark matter model with a vector mediator, exploring parameter spaces with specific mass constraints and analyzing multi-messenger signals.

Result: The proposed model aligns with the observed excess, predicting particular recoil tracks in paleo-detectors while also adhering to limits set by IceCube on neutrino fluxes.

Conclusion: If the LZ anomaly is confirmed, it suggests the presence of a dark sector characterized by inelastic transitions and leptophobic couplings, with paleodetectors potentially providing definitive evidence.

Abstract: The LUX-ZEPLIN collaboration recently reported a $2.6σ$ excess at a nuclear recoil energy of $248\ \text{keV}$, challenging the standard elastic WIMP paradigm. We show that a leptophobic inelastic dark matter model with a vector mediator naturally explains this anomaly while evading all low-energy direct detection constraints. The viable parameter space features $m_{χ_1}>100~\text{GeV}$ and mass splitting $δ\sim 200\text{--}300\ \text{keV}$. Our multi-messenger analysis reveals that neither the heavy ($10\ \text{GeV}$) nor light ($10\ \text{MeV}$) mediator scenario reproduces the Fermi-LAT Galactic center excess, and neutrino fluxes remain consistent with IceCube limits. Both scenarios predict recoil tracks near 80 nm in lead-bearing paleo-detectors. At an optimistic uranium-238 concentration of \(10^{-12}\) g/g, 10 mg Gyr of exposure could yield an excess above the estimated background even when astrophysical signals are negligible. The LZ anomaly, if confirmed, points toward a dark sector with inelastic transitions and leptophobic couplings, with the paleo-detector providing the smoking-gun evidence.

</details>


### [78] [High-Energy Nuclear Recoils from Boosted Dark Matter for the LZ 248-keV Event: Beyond the Halo-Dependent High-Velocity Tail](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06890)
*Haider Alhazmi,Doojin Kim,Kyoungchul Kong,Jong-Chul Park,Seodong Shin*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The LZ Collaboration reports a nuclear-recoil candidate linked to dark matter interactions, exploring both traditional heavy dark matter and a new approach involving light boosted dark matter.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding dark matter interactions, particularly from a recent LUX-ZEPLIN Experiment signal.

Method: The study examines multi-component boosted dark matter scenarios, utilizing elastic and inelastic scattering off xenon nuclei and proposes models that can confine the nuclear recoil signal.

Result: The proposed light boosted dark matter models can explain the observed recoil with specific kinematic constraints, suppressing low- and high-energy side events by focusing on threshold scattering.

Conclusion: Benchmark spectra indicate that light boosted dark matter could provide viable explanations for dark matter interactions, with implications for further experimental tests using various target materials.

Abstract: The LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) Collaboration has reported a nuclear-recoil candidate at $E_R=248\pm23_{\rm stat}\pm23_{\rm sys}$ keV, with a maximum local significance of $3.4σ$ and a global significance of $2.6σ$. A prominent interpretation invokes heavy halo dark matter near an inelastic threshold and therefore depends sensitively on the poorly constrained high-speed tail of the Galactic velocity distribution. In this Letter, we propose a qualitatively different possibility based on light boosted dark matter (BDM), whose incident energy is determined primarily by the dark-sector mass spectrum. We consider multi-component scenarios in which the boosted state scatters elastically or inelastically off xenon nuclei. For elastic scattering, pseudoscalar-mediated momentum dependence suppresses low-energy recoils. Near-threshold endothermic scattering of a nearly monochromatic BDM flux can instead confine the signal between kinematically determined recoil endpoints, suppressing events in both the low- and high-energy sidebands. The upscattered state may furthermore decay invisibly within the dark sector, preserving a single-nuclear-recoil signature without requiring it to be detector-stable. We present representative benchmark spectra and discuss complementary tests using other target nuclei and large-volume liquid-scintillator experiments.

</details>


### [79] [Inverse seesaw and inflation in a supersymmetric 331 model from $SU(6)$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06734)
*Imtiaz Khan,Tianjun Li*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper presents a supersymmetric $SU(6)$ model with an intermediate symmetry phase, exploring the dynamics of neutrinos, gauge mediation, and inflation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate the relationships among neutrinos, gauge mediation, unification, and symmetry-breaking within a unified theoretical framework.

Method: Construct a $N=1$ supersymmetric $SU(6)$ theory incorporating an intermediate phase and analyze the Dirac matrix properties and implications for neutrinos and inflation.

Result: The model demonstrates that an antisymmetric Dirac matrix leads to one massless neutrino and quantifies how corrections lift this mode, maintaining perturbative gauge couplings and stable scalar directions.

Conclusion: The study provides analytical relations between inverse seesaw mechanisms, gauge evolution, and inflationary dynamics, under specific assumptions about the underlying Higgs and threshold sectors.

Abstract: We propose a $N=1$ supersymmetric $SU(6)$ theory with an intermediate $(SU(3)_C\times SU(3)_L\times U(1)_X)$ phase, in which common fields and operators relate the neutrinos, gauge mediation, unification, and symmetry-breaking sectors. The inverse-seesaw Dirac matrix is antisymmetric and thus has rank two, leaving one massless neutrino at leading order. The leading symmetry-allowed correction lifts this zero mode through its projection onto the left and right null vectors of the Dirac matrix, resulting in a quartic suppression by the ratio of the intermediate and ultraviolet scales. The complete unified messenger multiplets preserve the relative one-loop gauge-coupling crossing, while the supersymmetry-breaking spurion entering the messenger sector also determines the scale of lepton-number violation. The coupling governing (331) breaking fixes both the inflationary normalization and the radial mass in the broken vacuum. For the conditional heavy spectrum adopted in the threshold calculations, the gauge couplings remain perturbative. The physical scalar directions transverse to the inflaton are stable within the neutral $((Φ,\barΦ,S))$ subspace. These results provide the analytical scale relations among the inverse seesaw, messenger sector, unified gauge evolution, and inflation, while keeping the assumptions associated with the ultraviolet Higgs and heavy-threshold sectors explicitly.

</details>


### [80] [Inelastic $B-L$ scalar dark matter and the LUX-ZEPLIN event](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06909)
*Nobuchika Okada,Osamu Seto*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper presents an inelastic scalar dark matter model explaining a high-energy recoil event observed by the LZ collaboration, requiring specific conditions for dark matter and gauge coupling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To account for a recent high-energy recoil event reported by the LZ collaboration.

Method: Utilizing an inelastic scalar dark matter model with a gauged $U(1)_{B-L}$ symmetry, focusing on the thermal relic abundance and specific mass relations between dark matter and the gauge boson.

Result: The model predicts a DM-nucleon inelastic cross section of $σ_	ext{SI} 	imes 10^{-45}$ cm${}^2$, aligning well with the observed LZ event.

Conclusion: The proposed scenario can be tested in future collider searches for the $Z^	ext{prime}$ boson resonance.

Abstract: We show that our previously proposed inelastic scalar dark matter (DM) model in gauged $U(1)_{B-L}$ symmetry naturally accounts for the $2.6σ$ high-energy recoil event recently reported by the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) collaboration. To satisfy the thermal relic abundance, TeV-scale scalar DM $S$ with $m_S \simeq m_{Z'}/2$ for resonant annihilation and the gauge coupling constant $g_{B-L} \sim 0.5$ are required, where $m_{Z^\prime}$ is the mass of $U(1)_{B-L}$ gauge boson $Z^{\prime}$. The resulting predicted DM-nucleon inelastic cross section of $σ_\mathrm{SI} \sim 10^{-45}$ cm${}^2$ is in excellent agreement with the LZ event. This scenario can be decisively tested for future collider searches for $Z^\prime$ boson resonance.

</details>


### [81] [GUT-induced FCC signatures of the LUX-ZEPLIN event](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06750)
*Wojciech Kotlarski,Kamila Kowalska,Enrico Maria Sessolo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper discusses the implications of a recent LUX-ZEPLIN observation in the context of pseudo-Dirac dark matter and proposes a model involving a heavy $Z'$ gauge boson, with predicted signals detectable by FCC-$hh$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide an explanation for large recoil energy events observed at LUX-ZEPLIN as related to pseudo-Dirac dark matter and heavier particle states.

Method: Using a specific model based on SU(6) to analyze mass splittings and gauge boson implications, and deriving the $Z'$ signal in the dilepton channel.

Result: Find that the favored mass splitting $δm$ necessitates a $Z'$ gauge boson with a mass ranging in the tens of TeV scale, which is within the detection reach of FCC-$hh$.

Conclusion: The potential discovery of the $Z'$ boson by FCC-$hh$ could validate aspects of GUT models and pseudo-Dirac dark matter scenarios.

Abstract: The recent observation of an event with large recoil energy at LUX-ZEPLIN has found a possible explanation in terms of the inelastic scattering of a pseudo-Dirac particle like the Higgsino of the MSSM. For pseudo-Dirac dark matter, the mass splitting $δm$ is correlated with the mass of some heavier states. While rough dimensional considerations seem to indicate mass ranges of the heavy particles hopelessly out of reach even at future colliders, we point out that this is not the case in GUT-induced scenarios. Working with a specific model based on SU(6), we show that the experimentally favored $δm$ implies the presence of a $Z'$ gauge boson with a mass in the 10s of TeV. We derive the predicted $Z'$ signal in the dilepton channel and we confront it with the sensitivity of the FCC-$hh$ to $Z'$ resonances. We show that the $Z'$ falls squarely in reach of the early discovery potential of the FCC-$hh$.

</details>


### [82] [LZ Nuclear-Recoil Excess from Boosted Light Magnetic Dipole-dipole Dark Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06756)
*Jin-Han Liang,Zuowei Liu,Van Que Tran,Yongheng Xu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The LZ collaboration observed a nuclear-recoil excess at 248 keV, suggesting possible dark matter interactions. The authors propose a boosted velocity distribution to explain this excess with lighter dark matter, improving the fit to the observed spectrum.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the nuclear-recoil excess reported by the LZ collaboration and resolve discrepancies with predicted events at lower recoil energies.

Method: Analytical and model-based approaches to demonstrate the effects of a boosted velocity distribution on dark matter interactions, analyzing how this leads to a better fit for the recoil spectrum.

Result: The introduction of a boosted velocity distribution significantly improves the fit to the LZ recoil data, and allows for the possibility of lighter dark matter, down to the GeV scale.

Conclusion: Boosted dark-sector particles effectively provide a viable explanation for the LZ excess enigmatic findings.

Abstract: The LZ collaboration has reported a nuclear-recoil excess near 248 keV with a global significance of $2.6σ$. Although halo dark matter with a magnetic dipole-dipole interaction and a TeV-scale mass provides the best fit to the excess among the interactions considered by LZ, it predicts a considerable number of events at lower recoil energies, where no excess is observed. We show that a boosted velocity distribution can alleviate this tension and provide a better fit to the LZ recoil spectrum. Moreover, the boost opens up the possibility of explaining the excess with much lighter dark matter, with masses down to the GeV scale. We demonstrate these features first in a model-independent analysis and then realize them in a concrete dark matter model, in which halo dark matter annihilates into on-shell mediators that subsequently decay into boosted dark-sector particles. Our results demonstrate that boosted dark sector particles provide a viable interpretation of the LZ excess.

</details>


### [83] [LZ nuclear recoil event from inelastic singlet-doublet scalar dark matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07451)
*Disha Bandyopadhyay,Debasish Borah,Pankaj Borah*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This study explores a model of singlet-doublet scalar dark matter that can explain a recent high-energy nuclear recoil event reported by LZ, examining its implications for relic abundance, direct detection, and neutrino phenomenology.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a theoretical framework for a recent high-energy nuclear recoil event observed by the LZ collaboration, using a scalar dark matter model.

Method: The authors consider a CP-even mass eigenstate formed from a scalar doublet and a real scalar singlet under an unbroken Z2 symmetry, analyzing the parameter space consistent with relic abundance and elastic scattering limits.

Result: The study identifies a significant parameter space allowing inelastic up-scattering of dark matter consistent with the observed LZ event, and demonstrates an expanded parameter space compared to pure scalar doublet dark matter.

Conclusion: Incorporating $Z_2$-odd right-handed neutrinos can yield interesting phenomenological implications, including insights into light neutrino masses and leptogenesis.

Abstract: We study the possibility of explaining the recently reported high-energy nuclear recoil event by the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) collaboration within the framework of singlet-doublet scalar dark matter (DM). Considering DM to be the CP even mass eigenstate formed out of a scalar doublet and a real scalar singlet, both being odd under an unbroken $Z_2$ symmetry, we find the parameter space of the model consistent with correct relic abundance and direct-detection limits on elastic scattering rates. A large part of the parameter space can also lead to inelastic up-scattering of DM with a rate and recoil energy consistent with the recent LZ event. Depending upon the singlet-doublet mixing, the model allows a much wider range of currently allowed parameter space compared to the pure scalar doublet DM limit. Presence of $Z_2$-odd right-handed neutrinos also leads to other interesting phenomenology related to the origin of light neutrino masses and leptogenesis.

</details>


### [84] [Solar Capture Tests of Inelastic Dark Matter after the LZ High-Recoil Event](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06760)
*Mattia Di Mauro,Halim Shaikh*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The LZ Collaboration reported a $248$ keV nuclear-recoil candidate linked to endothermic dark matter, examining thermal Higgsinos, pseudo-Dirac fermions, and neutron-philic scattering. The thermal Higgsino is excluded due to high energy constraints, while other interpretations remain viable.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To analyze the implications of a $248$ keV nuclear-recoil event associated with endothermic dark matter interpretations and ascertain their viability against solar-capture constraints.

Method: Investigated three dark matter scenarios: thermal Higgsinos, thermal pseudo-Dirac fermions with vector interactions, and neutron-philic scattering. Evaluated solar capture rates and compared them to previously established thresholds from experiments such as IceCube.

Result: The canonical thermal Higgsino model is excluded based on energy thresholds. The pseudo-Dirac fermion aspects show significantly suppressed annihilation, while neutron-philic interactions suggest possible interpretations but still fall below stringent limits set by IceCube.

Conclusion: Solar capture rules out thermal Higgsinos as a viable candidate, but does not dismiss other endothermic dark matter interpretations for the LZ candidate.

Abstract: The LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) Collaboration has reported a $248$ keV nuclear-recoil candidate for which endothermic dark matter (DM) gives some of the largest local significances. We study Solar-capture constraints on three simple interpretations: a thermal Higgsino, a thermal pseudo-Dirac fermion with off-diagonal vector interactions, and neutron-philic endothermic spin-dependent scattering through $\mathcal{O}_4$. The canonical full-density thermal Higgsino is excluded: its LZ-preferred splitting near $0.38,\mathrm{MeV}$ lies below the IceCube requirement, $δ\gtrsim0.51$--$0.56,\mathrm{MeV}$. For the pseudo-Dirac benchmark we find $C_\odot=1.48\times10^{20},\mathrm{s}^{-1}$, but two-state kinetics strongly suppresses annihilation. Including thermally assisted $χ_1\toχ_2$ re-excitation gives a maximum $Γ_A\simeq4.1\times10^{14},\mathrm{s}^{-1}$, about $1.8\times10^5$ below the adopted IceCube $b\bar b$ limit. For neutron-philic $\mathcal{O}_4$ scattering at $m_χ=1$ TeV and $δ=300$ keV, finite-temperature capture gives $C_\odot=3.98\times10^{17},\mathrm{s}^{-1}$, with spin-response range $(1.77$--$9.72)\times10^{17},\mathrm{s}^{-1}$. Even in equilibrium, the upper edge remains about a factor $154$ below the adopted $b\bar b$ limit, while an aggressive no-finite-$q$ suppression plus upper-spin stress test still leaves a factor about $19$. The $b\bar b$ mapping is used as a representative hadronic template for the two non-Higgsino benchmarks. Solar capture therefore excludes the thermal-Higgsino interpretation but does not generically exclude endothermic explanations of the LZ candidate.

</details>


### [85] [Inelastic Singlet-Doublet Fermion Dark Matter in light of the 248 keV LZ event](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07800)
*Debasish Borah,Sujit Kumar Sahoo,Narendra Sahu,Shashwat Sharma*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The LZ collaboration's detection of a dark matter-nucleus scattering event suggests inelastic scattering is responsible for the observed energy levels, explored through a singlet-doublet fermion model with implications for Majorana neutrino masses.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the absence of low-energy events in dark matter interactions, focusing on the inelastic scattering process which aligns with recent experimental findings.

Method: The model employs a singlet-doublet fermion framework featuring two pseudo-Dirac states that prohibit elastic scattering via the Majorana nature of the lighter state, while allowing inelastic scattering through $Z$ exchange, with additional elastic processes suppressed by Higgs mechanisms.

Result: This study shows that the singlet-doublet fermion model can explain the LZ observations effectively, incorporating the inelastic scattering dynamics and capturing the underlying physics of dark matter interactions and neutrino masses.

Conclusion: The proposed model not only aligns with observed data but also introduces new mechanisms for explaining Majorana neutrino masses, thus expanding the theoretical framework for understanding dark matter.

Abstract: Recently, the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) collaboration reported the observation of a single dark matter (DM)-nucleus scattering event at a nuclear recoil energy of $248\pm23_{\rm stat}\pm23_{\rm sys}$ keV, corresponding to an exposure of 2.84 tonne-year. The absence of events at lower nuclear recoil energies in the predicted spectrum is naturally explained if the underlying process is inelastic DM-nucleus scattering. Motivated by this, we investigate the singlet-doublet fermion DM model, in which the DM consists of two pseudo-Dirac states: the Majorana nature of the lighter state forbids tree level $Z$-mediated elastic scattering identically, while the same states enable inelastic DM-nucleus scattering via $Z$ exchange, with any residual elastic scattering proceeding only through a suppressed Higgs-mediated channel. We further extend the model with a $Z_2$-even scalar triplet, which is responsible both for generating the pseudo-Dirac splitting and for realizing Majorana neutrino masses via the Type-II seesaw mechanism.

</details>


### [86] [Revisiting two-body charmed anti-charmed baryonic $B$ decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08053)
*Chun-Khiang Chua*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper revisits the analysis of $ar{B}	o B_car{B}_c$ decays by incorporating $b	o uar{u} q$ contributions alongside $b	o car{c} q$. It highlights the significant amplification of $b	o uar{u} q$ influence in $ΔS=0$ transitions, aiding in decay rate predictions for specific baryonic decay processes. Measuring these decays is vital for understanding the impact of $b	o uar{u} q$ contributions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enhance the understanding of two-body charmed anti-charmed baryonic $ar{B}	o B_c ar{B}_c$ decays by incorporating previously overlooked contributions.

Method: Utilizing a topological amplitude approach to evaluate decay amplitudes and include contributions from both the $b	o car{c} q$ and $b	o uar{u} q$ processes.

Result: The study finds that the $b	o uar{u} q$ contributions significantly impact $ΔS=0$ transition rates, potentially leading to higher decay rates for specific processes than previously calculated based solely on $b	o car{c} d$.

Conclusion: Measuring the decay rates in question will clarify the contributions of $b	o uar{u} q$ in $ar{B}$ decays, enhancing the overall understanding of charmed baryonic decay processes.

Abstract: The study of two-body charmed anti-charmed baryonic $\overline B\to {\bf B}_c \overline {\bf B}_c$ decays using the topological amplitude approach is revisited. We include the $b\to u\bar u q$ contributions, in addition to the $b\to c\bar c q$ contributions, to these decays. The topological decomposition of $\overline B\to {\bf B}_c(\bf {\bar 3_f}) \overline {\bf B}_c(\bf { 3_f})$, ${\bf B}_c(\bf 6_f) \overline {\bf B}_c(\bf { 3_f})$, ${\bf B}_c(\bf {\bar 3_f}) \overline {\bf B}_c(\bf {\bar 6_f})$ and ${\bf B}_c(\bf 6_f) \overline {\bf B}_c(\bf {\bar 6_f})$ decay amplitudes are updated accordingly. Although the $b\to u\bar u q$ contributions are CKM suppressed in $ΔS=-1$ transitions, their effects are significantly amplified in $ΔS=0$ transitions, as the relative size of the CKM factors in $b\to u\bar u q$ and $b\to c\bar c q$ contributions in $ΔS=0$ transitions is enlarged by $λ^{-2}$, roughly a factor of 20, from the one in $ΔS=-1$ transitions. Present data in $\overline B\to {\bf B}_c(\bf {\bar 3_f}) \overline {\bf B}_c(\bf { 3_f})$ decay rates allow or even slightly prefer non-negligible $b\to u\bar u q$ contributions. These $b\to u\bar u q$ contributions can allow much larger rates for $B^-\to Ξ_c^0 \bar Ξ_c^{-}$, $\overline B{}_s^0\to Λ_c^+ \bar Ξ_c^{-}$ and $\overline B{}^0\to Ξ_c^+ \bar Ξ_c^{-}$ decays than those only have $b\to c\bar c d$ contributions. Measuring these decays will be interesting and useful in clarifying the role of $b\to u\bar u q$ contributions in two-body charmed anti-charmed baryonic $\overline B\to {\bf B}_c \overline {\bf B}_c$ decays.

</details>


### [87] [Reggeization of quarks from next-to-eikonal high-energy QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06833)
*Tolga Altinoluk,Guillaume Beuf,Jules Favrel,Michael Fucilla*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a detailed formulation of quark Reggeization in QCD using Wilson lines, emphasizing the evolution properties and interactions between quark and gluon degrees of freedom.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the structure and dynamics of quark Reggeization within Quantum Chromodynamics, particularly through the lens of Wilson lines and their evolution.

Method: The authors use a next-to-eikonal operator based on a semi-infinite Wilson line to develop an interpolating operator for Reggeized quarks, deriving its nonlinear rapidity evolution via the background-field method.

Result: In the dilute regime, the Reggeized quark exhibits distinct behavior between its positive and negative components, with positive components showing Regge-pole evolution, while negative components exhibit mixing with gluons. In the planar limit, this mixing is suppressed, allowing Reggeization to occur independently of signature projections. The formalism is also applicable to massive quarks, leading to the derivation of a coordinate-space evolution kernel.

Conclusion: The study reveals that the Reggeized-quark interpolating operator remains an eigenstate of rapidity evolution up to next-to-leading logarithmic accuracy, affirming the robustness of the formalism in various contexts.

Abstract: We develop a comprehensive Wilson-line formulation of quark Reggeization in QCD. Starting from a next-to-eikonal operator built from a semi-infinite Wilson line and a background-quark insertion, we identify an interpolating operator for the Reggeized quark and derive its nonlinear rapidity evolution using the background-field method. In the dilute regime, its positive-signature component exhibits Regge-pole evolution governed by the quark Regge trajectory, whereas the negative-signature sector displays mixing between quark and gluon degrees of freedom, in accordance with its known Regge-cut structure. In the planar limit, this mixing is suppressed, and Reggeization emerges without an explicit signature projection, recovering signature degeneracy. We illustrate the universality of the construction by extracting the same Reggeized-quark operator from a more general Wilson-line operator describing a gluon-to-quark transition. Furthermore, we extend the formalism to massive quarks, deriving a coordinate-space evolution kernel whose Fourier transform reproduces the massive-quark Regge trajectory. Finally, from the leading operator-mixing structure of the evolution equation and signature arguments, we show that the Reggeized-quark interpolating operator is expected to remain an eigenstate of the rapidity evolution up to next-to-leading logarithmic accuracy.

</details>


### [88] [Probing the $B^{0}$ meson in a hot medium with finite baryon chemical potential](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08299)
*A. Türkan,G. Bozkır,K. Azizi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper quantitatively analyzes the spectroscopic parameters of the $B^{0}$ meson in varying temperature and baryon chemical potential contexts, revealing a non-monotonic behavior in mass and decay constant, culminating in vanishing points influenced by both parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the behavior of the $B^{0}$ meson under hot and dense conditions, which is essential in exploring QCD effects in extreme environments.

Method: Using QCD two-point sum rules, the mass and decay constant of the $B^{0}$ meson are computed, accounting for perturbative and nonperturbative contributions as functions of temperature $T$ and baryon chemical potential $μ_B$.

Result: Both the mass and decay constant initially rise with temperature, peak, and then decrease to zero at high temperatures, with their vanishing points shifting to lower values as $μ_B$ increases. A slight initial increase followed by a decrease is also observed for increasing $μ_B$ at fixed temperatures, with specific intervals identified for vanishing points.

Conclusion: The study provides insights into the behavior of the $B^{0}$ meson in extreme conditions, highlighting the interplay between temperature and baryon chemical potential, and suggesting implications for understanding QCD in high-energy physics.

Abstract: We perform a quantitative analysis of the $B^{0}$ meson spectroscopic parameters in a hot and dense medium. Within the framework of QCD two-point sum rules, we calculate the mass and decay constant of the $B^{0}$ meson with the help of the perturbative spectral density and nonperturbative contributions up to mass dimension five as functions of temperature $T$ and baryon chemical potential $μ_B$. For various fixed values of the baryon chemical potential, our numerical results indicate that both the mass and decay constant of the $B^{0}$ meson initially increase with temperature, reach a maximum, and then gradually decrease before eventually vanishing at sufficiently high temperatures. The corresponding vanishing points are found to be in the temperature intervals $(0.182-0.273)~\mathrm{GeV}$ and $(0.118-0.173)~\mathrm{GeV}$, for the mass and decay constant, respectively. Moreover, these temperatures move toward lower values as the baryon chemical potential increases. On the other hand, for various fixed temperatures, both the mass and decay constant exhibit a slight initial increase with increasing baryon chemical potential, followed by a subsequent decrease, eventually vanishing at sufficiently high chemical potentials. The vanishing points for the decay constant is determined to be in baryon chemical potential interval $(0.23-0.83)~\mathrm{GeV}$. For the mass, the decrease at $T=0$ is much weaker than at finite temperatures; excluding this case, the vanishing points for the mass are determined to be in baryon chemical potential interval $(0.38-1.43)~\mathrm{GeV}$ for different fixed temperatures. It is further observed that the baryon chemical potential values corresponding to the vanishing of both the mass and decay constant move to lower values as the temperature increases. In the $T \to 0$ and $μ_B \to 0$ limit ...

</details>


### [89] [Quark mass functions in Minkowski space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06839)
*Elmar P. Biernat,Franz Gross,M. T. Peña,Alfred Stadler*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This study employs Covariant Spectator Theory to compute the dressed quark mass function and wave-function renormalization for the five quark flavors, validating the results against lattice data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The goal is to provide accurate calculations of quark masses and their renormalization in both spacelike and timelike regions, which are fundamental for understanding strong interactions in quantum chromodynamics (QCD).

Method: Using the Covariant Spectator Theory, the authors calculate the mass function and wave-function renormalization for quarks, utilizing a model dressed-gluon propagator fitted to lattice data. The quark self-energy parameters are determined through fitting processes against lattice results.

Result: The fits yield quark mass predictions that align with typical constituent masses reported in quark models, confirming the viability of the chosen parameterization and methodology.

Conclusion: The results underscore the effectiveness of CST in accurately modeling quark properties and suggest compatibility with existing quark mass frameworks.

Abstract: Using the Covariant Spectator Theory (CST), we calculate the dressed quark mass function and wave-function renormalization for the five quark flavors from up/down to bottom in both the spacelike and timelike regions of Minkowski space. The calculation employs a model dressed-gluon propagator fitted to lattice data in the spacelike region. The remaining free parameters of the quark self-energy are determined by fits to lattice results for the quark mass function including a constraint built into the wave-function renormalization. We perform these fits using lattice data from two different groups and find that the resulting mass predictions are within the range of the typical constituent masses used in quark models.

</details>


### [90] [Inclusive electron-nucleus cross section models from domain adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08463)
*Krzysztof M. Graczyk,Beata E. Kowal,Rwik Dharmapal Banerjee,Jose Luis Bonilla,Hemant Prasad,Jan T. Sobczyk*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Transfer learning (TL) is applied to develop electron-nucleus cross section models, fine-tuning pre-trained neural networks for various nuclei with differing data availability and robustness.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enhance the predictive capabilities of electron-nucleus cross section models using data from similar nuclei as a basis.

Method: Initial deep neural networks pretrained on carbon data were fine-tuned for specific nuclei (He, Li, O, Al, Ca, Fe) and assessed for model performance based on training data characteristics.

Result: All models showed improvements, with varying degrees of robustness influenced by available data; models for oxygen required less adjustment, while others like lithium showed limitations due to sparse data.

Conclusion: Adapted models successfully predicted measurements for selected configurations, maintaining consistency within uncertainties, and were compared favorably against a standard phenomenological model.

Abstract: We apply transfer learning (TL) to construct data-driven models of inclusive electron-nucleus cross sections. Starting from an ensemble of deep neural networks pretrained on \(^{12}\)C data, we fine-tune the models separately for \(^{3}\)He, \(^{6}\)Li, \(^{16}\)O, \(^{27}\)Al, \(^{40}\)Ca, and \(^{56}\)Fe. The resulting models improve for all targets, marginally so for oxygen, where the carbon baseline is already adequate, although their predictive robustness depends on the amount, coverage, and precision of the available target data. We systematically study how model performance depends on the number of fine-tuned layers, on the fraction and selection of the training data, and on the overlap between the source and target kinematic domains. The layer-wise analysis shows that oxygen requires only shallow adaptation, whereas helium, calcium, and iron require substantially deeper fine-tuning. Lithium represents the least robust case because of its limited dataset, while aluminum demonstrates a strong sensitivity to a small subset of highly constraining measurements. For selected kinematic configurations outside the coverage of the carbon training data, the adapted models remain consistent with the measurements within their estimated uncertainties. Finally, we compare the resulting predictions with those of the phenomenological F1F2 model.

</details>


### [91] [Geometric Phases in Two-Level Mixing: From Neutral Mesons to Holonomic Qubits and Topological Majorana Modes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06857)
*Swarup Sangiri,Utpal Sarkar,A Taraphder*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper examines the geometric structure of a Hermitian two-level mixing Hamiltonian, linking it to qubit, fermionic, and topological descriptions and analyzing associated geometric phases and invariants.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the geometric properties of a Hamiltonian related to neutral meson oscillations and establish connections across various quantum descriptions.

Method: Mapping the Hamiltonian to an effective qubit representation, analyzing cyclic parameter evolutions on the Bloch sphere, and examining third-order Bargmann invariants.

Result: Discovery of a CP-like geometric parameter that influences the Hamiltonian vector, along with a correlation between momentum-space topology and quantized Berry phase across different models.

Conclusion: The findings offer a comprehensive geometric perspective that interrelates phase structures and various quantum systems, enhancing the understanding of two-level Hamiltonians.

Abstract: We investigate the geometric structure of a Hermitian two-level mixing Hamiltonian motivated by neutral meson oscillations and its connections with qubit, fermionic, and topological descriptions. Mapping the Hamiltonian to an effective qubit representation, we analyze the geometric phase associated with cyclic parameter evolution on the Bloch sphere. Without identifying the complex mixing phase with a physical CP-violation observable, we interpret it as a CP-like geometric parameter controlling the azimuthal orientation of the Hamiltonian vector and its reversal under phase conjugation. Third-order Bargmann invariants constructed from the eigenstates yield an associated discrete phase whose dependence on the mixing parameters is examined alongside the continuous geometric construction. The cyclic evolution is also represented as a single-qubit $R_z$ phase operation and expressed through Majorana bilinears. Extending the construction to the momentum-dependent Bogoliubov-de Gennes Hamiltonian of the Kitaev chain, we relate momentum-space winding to the topological regimes and the quantized Berry (Zak) phase in the real-parameter model, while the corresponding Bargmann construction approaches the global phase in the continuum limit. Together, these results provide a geometric perspective connecting phase structure, qubit operations, fermionic representations, and momentum-space topology within related two-level Hamiltonians.

</details>


### [92] [A Vector-Like Lepton Interpretation of the High-Energy Nuclear Recoil Candidate in LUX-ZEPLIN](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08993)
*Fatemeh Elahi,Pedro Schwaller*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The LZ experiment reports a nuclear-recoil candidate at high energy, suggesting dark matter interactions through spin-dependent scattering can explain this. The study focuses on singlet-doublet Majorana dark matter, with potential observational tests from both LZ and solar neutrino searches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore dark matter interactions that could explain a recently detected nuclear-recoil candidate at unusually high energy, where conventional spin-independent responses are suppressed.

Method: The authors analyze elastic spin-dependent scattering using a nonrelativistic operator and consider the implications of singlet-doublet Majorana dark matter with specific coupling scenarios.

Result: They identify a region in the Higgs blind spot that predicts a significant event rate at high recoil energies in LZ and matches the observed thermal relic abundance, while also allowing for solar capture via the same interaction.

Conclusion: Testing this framework requires additional xenon exposure and solar-neutrino searches, with current experiments like IceCube already constraining part of the parameter space.

Abstract: The LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) experiment has recently reported a nuclear-recoil candidate at $E_R=248\pm23_{\rm stat}\pm23_{\rm sys}\,{\rm keV}$ in an exposure of $2.84\,{\rm tonne\,yr}$. At this unusually high recoil energy, the spin-independent (SI) xenon response is strongly suppressed, motivating dark matter interactions with a harder recoil spectrum. We show that elastic spin-dependent (SD) scattering through the nonrelativistic operator $\mathcal O_4=\mathbf S_{\rm DM}\cdot\mathbf S_N$ provides a simple realization of such a spectrum. We consider singlet-doublet Majorana dark matter, for which a diagonal $Z$ coupling generates the required SD interaction. The accompanying Higgs-mediated SI interaction would generically produce too many low-energy recoils, but can be suppressed along a Higgs blind spot while retaining a nonzero $Z$ coupling. We find a region of the Higgs blind spot that simultaneously predicts an $\mathcal O(1)$ event rate in the LZ high-recoil window and reproduces the observed thermal relic abundance. The same SD interaction leads to solar capture, allowing this region to be tested independently with solar-neutrino searches. Current IceCube limits already probe part of the LZ-compatible thermal parameter space, while a viable region remains. Additional xenon exposure and improved solar-neutrino searches can therefore provide complementary tests of this interpretation.

</details>


### [93] [Neutron disappearance and the LZ nuclear recoil event](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09037)
*Mohammad Aghaie,Alessandro Strumia*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The LZ experiment found an anomalous nuclear recoil event that may indicate neutron disappearance due to decay or dark matter interactions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The observation of a nuclear recoil with anomalous energy suggests novel interactions potentially linked to dark matter or nuclear decay processes.

Method: The analysis involves modeling neutron disappearance events in xenon, considering their energy distribution and association with nuclear activity.

Result: Only about 10% of xenon events showed no extra nuclear activity, with a broader recoil spectrum expected if the energy released is significant. Under specific conditions, about 30% of events could exhibit a quiet fraction with line-like recoil spectra in xenon.

Conclusion: The disappearance rates are consistent across targets, with interesting implications for dark matter interaction and nuclear behavior in different materials.

Abstract: The LZ experiment reported a nuclear recoil with anomalous energy $E_R=248$ keV, around the typical neutron motion in xenon. This motivates interpreting the event as the disappearance of a bound neutron, either through spontaneous decay or a dark-matter-induced reaction. If the energy released is large, the predicted recoil spectrum is broad around the observed value; only $\sim10\%$ of xenon events avoid extra nuclear activity; the disappearance rate is target-independent and mildly constrained by oxygen experiments. In the special case of near-threshold DM-induced neutron disappearance, it can be open in xenon and closed in oxygen, the quiet fraction rises to $\sim30\%$, the recoil spectrum turns into lines.

</details>


### [94] [Electromagnetic structure of strange vector mesons in nuclear medium](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06938)
*Parada T. P. Hutauruk,Terry Mart,Kazuo Tsushima*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The study examines the in-medium modifications of the $K^{*+}$ meson's form factors in nuclear matter using the NJL model, finding suppression of these factors with increased density and a calculated charge radius slightly smaller than that of the $ρ^{+}$ meson.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how the electromagnetic structure of the $K^{*+}$ meson is altered in symmetric nuclear matter, providing insights into its behavior under dense conditions.

Method: Utilizing the Schwinger proper-time Nambu-Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model to consistently describe the nuclear medium effects and the $K^{*+}$ meson's electromagnetic structure at the quark level.

Result: The charge, magnetic, and quadrupole form factors of the $K^{*+}$ meson are suppressed with increased nuclear density, indicating significant modifications to its internal structure.

Conclusion: A charge radius of $r_{K^{*+}}^{*}=0.74~	ext{fm}$ at normal nuclear density is obtained, which is marginally smaller than the charge radius of the $ρ^{+}$ meson, $r_{ρ^{+}}^{*}	ext{simeq}0.75~	ext{fm}$.
