Abstract: We investigate the in-medium modifications of the charge (electric) $G_C^{*}(Q^2)$, magnetic $G_M^{*}(Q^2)$, and quadrupole $G_Q^{*}(Q^2)$ form factors of the positively charged vector meson $K^{*+}$ in symmetric nuclear matter at zero temperature within the Schwinger proper-time Nambu-Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model. In this framework, both the nuclear medium effects and the electromagnetic structure of the $K^{*+}$ meson are described consistently in the NJL model at the quark level. We find that the charge, magnetic, and quadrupole form factors are suppressed with increasing nuclear density, indicating substantial in-medium modifications of the strange vector meson's internal structure. We further obtain a charge radius of $r_{K^{*+}}^{*}=0.74~\mathrm{fm}$ at normal nuclear density, which is slightly smaller than the corresponding value for the $ρ^{+}$ meson, $r_{ρ^{+}}^{*}\simeq0.75~\mathrm{fm}$.

</details>


### [95] [Bottomonium suppression with a machine-learning-informed Debye mass](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09047)
*Ajaharul Islam,Shibo Chen,Fu-Peng Li,Long-Gang Pang,Guang-You Qin*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a machine-learning-informed Debye mass for modeling bottomonium in quark-gluon plasma, leading to better agreement with experimental data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enhance modeling of bottomonium in quark-gluon plasma using recent advancements in data-driven methods.

Method: A machine-learning-informed Debye mass is extracted from lattice inputs integrated into the heavy-quark KSU potential, utilized in a real-time Schrödinger equation framework.

Result: The model shows improved agreement with experimental data for bottomonium states, particularly excited states, compared to the original KSU model.

Conclusion: Machine learning proves beneficial for accurately capturing in-medium QCD effects in bottomonium production.

Abstract: Motivated by recent progress in data-driven approaches, we introduce a machine-learning (ML)-informed Debye mass, extracted from lattice-informed inputs, exclusively in the complex-valued heavy-quark Kent State University (KSU) potential. The resulting complex potential is used to solve the real-time Schrödinger equation within the quantum trajectories (QTraj) framework for the evolution of bottomonium in the quark-gluon plasma. We then compute the nuclear modification factors and double ratios for bottomonium $Υ(1S)$, $Υ(2S)$, and $Υ(3S)$ states in Pb-Pb collisions at $\sqrt{s_{NN}} = 5.02$ TeV. We compare our ML-induced results with those from the original KSU model and with experimental measurements from ALICE, ATLAS, and CMS collaborations. We find that the machine-learned Debye mass leads to improved agreement with data, particularly for excited states, highlighting the utility of machine learning in modeling in-medium QCD effects.

</details>


### [96] [Exploring the $P$-wave bottom-strange molecular pentaquarks with the complex scaling method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07068)
*Yuhang Zheng,Qing-Fu Song,Xiaonu Xiong*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper investigates the resonance properties of various $P$-wave $Σ_b$ and $ar{K}$ systems, identifying several resonances across different isospin and parity combinations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the positive-parity sector of $P$-wave $Σ_b$ and related systems, building on previous studies of negative-parity states.

Method: Systematic investigation of $P$-wave resonances in the $Σ_b K/Λ_b K^{*}/Σ_b K^{*}$ and $Σ_bar{K}/Λ_bar{K}^{*}/Σ_bar{K}^{*}$ systems for all $I(J^P)$ combinations using theoretical models.

Result: Predictions include a narrow resonance above the $Σ_b K$ threshold, a molecular resonance candidate in the $Λ_b K^{*}$ system, and several resonances in the higher-isospin $I=3/2$ sector with both compact and cutoff-sensitive features.

Conclusion: Predictions are made to assist LHCb in the experimental search for bottom-strange molecular pentaquarks.

Abstract: In this work, we perform a systematic investigation of the $P$-wave $Σ_b K/Λ_b K^{*}/Σ_b K^{*}$ and $Σ_b\bar{K}/Λ_b\bar{K}^{*}/Σ_b\bar{K}^{*}$ systems for all possible $I(J^P)$ combinations. In contrast to our earlier study of the negative-parity sector [Eur. Phys. J. C \textbf{85}, 1026 (2025)], the positive-parity sector is dominated by resonances. For the coupled-channel $I(J^P)=1/2(1/2^+)$ $Σ_b K/Λ_b K^*/Σ_b K^*$ system, we predict a narrow resonance above the $Σ_b K$ threshold. In the $I(J^P)=1/2(3/2^+)$ $Λ_b K^*/Σ_b K^*$ system, a molecular resonance candidate is found above the $Λ_b K^*$ threshold. For the higher-isospin $I=3/2$ sector, resonances are identified in the $Σ_b K^*$ subsystem for both $J^P=1/2^+$ and $3/2^+$ cases, though both states exhibit relatively compact sizes. We also extend our analysis to the $Σ_b\bar{K}/Λ_b\bar{K}^{*}/Σ_b\bar{K}^{*}$ systems, where a cutoff-sensitive resonance is found in the $I(J^P)=1/2(1/2^+)$ configuration. A broad molecular resonance candidate is found below the $Σ_b\bar{K}^*$ threshold in the $I(J^P)=1/2(3/2^+)$ system. We hope that these predictions provide useful guidance for future experimental searches for bottom-strange molecular pentaquarks by LHCb.

</details>


### [97] [Majoron-driven spontaneous leptogenesis in type-II seesaw model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07130)
*Eung Jin Chun,Tae Hyun Jung,Jin Sun*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a minimal approach to spontaneous leptogenesis using the type-II seesaw model, highlighting the contributions of decay processes and the effects of a Majoron background on lepton and Higgs asymmetries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To offer a simpler framework for leptogenesis that avoids complexities found in thermal processes and does not require additional scalar fields or CP-violating interactions.

Method: The authors derive Boltzmann equations that describe the evolution of lepton and Higgs asymmetries, analyzing both analytically and numerically how these asymmetries emerge from the interactions under a Majoron background.

Result: The asymmetries are shown to depend on the triplet branching fractions, scalable as the square root of suppressed decay modes, while being nearly independent of the triplet mass. The Majoron background does not contribute to CP-violating effects in a specific limit.

Conclusion: The findings affirm that spontaneous leptogenesis provides a viable and simplified alternative to traditional thermal leptogenesis in the context of the type-II seesaw framework.

Abstract: We investigate a minimal realization of spontaneous leptogenesis in the type-II seesaw model, where the lepton and Higgs asymmetries are generated as decay and inverse-decay processes drive the plasma toward the displaced equilibrium dictated by a rotating Majoron background. We derive the complete set of Boltzmann equations governing the evolution of asymmetric particle densities and analytically and numerically identify the freeze-out and freeze-in regimes. The resulting asymmetries depend primarily on the triplet branching fractions and are nearly independent of the triplet mass. The asymmetry scales as the square root of the smaller branching fraction when either decay mode into leptons or Higgs fields is suppressed, reflecting the symmetry structure; the lepton number is conserved once either coupling is turned off. We also show that the Majoron background does not induce CP-asymmetric decay of the scalar triplet in the massless final state limit, and thus its impact is negligible. Our results establish spontaneous leptogenesis as a simple and robust alternative to thermal type-II leptogenesis that requires neither additional scalar triplets nor explicit CP-violating interactions.

</details>


### [98] [A Dark-Dimension Origin of Geometric Inelastic Dark Matter: The LUX-ZEPLIN High-Recoil Event and Multi-Target Tests](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07138)
*Waqas Ahmed,George K. Leontaris*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper investigates a pseudo-Dirac electroweak-doublet dark-matter model in a five-dimensional framework, linking it to the LUX-ZEPLIN experiment's observed recoil energy that suggests endothermic dark-matter scenarios.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) experiment reported an event with recoil energy of about 248 keV, which prompts investigation into models of dark matter that can explain such high-energy interactions.

Method: The study explores a pseudo-Dirac electroweak-doublet dark-matter model set in a five-dimensional framework, where the mass splitting is generated geometrically and is relevant for inelastic scattering. A two-bin Poisson diagnostic is applied to assess the compatibility of dark matter parameters with LZ findings.

Result: The analysis identifies a minimum in the parameter space around (m_χ,δ) = (1.35 TeV, 366 keV) and shows that a thermally motivated point lies very close in parameter space, indicating potential compatibility with the LZ event. The calculated scattering rates for various materials, particularly CaWO$_4$, suggest experimental avenues for testing the model.

Conclusion: The findings establish a link between the geometric origin of mass splitting in the proposed dark-matter model and its potential for high-energy recoil detections, indicating promising directions for future experiments involving multiple target materials.

Abstract: The extended nuclear-recoil analysis of LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) has reported one event compatible with a recoil energy near $248$ keV, motivating endothermic dark-matter scenarios with support at large recoil energy. We study a pseudo-Dirac electroweak-doublet dark-matter model embedded in a five-dimensional Dark-Dimension framework. The Standard Model and the vectorlike electroweak doublets are localized on the visible brane, while dark-number violation is communicated through a neutral bulk state. The resulting mass splitting is exponentially suppressed, $δ=δ_{\rm UV}e^{-2πMR}$, providing a geometric origin for the few-hundred-keV scale relevant to inelastic scattering. The neutral weak current is dominantly off diagonal, fixing the neutron-scale normalization at $σ_{χn}^{Z}\simeq1.86\times10^{-39}\,\mathrm{cm^2}$. Using a two-bin Poisson diagnostic of the extended LZ recoil window, we find a shallow minimum near $(m_χ,δ)\simeq(1.35~\TeV,366~\keV)$, while the thermally motivated point $(1.1~\TeV,360~\keV)$ lies only $Δχ^2\simeq0.21$ higher. The same benchmark is kinematically inaccessible to light targets but remains open for xenon and tungsten, providing a characteristic multi-target test of the model. For CaWO$_4$ we obtain an integrated tungsten rate of $3.15\times10^{-2}$ events kg$^{-1}$ yr$^{-1}$ in the $95$--$150$ keV interval. These results connect the geometric origin of the pseudo-Dirac splitting with high-recoil direct-detection phenomenology.

</details>


### [99] [Probing Neutral Triple Gauge Couplings at e- p colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07168)
*Xue-Jia Cheng,Chong-Xing Yue,Ji-Chong Yang,Si-Tong Liu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates the use of SMEFT at $e^{-}p$ colliders to analyze new interactions, specifically nTGCs, and presents event selection strategies and constraints on dimension-8 operators.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The need for model-independent methods to explore new physical interactions in high-energy physics.

Method: Analysis of six processes at the FCC-he, including signal/background study and event selection strategies, to derive constraints on nTGCs.

Result: Constraints on dimension-8 operators for multiple processes, showing that FCC-he has competitive sensitivity to nTGCs compared to LHC and CEPC.

Conclusion: The study indicates that $e^{-}p$ colliders are effective for probing nTGCs, matching LHC sensitivity and outperforming CEPC.

Abstract: The Standard Model Effective Field Theory~(SMEFT) has attracted much attention as a model-independent way for probing new physical signals. In the SMEFT framework, $e^{-}p$ colliders can be used to study new interactions of gauge bosons, such as neutral Triple Gauge Couplings~(nTGCs). We study the signals and backgrounds of six different processes at the Future Circular Collider-hadron electron~(FCC-he) and propose different event selection strategies. The expected constraints on the coefficients of the dimension-8 operators for each process, as well as the combined constraints are obtained. The results show that the sensitivity of $e^{-}p$ colliders to nTGCs is similar to the current Large Hadron Collider~(LHC) experiments and is more competitive than that of Circular Electron Positron Collider~(CEPC) experiments.

</details>


### [100] [Hadronic heavy neutral lepton decays to the limit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07189)
*Jonathan L. Schubert,Babette Döbrich*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores the decay structure of Heavy Neutral Leptons (HNLs) using a hadronic approach, proposing a link to partonic descriptions for better understanding and potential experimental implications.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: HNLs are proposed to address several unresolved issues in the Standard Model of particle physics.

Method: The paper develops a decay model for HNLs that transitions from a hadronic to a partonic description based on invariant mass.

Result: The analysis reveals that the resulting multi-hadron decay channels could serve as significant experimental signatures for detecting HNLs.

Conclusion: Understanding these decay structures might enhance the search for HNLs, providing more insights into their lifetimes and characteristics.

Abstract: Heavy Neutral Leptons are a class of hypothetical particle, motivated by simultaneously solving multiple of the standing issues of the Standard Model. In this work we investigate the decay structure of this type of particle in the hadronic picture for multi-body final states. We propose to link the resulting hadronic description to a partonic description in a seamless transition based on the hadron system's invariant mass. Beyond the impact on the total HNL lifetime, we will show that the multi-hadron channels emerging in this description can constitute interesting experimental signatures.

</details>


### [101] [Chiral Doubling, Renormalization Group Fixed Points, and Dense Matter Equations of State](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07190)
*Chihiro Sasaki*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The study proposes a unified effective field theory framework to explore hadronic spectroscopy, dense baryonic matter, and compact star physics, leveraging renormalization group fixed points for extrapolation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To create a cohesive framework for understanding advanced concepts in hadronic physics and astrophysics, particularly the interplay of symmetries and matter density.

Method: Effective Lagrangian is formulated at RG fixed points with minimal symmetry breaking, allowing extrapolation to physical environments.

Result: The model successfully reproduces heavy-light meson spectra in vacuum and aligns with gravitational-wave evidence for massive neutron stars, indicating a relationship between nucleon mass and vector couplings.

Conclusion: The framework reveals how RG fixed points govern hadronic dynamics across different environments, providing insights into deconfinement and baryon fluctuations in neutron star interiors.

Abstract: We review a top-down fixed-point-extrapolation paradigm as a unified effective field theory framework for hadronic spectroscopy, dense baryonic matter, and compact star physics. By formulating our effective Lagrangian directly at renormalization group (RG) fixed points and introducing minimal symmetry breaking, we extrapolate the theory back to physical environments. In the vacuum, this framework naturally reproduces the heavy-light meson parity-doubling spectrum via light vector meson loops. In dense matter, the interplay between the chiral-invariant nucleon mass m0 and walking vector couplings reconciles gravitational-wave constraints with $2M_\odot$ massive neutron stars. Generalization to a quark-hadron hybrid approach further illuminates sequential deconfinement, core stability, and baryon number fluctuations, demonstrating how underlying RG fixed points dictate hadronic dynamics from vacuum to neutron star interiors.

</details>


### [102] [Pseudo-Dirac Inelastic Dark Matter in the Leptophobic $U(1)_B$ Model: Confronting the LUX-ZEPLIN High-Recoil Event with Collider Searches](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07225)
*Xin-Yu Du,Wenjie Huang,Keping Xie*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper discusses interpreting a nuclear-recoil candidate from LUX-ZEPLIN as an endothermic transition, using a model involving stable dark states and baryon number gauging. It identifies specific mass and coupling parameters that are consistent with existing experimental limits and suggests future implications for high-energy searches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper aims to explain the observed 248 keV nuclear-recoil candidate by utilizing a model that incorporates stable dark states and novel current interactions through baryon number gauging, addressing gaps in conventional interpretations.

Method: It postulates a model with dark charges and Majorana masses, leading to stable light states and a dominant vector current transition, thereby deriving mass and coupling relationships that align with findings from LZ and LHC experiments.

Result: The model suggests that for the parameters (M_{Z_B}, g_B) = (1.44 TeV, 0.233), all relevant experimental findings converge, supporting the validity of the dark state interpretation and its stability just below key experimental limits.

Conclusion: Significant implications for future searches in high-energy physics are noted, particularly regarding the stability of the interpretations and the prospects of exclusion or confirmation through updated LHC analyses.

Abstract: Interpreting the $248$~keV nuclear-recoil candidate reported by LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) as an endothermic transition needs two ingredients usually put in by hand: a stable dark state, and a coherent current that is purely off diagonal. Gauging baryon number supplies both. For the minimal dark charges $B(χ_L)=-3/2$ and $B(χ_R)=-9/2$, the scalars that generate the dark masses leave an exact residual parity $P=(-1)^{6B}$ stabilizing the lightest state, while the same Majorana masses make the vector current transition dominated as an identity rather than a choice. At $m_χ=1$~TeV and $δ=300$~keV the model is over-determined: the observed LZ interval, a leading-order relic estimate and the LHC dijet limit meet at the single perturbatively acceptable point $(M_{Z_B},g_B)\simeq(1.44~\mathrm{TeV},0.233)$. That point is allowed by currently existing searches but sits just below the trigger-level dijet boundary, so it is alive and not yet decided--and a re-run of that analysis on the Run-3 data at $\sqrt s=13.6$~TeV, already on tape, will exclude it or find it. Nor is the interpretation confined to that splitting: at $δ=200$~keV, the same construction predicts a heavier mediator at comparable coupling, $M_{Z_B}\simeq2.4$-$3.0$~TeV with $g_B\simeq0.15$-$0.34$, a range the HL-LHC dijet program will probe.

</details>


### [103] [Vector-meson properties in a data-driven AdS/QCD model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07374)
*Xun Chen,Floriana Giannuzzi,Stefano Nicotri*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores properties of mesons in a holographic AdS/QCD framework, focusing on their vacuum characteristics and behavior in a hot medium.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To better understand mesonic properties and dynamics in both vacuum and dense medium conditions using a holographic approach.

Method: The authors use a holographic model to compute meson properties, including mass spectrum and decay constants, and analyze dynamics under varying thermal conditions.

Result: The study provides insights into mass shifts, resonance broadening, and conductivity of the medium, revealing how vector bound states react under temperature and density variations.

Conclusion: Findings have implications for understanding QCD in extreme conditions, contributing to the study of heavy-ion collisions and the early universe.

Abstract: We investigate the properties of $ρ$, $ψ$, and $Υ$ mesons within a holographic AdS/QCD framework, capturing both their vacuum phenomenology and in-medium dynamics. By reconstructing flavor-specific gauge kinetic functions within a model originally developed to study thermodynamic functions, we compute the vector-meson mass spectrum, the decay constants and the hadronic vacuum polarization contributions to the anomalous magnetic moment of the muon at zero temperature. Furthermore, we evaluate the thermal spectral functions to analyze mass shifts, resonance broadening, and the melting of vector bound states in a hot and dense medium. Finally, using the Kubo formula, we extract the electrical conductivity of the plasma as a function of temperature and chemical potential.

</details>


### [104] [Connecting Regge factorization and Collins-Soper evolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07424)
*Andrea Simonelli*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper investigates the link between Regge factorization of amplitudes and Collins-Soper-Sterman factorization in hadronic cross sections, highlighting a relationship between rapidity divergences and transverse-momentum-dependent factorization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify the understanding of rapidity divergences in high-energy scattering with transverse-momentum-dependent factorization.

Method: Examining the connection between the Collins-Soper kernel and Regge trajectory to establish a framework for integrating different factorization approaches.

Result: A connection is established that bridges high-energy scattering and low-transverse-momentum resummation, facilitating a comprehensive understanding of factorization.

Conclusion: The findings suggest potential for extending $k_T$-factorization beyond leading-logarithmic accuracy, thereby enhancing theoretical predictions in scattering processes.

Abstract: The relationship between Regge factorization of amplitudes and the Collins-Soper-Sterman factorization of hadronic cross sections is explored. Seeking a unified description of rapidity divergences in high-energy scattering and transverse-momentum-dependent factorization, a connection emerges between the Collins-Soper kernel and the Regge trajectory. This provides a framework for bridging high-energy and low-transverse-momentum resummation and for future extensions of $k_T$-factorization beyond leading-logarithmic accuracy.

</details>


### [105] [Baryon number freeze-out in the Standard Model, precisely](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07467)
*Cristina Benso,Dietrich Bödeker,Kohei Kamada,Kyohei Mukaida,Laura Sagunski,Philipp Schicho,Kai Schmitz*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This study analyzes weak sphaleron transitions in the early Universe, showing how lepton asymmetries can convert into baryon asymmetries while conserving $B-L$ and flavored charges, and provides refined calculations of baryon number evolution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the mechanisms converting lepton asymmetries to baryon asymmetries in the early Universe, particularly considering the temperature-dependent behavior of relevant parameters.

Method: Derivation of a Boltzmann equation for baryon number evolution across the electroweak crossover, incorporating higher-order corrections to the grand canonical partition function and the Higgs expectation value.

Result: Calculation of two sphaleron conversion factors: $C_	ext{sph} = 0.3328(5)$ for $B-L$ and $\	ext{F}_\text{sph} = 0.0279(19)$ for flavored charges, indicating refined estimates of baryon asymmetry.

Conclusion: The findings provide a more accurate picture of baryon asymmetry generation in the early Universe, factoring in temperature dependencies that previous equilibrium calculations overlooked.

Abstract: Weak sphaleron transitions turn a lepton asymmetry of the Standard Model plasma in the early Universe into a baryon asymmetry, conserving baryon-minus-lepton number $B-L$ and its individual flavored charges. A baryon asymmetry can thus also arise from flavored lepton asymmetries with vanishing $B-L$. Standard equilibrium calculations in the symmetric and broken phases are performed at constant temperature and hence neglect the fact that both the Higgs expectation value and the sphaleron rate vary as functions of temperature across the electroweak crossover. We derive a Boltzmann equation for the baryon number evolution across the crossover and calculate the freeze-out abundance including higher-order corrections to both the grand canonical partition function and the perturbative Higgs expectation value. This yields two sphaleron conversion factors: $C_\text{sph} = 0.3328(5)$ for $B-L$ and $\mathcal{F}_\text{sph} = 0.0279(19)$ for the flavored charges weighted by the charged-lepton Yukawa couplings.

</details>


### [106] [Gravitational Wave in an $A_4 \otimes Z_4$ Neutrino Model with Generalised CP](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07661)
*Pulakesh Borah,Mrinal Kumar Das*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper discusses an extension of the Standard Model using an $A_4 	imes Z_4$ flavor symmetry and a generalized CP symmetry, introducing new couplings that influence lepton mixing, reactor angles, mass predictions, and gravitational-wave signals.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the observed patterns in lepton mixing and CP violation while exploring potential connections to gravitational-wave background.

Method: The authors utilize an $A_4 	imes Z_4$ flavor symmetry and two flavon triplets to generate lepton mixing patterns, establishing a quartic interaction that affects CP and reactor mixing angles.

Result: The model matches mixing angles and mass splittings from oscillation data, predicting a maximal Dirac CP phase and an effective Majorana mass that is experimentally relevant, along with predictions for gravitational-wave signatures.

Conclusion: The findings suggest a testable relationship between neutrino physics and gravitational waves, offering new avenues for experimental verification.

Abstract: We extend the Standard Model with an $A_4 \times Z_4$ flavor symmetry and generalized CP (GCP) symmetry, realized through two flavon triplets that spontaneously break $A_4$ and generate the observed lepton mixing pattern. A new $A_4$-singlet flavon couples to one of these triplets through a complex quartic interaction that breaks GCP and fixes the reactor mixing angle; this same coupling biases the domain walls formed when that triplet breaks $A_4$. A global fit to oscillation data reproduces all mixing angles and mass splittings, predicting a maximal Dirac CP phase of $270^\circ$ and an effective Majorana mass near $15\text{ meV}$, testable by upcoming neutrinoless double-beta-decay ($0νββ$) experiments. The same coupling generates a stochastic gravitational-wave background peaking in the pulsar-timing-array and LISA bands, with peak frequency and amplitude predicted to scale as $\sinθ_{13}$ and $\sin^{-4}θ_{13}$, a falsifiable link between neutrino oscillation and gravitational-wave (GW) observations.

</details>


### [107] [Boosted dark particles and the LZ nuclear recoil event](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07742)
*Kristjan Kannike,Martti Raidal,Alessandro Strumia*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The study analyzes an anomalous event in the LZ experiment, suggesting that elastic collisions of dark sector particles explain the findings better than traditional heavy dark matter models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The anomalous event in the LZ experiment suggests new physics beyond traditional dark matter models, particularly regarding the energy levels produced by dark matter interactions.

Method: The paper investigates elastic collisions of dark sector particles with specific momentum and mass ranges, while considering constraints from existing experiments like SNO.

Result: Findings indicate that elastic collisions with defined properties could explain the observed anomalous event, while ruling out heavy DM and certain interactions due to absence of low-energy recoils.

Conclusion: Elastic interactions may lead to negligible signals in argon and only slight annual modulation effects, reshaping the understanding of possible dark matter interactions.

Abstract: The LZ experiment reported one anomalous event with nuclear recoil energy of 248 keV. Galactic halo dark matter (DM) cannot produce such a large energy unless heavier than 74 GeV, and heavy DM with spin-independent interactions would have produced unseen recoils at lower energy. Inelastic DM has been proposed as a way out. We consider, instead, an elastic collision of a dark sector particle with a momentum of at least 123~MeV and mass in the range 1-74 GeV as deuteron break-up in SNO disfavors sub-GeV masses. The absence of accompanying lower-energy recoils favours interactions whose rate grows with the momentum transfer, such as a pseudoscalar-pseudoscalar nucleon operator. This implies no leading-order signal in argon, an annual modulation below 1 %, and recoils in spinful light nuclei where inelastic halo DM gives nothing.

</details>


### [108] [Not so good $ν$s for Higgsino dark matter as LZ excess: stringent limits from Super-Kamiokande and IceCube](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07807)
*Debajit Bose,Akash Kumar Saha,Deep Jyoti Das,Rinchen Sherpa,Abhijeet Singh,Durba Ghosh,Jaya Doliya,Subhadip Bouri,Biprajit Mondal,Ranjini Mondol,Nirmal Raj,Ranjan Laha*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The LUX-ZEPLIN collaboration reported a significant nuclear recoil event, interpreted as dark matter interactions, specifically inelastic scattering of Higgsino, with constraints on its properties derived from neutrino measurements.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the implications of a recent nuclear recoil event observed by the LZ collaboration and its connection to dark matter, specifically Higgsino models.

Method: The analysis involves using atmospheric neutrino measurements from Super-Kamiokande and IceCube to constrain the parameters of Higgsino dark matter, particularly focusing on mass splittings.

Result: The study excludes inter-state mass splittings of the Higgsino dark matter below 557 keV, suggesting limitations on certain Higgsino interpretations of the LZ event.

Conclusion: The findings challenge the viability of certain Higgsino models of dark matter in light of the LZ event and traditional halo velocity assumptions.

Abstract: The LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) collaboration has recently reported a single nuclear recoil event at a high recoil energy of about 250 keV. This has been interpreted as inelastic scattering of dark matter that is a supersymmetric Higgsino with a mass splitting between the neutral states of a few 100 keV. Such dark matter may be captured at high recoil in the Sun through scattering on heavy elements in it, and annihilate to $W^+W^-$ and $ZZ$, in turn giving rise to a neutrino flux detectable on Earth. Using measurements of atmospheric electron- and muon-neutrino fluxes by Super-Kamiokande and IceCube, we constrain thermal and non-thermal Higgsino dark matter, excluding inter-state mass splittings $\lesssim 557$ keV. This disfavors Higgsino-like interpretations of the LZ event for standard halo velocities.

</details>


### [109] [Higgsino Dark Matter Interpretation of the LZ High-Recoil Event in the GNMSSM with TeV-Scale Gauginos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07811)
*Subhadip Bisal,Junjie Cao,Fei Li*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The study explores endothermic dark matter scattering in the context of the GNMSSM, addressing a nuclear recoil event reported by the LUX-ZEPLIN collaboration. By analyzing Higgsino-dominated neutralino DM interactions, it presents benchmark models that align with relic abundance and various constraints without requiring extremely heavy gauginos.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The LUX-ZEPLIN collaboration's nuclear recoil event indicates potential new parameters for dark matter interactions, particularly in endothermic scenarios.

Method: The authors investigate the GNMSSM framework, focusing on Higgsino-dominated neutralino scattering, neutralino splitting contributions, and coannihilation effects to match observed relic densities.

Result: Six benchmark points with dark-matter masses and neutralino splittings successfully align with experimental constraints and cosmic relic abundance, demonstrating the GNMSSM's flexibility in accommodating the LZ event.

Conclusion: The GNMSSM can explain the LZ high-recoil event without reliance on ultraheavy gauginos, though further assessments of solar capture and neutrino telescope limits are essential for viability.

Abstract: The nuclear recoil event at approximately 248 keV reported by the LUX-ZEPLIN collaboration motivates an investigation of endothermic dark matter scattering. We study this within the General Next-to-Minimal Supersymmetric Standard Model (GNMSSM), with Higgsino-dominated neutralino DM undergoing the $Z$-mediated transition $\widetildeχ_1^0N\to\widetildeχ_2^0N$. In the conventional thermal Higgsino limit of the MSSM, the observed relic abundance selects a mass near 1.1 TeV, while a neutralino splitting of a few hundred keV typically requires gaugino masses of order $10^7$ GeV. In the GNMSSM, Higgsino-Singlino mixing introduces an additional contribution to the splitting that can cancel the gaugino-induced contribution, allowing sub-MeV splitting with multi-TeV gauginos. This mixing also modifies the inelastic scattering coupling and annihilation rates, while coannihilation with sleptons provides freedom in obtaining the observed relic abundance. We present six benchmark points with dark-matter masses of 0.66-1.11 TeV, neutralino splittings of 333-350 keV, and gaugino masses of 2-5 TeV. These points reproduce the observed relic abundance and satisfy direct-detection, Higgs, flavor, and collider constraints. Within the Standard Halo Model and extended-likelihood analysis, all six points yield $Δχ^2<1$ relative to the best fit. Our results show how the GNMSSM can accommodate the LZ high-recoil event without an ultraheavy gaugino sector. A quantitative assessment of solar-capture and neutrino-telescope constraints remains necessary for establishing viability.

</details>


### [110] [Quantum-Geometric Meissner Effect in Magnetized Color Superconductors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07835)
*Kazuya Mameda,Noriyuki Sogabe*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper explores a quantum-geometric Meissner response in magnetized two-flavor color-superconducting quark matter, revealing unconventional scaling mechanisms tied to quantum metrics of Landau levels.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Investigating the unique properties of quark matter in strong magnetic fields, particularly the behavior of the transverse Meissner response.

Method: Analyzing Landau quantization effects on quasiparticle dispersion and exploring the quantum metrics of Landau levels to understand the Meissner response.

Result: The response is dominated by the quantum metric of the lowest Landau level, leading to a transverse Meissner mass scaling defined by the pairing gap, differing from conventional superconductors.

Conclusion: The findings imply potential significance for understanding kHz oscillations in magnetars, driven by the unique implications of the quantum-geometric Meissner effect.

Abstract: We find a quantum-geometric Meissner response in magnetized two-flavor color-superconducting (2SC) quark matter. Landau quantization quenches the transverse quasiparticle dispersion, suppressing the conventional Fermi-surface contribution and giving rise to a Meissner response governed by the quantum geometry of the Landau levels. In the strong-field regime, the response becomes dominated by the quantum metric of the lowest Landau level (LLL), and the leading scaling of the transverse Meissner mass is consequently set by the pairing gap, in contrast to the chemical potential scaling of conventional color superconductors. This unconventional scaling has a topological origin, as the LLL quantum metric is constrained by its Chern number. The reduced transverse Meissner mass provides potential implications for kHz quasi-periodic oscillations in magnetars.

</details>


### [111] [Axion Hair and Pulsar Electrodynamics: modelling, discharge dynamics, and particle-in-cell simulations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08840)
*Samuel J. Witte,Andrea Caputo,Stefan Stelzl,Alexander Chernoglazov,Alexander A. Philippov,Surjeet Rajendran*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends previous work on how static axion field gradients from dense nuclear matter affect pulsar electrodynamics, with implications for QCD axions and nucleon interactions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how axion hair from dense nuclear matter influences the electrodynamics in rotation-powered pulsars, crucial for understanding light QCD axions and CP-violating interactions.

Method: The study involves analytical calculations of effective discharge parameters and numerical 1D particle-in-cell simulations to investigate the effects of axion gradients on pulsar electrodynamics.

Result: Findings show that axion hair alters acceleration and screening effects differently in the northern and southern hemispheres of pulsars, depending on field lines tied to out-flowing and return currents.

Conclusion: The results emphasize the role of axion hair in modifying pulsar dynamics, providing a framework for future research on axions and their interactions.

Abstract: In a companion paper, we demonstrated that static axion field gradients sourced by dense nuclear matter (\emph{axion hair}) can dominate the near-field electrodynamics of old rotation-powered pulsars, leading to new constraints on light QCD axions and on CP-violating axion-nucleon interactions. This article provides the extended theoretical and numerical framework underlying those results. We begin by providing a detailed description of the sourcing of axion hair from dense nuclear matter, computing self-consistent field profiles for each interaction across the relevant parameter space. We then study the modification to the electrodynamics induced in the polar gap region by these axion gradients; this is done at the analytic level by studying the modification induced by axion field gradients on the effective discharge parameter (computed in the force-free limit of the split monopole magnetic field configuration, and looking at leading deviations from the force-free limit for dipolar field configurations), and numerically by developing dedicated 1D particle-in-cell simulations which capture the leading order dynamical behavior near the star. Our results demonstrate that axion hair serves to either enhance acceleration, or enhance screening, where the relevant effect changes between the northern and southern hemispheres of the star, and between the field lines which support out-flowing and return currents.

</details>


### [112] [Initial momentum anisotropies in the kT-factorization of the CGC I: Gradient Expansion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07847)
*Oscar Garcia-Montero*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper revisits gluon production in the Color Glass Condensate framework, showing that $k_\perp$-factorization is a zeroth-order approximation. It presents a systematic gradient expansion, yielding an extended $k_\perp$-factorized formula that incorporates spatial dependence and initial-state momentum anisotropies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the limitations of traditional $k_\perp$-factorization in capturing spatial and momentum anisotropies in gluon production.

Method: A gradient expansion in powers of $1/(Q_sR)$ is employed, leading to an extended formula that includes local distributions and transverse derivatives, combined with a real-time computation of classical Glasma fields.

Result: The systematic expansion reveals momentum-space anisotropies in the initial state that standard $k_\perp$-factorization overlooks, showing enhanced structures influenced by coherent interference in the eikonal limit.

Conclusion: The derived corrections can be integrated into existing models to provide a more accurate description of initial-state momentum anisotropies, especially relevant in dilute systems like light-ion collisions.

Abstract: We revisit single-inclusive gluon production in the dilute--dilute limit of the Color Glass Condensate and show that $k_\perp$-factorization is only the zeroth order of a systematic gradient expansion in the transverse positions of the two colliding sources, organized in powers of $1/(Q_sR)$, with $Q_s$ the saturation scale and $R$ the size over which the sources vary. Collecting terms order by order builds a ladder of scalar and tensor structures from the local distributions and their transverse derivatives, yielding an extended $k_\perp$-factorized formula that retains the spatial dependence of the sources and, through kinetic moments of the spectrum, the energy-momentum tensor. Repeating the expansion from a real-time computation of the classical Glasma fields in the future light cone reproduces the momentum-space result in the eikonal limit, but additionally retains coherent interference between amplitude and conjugate. This adds terms to the ladder (flow responses, chromo-electric--magnetic interference, and a longitudinal energy flux) further suppressed by powers of the large outgoing momentum $|\pp|\gg Q_s$. The hierarchy thus reveals momentum-space anisotropies present in the initial state before any hydrodynamic evolution, which leading $k_\perp$-factorization misses by construction. Since each order is a local operator on the same unintegrated distributions, these corrections can be added directly to existing saturation-based initial-state models, giving a dynamically generated initial momentum anisotropy and a more faithful early-time energy-momentum tensor without abandoning the tractable factorized form. Set by gradients of the transverse density profiles, they grow towards more dilute, fluctuation-dominated systems, e.g. light-ion collisions. Phenomenological consequences will be developed in a companion paper.

</details>


### [113] [Recent applications of the gradient flow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07940)
*R. Harlander,A. Shindler*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper discusses using gradient flow as a regulated method to combine non-perturbative and perturbative QCD calculations, particularly in operator-product expansion contexts, with implications for various observables and quantum gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enhance integration of non-perturbative lattice calculations with perturbative results in QCD using a novel gradient flow approach.

Method: The paper presents a review of gradient flow as an ultraviolet regulator, focusing on the flow-time dependence of conversion between flowed and regular Wilson coefficients within operator-product expansions.

Result: It is shown that the conversion at each perturbative order is exact, leading to successful applications in parton distribution functions and other QCD observables.

Conclusion: The paper concludes by connecting gradient flow to perturbative Ricci flow in quantum gravity, exploring implications for non-Gaussian fixed points.

Abstract: The gradient flow provides a gauge- and O(4)-invariant ultraviolet regulator for QCD. This opens new opportunities for combining non-perturbative lattice calculations with perturbative results, in particular in the context of an operator-product expansion (OPE). We review the concepts behind this and argue that, within the operator basis of a given OPE, the flow-time dependence of the conversion between flowed and regular Wilson coefficients is exact at each perturbative order. Recent applications to parton distribution functions, heavy-meson observables, quark masses, and gradient-flow definitions of the strong coupling are discussed. Finally, the perturbative formulation of the Ricci flow in quantum gravity and its application to the study of non-Gaussian fixed points is reviewed.

</details>


### [114] [Near-Threshold Dynamics of \c{hi}c1(3872) and T+cc(3875) States via Effective Range Expansion and Monte Carlo Uncertainty Propagation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08289)
*A. A. Atangana Likéné,A. Franck Rothen,J. M. Ema'a Ema'a,P. Pradyun Hebbar,Saralees Nadarajah,Tobias Golling,G. H. Ben-Bolie*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This study uses effective-range expansion to analyze the tetraquark candidates $χ_{c1}(3872)$ and $T_{cc}^+(3875)$, revealing large negative scattering lengths and strong evidence for their molecular structures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the near-threshold behavior and structural characteristics of exotic tetraquarks through the effective-range expansion method.

Method: The authors applied effective-range expansion and resonance compositeness relations using experimental data to extract scattering lengths, effective ranges, and compositeness coefficients, supported by Monte Carlo sampling for uncertainty propagation.

Result: Findings include large negative scattering lengths for both tetraquarks and dominant molecular components with values of $X_2>0.95$, indicating a strong molecular nature.

Conclusion: The results suggest that both $χ_{c1}(3872)$ and $T_{cc}^+(3875)$ have predominantly molecular structures, reaffirming the effectiveness of the ERE method in studying exotic hadrons.

Abstract: We investigate the near-threshold structure of the exotic tetraquark candidates $χ_{c1}(3872)$ and $T_{cc}^+(3875)$ using the effective-range expansion (ERE) and resonance compositeness relations. From the experimental masses and widths, we extract scattering lengths, effective ranges, and molecular compositeness coefficients within a two-channel framework, with uncertainties propagated through $N=50000$ Monte Carlo samples. We find large negative scattering lengths, $a=-8.49^{+0.93}_{-1.05}$ fm for $χ_{c1}(3872)$ and $a=-14.57^{+1.70}_{-1.66}$ fm for $T_{cc}^+$, and dominant molecular components $X_2>0.95$. The results are robust against variations of compositeness, experimental correlations, and the loop-function subtraction constant. An explicit ERE+CDD-pole analysis finds no physically reasonable bare-state contribution consistent with the observed poles and a natural background. Our results provide strong evidence for predominantly molecular structures of both states and demonstrate the robustness of the ERE approach to near-threshold exotic hadrons.

</details>


### [115] [Top-quark flavor-changing neutral currents and charged-lepton flavor violation in a generational three-Higgs-doublet model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08350)
*Hao-Ran Ma,Ti-Bin Hou,Yu-Ju Peng,Jin-Lei Yang,Tai-Fu Feng*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This study analyzes flavor-changing processes in a three-Higgs-doublet model, detailing decay rates and conversion rates under various constraints and conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore flavor-changing neutral-current decays and charged-lepton flavor violations in the context of the generational three-Higgs-doublet model (G3HDM).

Method: The work employs a systematic investigation of decay processes and conversion rates based on a large sample (4839 points) under defined theoretical constraints.

Result: Decay rates are constrained with limits set on the branching ratios, while conversion rates for $μ-e$ processes are within experimental reach, indicating significant collider signals driven by tree-level couplings.

Conclusion: The findings suggest that the G3HDM can predict substantial rates for both decay and conversion processes, potentially observable in future experiments.

Abstract: In this work, we systematically investigate top-quark flavor-changing neutral-current decays, charged-lepton flavor-violating processes, and coherent $μ-e$ conversion in the CP-conserving generational three-Higgs-doublet model (G3HDM), including the relevant tree-level and one-loop contributions. The 4839-point sample passes conservative sufficient vacuum-stability conditions, necessary perturbative-unitarity preselection cuts, Higgs-mass and signal-strength constraints, electroweak precision data, the UTfit 2025 neutral-meson-mixing ranges, $\overline{B}\to X_sγ$, and $B_s\toμ^+μ^-$. In the scanned region, $\mathrm{Br}(t\to c h_1)\leq 1.04\times10^{-5}$ and $\mathrm{Br}(h_1\toμτ)\leq 4.83\times10^{-4}$, while the maximal conversion rates are $\mathrm{CR}(μ\mathrm{Al}\to e\mathrm{Al})\approx 4.95\times10^{-15}$ and $\mathrm{CR}(μ\mathrm{Au}\to e\mathrm{Au})\approx 1.05\times10^{-14}$. The dominant collider signals are mainly driven by tree-level flavor-changing neutral-Higgs couplings, which determine the relative strengths of these processes and induce the pronounced enhancement of $t\to c h_1$ and $h_1\toμτ$. Meanwhile, the upper range of the predicted $μ-e$ conversion rate in aluminum lies within the sensitivity reach of next-generation $μ-e$ conversion experiments.

</details>


### [116] [Light cone distributions of $P$-wave quarkonia](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08393)
*Yang Li,Tianyang Hu,Xianghui Cao,Meijian Li*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The study investigates leading-twist distribution amplitudes of $P$-wave quarkonia using the BLFQ method, revealing relativistic effects that introduce significant structural features, such as 'W'-shaped structures in axial vector mesons, and emphasizing their importance for high-energy collider physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Renewed interest in light-cone distributions of $P$-wave quarkonia.

Method: Utilization of the basis light-front quantization (BLFQ) formalism to extract leading-twist distribution amplitudes.

Result: Extracted distributions show macroscopic shapes consistent with non-relativistic limits, but with critical relativistic effects causing novel 'W'-shaped structures in axial vector mesons.

Conclusion: These findings provide non-perturbative inputs for hard exclusive processes and stress the need for relativistic frameworks in studying heavy quarkonium production and structure.

Abstract: Motivated by renewed interest in the light-cone distributions of $P$-wave quarkonia, we investigate the leading-twist distribution amplitudes of these states within the basis light-front quantization (BLFQ) formalism. While our extracted distributions exhibit macroscopic shapes consistent with expectations from the non-relativistic limit, we find that relativistic effects introduce critical structural features. Most notably, we observe novel ``W"-shaped structures in the distribution amplitudes of the axial vector mesons, which arise from relativistically induced $S/D$ partial waves and cannot be explained by non-relativistic dynamics. These findings provide essential non-perturbative inputs for analyzing hard exclusive processes and highlight the importance of relativistic frameworks for understanding heavy quarkonium production and structure at modern high-energy colliders.

</details>


### [117] [Time evolution of scalar condensate decay](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08420)
*Ayuki Kamada,Kodai Sakurai*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This study analyzes the decay of scalar fields in cosmology, comparing parametric resonance and Feynman diagram approaches, revealing differing behaviors in instability bands and confirming findings through simpler Rabi models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding the decay processes of scalar fields in cosmology is crucial for analyzing the behavior of the cosmos, particularly regarding particle production and energy dynamics.

Method: The study uses numerical simulations to follow the time evolution of daughter particle phase density and compares results from parametric-resonance and Feynman-diagrammatic approaches, alongside Rabi models for analytical insights.

Result: It is demonstrated that the decay rates align in the lowest-order instability band after several scalar oscillation periods, with varying dependencies in higher-order bands, and the Bosonic Rabi model shows complete agreement between approaches while the Fermionic model reveals discrepancies attributed to quantum effects.

Conclusion: The findings highlight that, despite the complexities of decay widths in different bands, the lowest-order instability's contributions dominate, and discrepancies in the Fermionic model shed light on fundamental differences in particle statistics.

Abstract: A scalar field, which oscillates coherently over the space and decays through production of daughter particles, plays an important role in cosmology. It was recently shown that the parametric-resonance and Feynman-diagrammatic approaches give the same decay rate for a Bosonic daughter particle at a large time duration. We study how the parametric-resonance result approaches to this asymptotic value, by numerically following the time evolution of the phase density of the daughter particle. We see a difference among different instability bands in the narrow-resonance regime. The lowest-order instability band approaches to the Feynman-diagrammatic result in tens of scalar oscillation periods, while the high-order instability bands approach dependently also on a coupling between the scalar and daughter particles. This would infer that the total decay width, to which the lowest-order instability band contributes most dominantly in the perturbation theory, approaches to the Feynman-diagrammatic result also independently of the coupling. To confirm it, we consider a simpler and analytically solvable quantum mechanical model: Rabi models. In the Bosonic Rabi model, the parametric-resonance and Feynman-diagrammatic results agree with each other for all the time. On the other hand, in the Fermionic Rabi model, they disagree at a large time duration. This difference would be attributed to Bose enhancement vs Pauli blocking.

</details>


### [118] [Nonperturbative functional renormalization group for Higgs-singlet models with physics-informed neural networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08470)
*Norimi Yokozaki*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: A nonperturbative framework using physics-informed neural networks is developed to solve the Wetterich flow equation for a $Z_2$-symmetric extension of the Standard Model, offering a continuous description of the effective potential, particularly for the electroweak phase transition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome conventional polynomial expansions in the analysis of the effective potential in the context of the Wetterich flow equation, and to better model the electroweak phase transition.

Method: The authors employ a hybrid approach combining tree-level interactions with a neural network for a continuous, mesh-free treatment of the effective potential, supported by a multi-domain renormalization group flow setup.

Result: The method showcases successful application to the finite-temperature effective potential at benchmark temperatures, revealing a two-step bounce action and establishing improved numerical stability across the field-space.

Conclusion: The introduction of a soft consistency constraint is critical for deriving a physically sensible solution and highlights the challenge of balancing neural network guidance with physical accuracy.

Abstract: We develop a nonperturbative functional renormalization group framework within the LPA' to solve the Wetterich flow equation for the $Z_2$-symmetric real singlet extension of the Standard Model at zero and finite temperature, without a polynomial expansion of the scale-dependent effective potential, using a physics-informed neural network (PINN) representation. In contrast to conventional truncations based on low-order field expansions, our hybrid tree-level--plus--neural-network ansatz yields a continuous, mesh-free description of the effective potential over the full relevant field and scale range. As a proof of concept, we apply the framework to the finite-temperature effective potential relevant to the electroweak phase transition in this model: one-dimensional field-space slices at two benchmark temperatures, and a two-dimensional reconstruction at $T=100$ GeV from which a two-step bounce action is extracted. The renormalization group flow is implemented in a multi-domain setup in scale and field space with derivative matching conditions that ensure smoothness and numerical stability. Gauge and Yukawa sectors are incorporated via independently computed perturbative two-loop running couplings, and anomalous dimensions are included in the flow at the LPA' level. We benchmark the network against resummed perturbation theory and against a grid-based relaxation solver of the same equation. Finally, we introduce a soft consistency constraint that keeps the solution close, in sign and order of magnitude, to perturbation theory across the field-space domain; we find it necessary, rather than merely helpful, for selecting a physically sensible solution of the flow equation, and find that the converged result retains a residual dependence on this guidance -- through hand-tuned weights and an analytic thermal target -- which we identify as the central open problem.

</details>


### [119] [Gravitational transverse momentum dependent distributions for the gluons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08499)
*Kauship Saha,Dipansu Mishra,Dipankar Chakrabarti,Asmita Mukherjee*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper studies the gluon energy-momentum tensor in light-front formalism, establishing connections between nonlocal gluon EMT and gravitational TMDs, providing insights into hadron dynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the structural and mechanical properties of hadrons through the gluon energy-momentum tensor in a comprehensive framework.

Method: Utilizes light-front formalism to parameterize the nonlocal gluon EMT and link it to gravitational and twist-dependent distributions.

Result: Identified specific components of the gluon EMT that reveal information about gluon kinetic momentum and hadron internal properties such as pressure and shear.

Conclusion: The findings enhance understanding of hadronic structure by linking the gluon EMT to intrinsic mechanical characteristics of hadrons.

Abstract: We investigate the momentum-space structure of the gauge-invariant gluon energy-momentum tensor (EMT) for spin-$0$ and spin-$\tfrac{1}{2}$ hadrons in the light-front formalism. We parameterize the nonlocal gluon EMT in terms of so-called gluon gravitational transverse-momentum-dependent distributions (gravitational TMDs) and derive their connections to twist-2 and twist-3 gluon TMDs. Moreover, we demonstrate that particular components of the gluon EMT encode information about the average kinetic four-momentum of gluons and the internal mechanical properties of hadrons-such as pressure and shear distributions.

</details>


### [120] [A distance-independent constraint on the axion-electron coupling from RGB stars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08504)
*Thomas Levasseur,Oscar Straniero,Andrea Caputo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This study improves bounds on axion-electron coupling using the luminosity of RGB features in globular clusters, yielding a robust limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To refine existing constraints on axion-electron coupling linked to dark matter and the strong CP problem.

Method: Employing a differential observable (RGBB luminosity) to reduce sensitivity to distance and systematic uncertainties, and using maximum likelihood analysis with Monte Carlo simulations.

Result: Achieved maximum likelihood at g13 = 0.8 with a 95% C.L. upper limit of 1.49, ruling out g13 > 7.5 based on stellar evolution predictions.

Conclusion: This distance-independent method effectively constrains physics beyond the Standard Model, with potential for broader application across more globular clusters.

Abstract: Interest in axions and axion-like particles has resurged, driven by their role in solving the strong CP problem and their appeal as dark matter candidates. The luminosity of the tip of the red giant branch (TRGB) offers a key observable for probing these particle properties. This work aims to improve existing bounds on the axion-electron coupling by adopting a differential observable that is less sensitive to distance, interstellar extinction, the zero-point of bolometric corrections (BCs), and other systematic uncertainties. We use the bolometric magnitude difference between the TRGB and the RGB bump (RGBB) as a distance-independent constraint, applied to three globular clusters of intermediate-to-high metallicity: NGC 104 (47 Tuc), NGC 362, and NGC 5904 (M5). Using photometric catalogs of RGB stars, we determine V- and I-band magnitudes of both features and convert them to bolometric values. After validating that our stellar models reproduce the observed RGBB luminosity, we perform a maximum likelihood analysis with Monte Carlo simulations to propagate uncertainties and derive new bounds on the coupling. A combined analysis of the three clusters yields a maximum likelihood at g13 = gae/10^-13 = 0.8 and a 95% C.L. upper limit of 1.49. Although slightly less stringent than recent bounds from larger multi-cluster samples, this limit is substantially more robust. Under reasonable mass-loss assumptions, g13 > 7.5 is ruled out, as it predicts the disappearance of the HB and AGB phases routinely observed in globular clusters, a limit more than an order of magnitude stronger than current direct experimental bounds such as XENONnT. We demonstrate the effectiveness of this differential, distance-independent method for constraining physics beyond the Standard Model. Applying it to a wider sample of globular clusters would further refine the constraint on gae.

</details>


### [121] [Hot QCD: transport coefficients in strong and weak coupling regimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08521)
*Gabriele Parisi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This study evaluates shear viscosity and diffusion coefficients in a Quasi-Particle Model using novel lattice QCD data. The authors employ a Fokker-Planck approach and a dual-component Chapman-Enskog formalism to analyze quark-gluon interactions and compare findings across different coupling regimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand heavy quark dynamics in a QPM framework and to compute transport coefficients using advanced lattice QCD data.

Method: The authors assess shear viscosity and diffusion coefficients using tree-level Feynman diagrams, a Fokker-Planck approximation, and a dual-component Chapman-Enskog formalism, alongside comparisons to Green-Kubo approaches.

Result: They find temperature-dependent shear viscosity values and ratios for diffusion coefficients across strong and weak coupling regimes, providing insights into quark-gluon interactions.

Conclusion: This work enhances the understanding of transport phenomena in QCD and establishes benchmarks for heavy quark behavior in different thermal environments.

Abstract: We evaluate the transport coefficients, namely the shear viscosity $η$ and the spatial diffusion coefficient of heavy quarks $D_s$, within a Quasi-Particle Model (QPM). In particular, for the first time this has been done by exploiting novel lQCD data at $N_f=3+1$. In such a framework, in which the gluons are massive, the interactions among quarks and gluons have been evaluated via the scattering matrices evaluated from tree level Feynman diagrams. As far as the evaluation of $D_s$ is concerned, the Fokker-Planck approximation for the relativistic Boltzmann equation has been employed for the diffusion of both charm and bottom quarks in either a $N_f=3$ and a $N_f=3+1$ bulk. We have developed a dual-component Chapman-Enskog formalism for the ratio $η/s$ (being $s$ the entropy density) of a mixture of quarks and gluons. The temperature dependence of the shear viscosity is then compared to the results from a Green-Kubo approach, based on the numerical resolution of the relativistic Boltzmann equation with a stochastic implementation of the collision integral. Finally, using the above results, we compare the strong and weak coupling regimes for the ratio $(2πT D_s)/(4πη/s)$.

</details>


### [122] [Free $μ^{+}μ^{-}$ and bound-free $e^{+}e^{-}$ pair production in relativistic heavy-ion collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08575)
*Melek Yilmaz Şengül*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This study calculates the cross-sections for free $μ^{+}μ^{-}$ and bound-free $e^{+}e^{-}$ pair production in relativistic $Pb+Pb$ collisions at the LHC using a probability function that depends on impact parameter, employing second-order perturbation theory and Monte Carlo integration techniques. Predictions from the results aim to validate quantum electrodynamics in strong electromagnetic fields against future experiments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The study seeks to enhance understanding of particle pair production in high-energy physics, specifically within the context of quantum electrodynamics (QED) under strong electromagnetic fields.

Method: The approach involves calculating cross sections for free $μ^{+}μ^{-}$ and bound-free $e^{+}e^{-}$ pairs using impact parameter-dependent probability functions, applying second-order perturbation theory and Monte Carlo integration techniques.

Result: The study provides cross-section calculations for the simultaneous production of $μ^{+}μ^{-}$ and bound-free $e^{+}e^{-}$ pairs in high-energy collisions, comparing results with existing theoretical methods.

Conclusion: The findings offer valuable predictions that can be tested with upcoming experimental data, thus contributing to the validation of quantum electrodynamics in extreme conditions.

Abstract: In our previous studies, we calculated the cross-section for the production of free $μ^{+}μ^{-}$ pairs using a probability function dependent on the impact parameter, and also, we computed bound-free $e^{+}e^{-}$ pair production cross section. In this study, unlike the others, we obtained the cross section for the production of bound-free $e^{+}e^{-}$ pairs using the probability function dependent on the impact parameter. Here also, we simultaneously calculated the cross sections for producing free $μ^{+}μ^{-}$ with bound-free $e^{+}e^{-}$ pairs in relativistic $Pb+Pb$ collisions at the LHC with Lorentz factors at $γ=1500$ and $γ=3000$ reported in the literature. Our calculation is based on second-order perturbation theory, implemented by using Monte Carlo integration techniques. We present impact parameter dependent pair production probabilities for free $μ^{+}μ^{-}$ and bound-free $e^{+}e^{-}$ pair production processes. Notably, the bound-free $e^{+}e^{-}$ pair production was evaluated by using a formalism adapted from that of free $μ^{+}μ^{-}$ pair production. Our results obtained here for the free $μ^{+}μ^{-}$ with bound-free $e^{+}e^{-}$ pair production cross sections are compared with those from other theoretical methods available in the literature. This study aims to provide predictions that can be tested against future experimental data, thereby offering a valuable check for quantum electrodynamics (QED) in strong electromagnetic fields.

</details>


### [123] [The transverse-spin asymmetry $A^{\sinφ_{S_Λ}}_{UUT}$ in $Λ$ production in SIDIS within the collinear framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08644)
*Yongliang Yang,Zhun Lu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper analyzes the transverse-spin asymmetry in Λ hyperon production in deep inelastic scattering, focusing on the significance of a twist-3 fragmentation function and the role of strange quarks in this asymmetry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the transverse-spin asymmetry in Λ hyperon production and its dependence on quark-gluon dynamics, particularly the influence of the strange quark.

Method: The authors employ a diquark model to calculate the twist-3 T-odd quark-gluon-quark fragmentation function, fit model parameters using known fragmentation functions, and predict asymmetries in specific kinematic regimes by including QCD evolution.

Result: They determine that the transverse-spin asymmetry is sizable and can be notably altered by the contribution from strange quarks, impacting the predictions for electroproduction at EIC and EicC.

Conclusion: The findings highlight the importance of including strange-quark contributions in future analyses of transverse-spin asymmetries in hyperon production.

Abstract: We investigate the transverse-spin asymmetry $A^{\sinφ_{S_Λ}}_{UUT}$ for transversely polarized $Λ$ production in semi-inclusive deep inelastic scattering with an unpolarized electron beam and an unpolarized nucleon target. The spin-dependent asymmetry is sensitive to the twist-3 T-odd quark-gluon-quark fragmentation function $\tilde{D}_{T}$, which describes the correlation between an unpolarized fragmenting quark and the transverse polarization of the produced hadron. We calculate $\tilde{D}_{T}$ of the $Λ$ hyperon within a diquark model and investigate its contribution to the asymmetry. We determine the model parameters by fitting the fragmentation functions $D_1$ and $G_1$ to the DSV parametrization. Using the numerical results of the model and the available parametrization of the unpolarized parton distribution function $f_1$, we predict the asymmetry in the electroproduction of the $Λ$ hyperon in the kinematic regions of the EIC and the EicC. We also examine the impact of including the strange-quark contribution on the transverse-spin asymmetry. In our phenomenological analysis, we include the QCD evolution of the parton distribution function $f_1$ and the fragmentation function $\tilde{D}_{T}$. The results show that the asymmetry is sizable and can be significantly modified by the strange-quark contribution.

</details>


### [124] [From LUX-ZEPLIN to Colliders: Probing Higgsino Dark Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08712)
*Kingman Cheung,Sin Kyu Kang,Ranjeet Kumar*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper discusses a potential model for inelastic dark matter involving a 1.1 TeV Higgsino, tied to a significant recoil event observed by the LZ collaboration. It suggests specific mass splittings between neutral and charged states, and implications for future collider experiments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper aims to explore the implications of a high-energy nuclear recoil event on the search for inelastic dark matter models, specifically relating to the Higgsino.

Method: It analyzes the mass spectrum of nearly-degenerate neutral and charged states within the MSSM, investigating decay products and potential detection methods at future colliders.

Result: It predicts a mass splitting of approximately 350 MeV between the chargino and neutral Higgsino states, which produces charged tracks detectable at colliders.

Conclusion: The scenario poses no current constraints from LHC data and suggests promising avenues for testing at future high-energy colliders, highlighting the need for advanced track reconstruction capabilities.

Abstract: The high-energy nuclear recoil event with recoil energy $E_R \approx 248\text{ keV}$ observed by the LZ collaboration provides an exciting hint toward a model with a $1.1\text{ TeV}$ Higgsino inelastic dark matter. Such a recoil energy requires a mass splitting of order ($δ\approx 350\text{ keV}$) between the two nearly-degenerate neutral states. We show that within the framework of the MSSM, the model predicts a nearly-degenerate charged Higgsino state, chargino, whose mass splitting from the neutral states is of order $\mathcal{O}(350)$~MeV. The subsequent decays of such charginos once produced at colliders would lead to a sub-centimeter charged track or tracklet (with lifetime $τ\approx 0.025\text{ ns}$) at the detector. We show that such a scenario is not constrained by the current LHC bounds, and could be tested at future high-energy colliders, including HL-LHC, 100 TeV $pp$ colliders, and muon colliders. The most critical requirement is how short a track or tracklet can be reconstructed.

</details>


### [125] [Backward Compton scattering with three vortex particles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08758)
*Yi Liao,Zhaolong Teng,Hao-Lin Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The study analyzes backward Compton scattering involving vortex photons and plane-wave electrons, deriving scattering amplitudes and differential cross sections, and demonstrates potential for enhancing vortex states through energy and angular post-selection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the interactions between vortex photons and plane-wave electrons in backward Compton scattering, focusing on how final-state particles can be manipulated and controlled.

Method: Analytical derivation of scattering amplitude and differential cross sections, using a Bessel-Gaussian wave packet for the incident photon, along with numerical results for interactions with varying energies of electrons and photons.

Result: Strong energy-angle correlations and interference fringes in final-photon distributions; enhancement of different topological charge sectors through selective angular or energy measurements; matching helicity favors dominant photon channels from longitudinally polarized electrons.

Conclusion: Triple-vortex backward Compton scattering presents a promising approach for generating and controlling high-energy vortex photons and electrons.

Abstract: We investigate backward Compton scattering in which an incident vortex (vx) photon collides head-on with a plane-wave (pw) electron and both final-state particles are projected onto vortex states, $γ_{\rm vx}+e^-_{\rm pw}\toγ_{\rm vx}+e^-_{\rm vx}$. We derive analytically the scattering amplitude and differential cross sections in the energy and cone angle of either final particle. Rotational symmetry implies a selection rule associated with the conservation of the total angular momentum along the collision axis. A Bessel-Gaussian wave packet is adopted for the incident photon to provide a physical normalization and regularize the boundary singularities of ideal Bessel states. We present numerical results for a $10~{\rm MeV}$ electron colliding with a vortex photon of central energy $1~{\rm MeV}$ or $10~{\rm keV}$. The final-photon distributions exhibit strong energy--angle correlations, topological-charge-dependent interference fringes, and systematic shifts of their dominant peaks, allowing different topological charge sectors to be enhanced through angular or energy post-selection. For a longitudinally polarized incident electron, the dominant channels favor final photons of matching helicity. For the $1~{\rm MeV}$ photon benchmark, the final electron can likewise be produced as a MeV-scale vortex state with a sizable cone angle and a large angular-momentum projection. These results demonstrate that triple-vortex backward Compton scattering offers a potential means of generating and controlling high-energy vortex photons and electrons.

</details>


### [126] [Theory updates for parton distributions in Hessian formalism](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08761)
*Petja Paakkinen,Hannu Paukkunen,Sami Yrjänheikki*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper introduces a new method for parton-distribution reweighting, integrating traditional reweighting with a deweighting approach to assess theoretical updates in global analyses.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To allow researchers to evaluate the effects of new theoretical predictions on established parton-distribution analyses.

Method: The methodology combines classic reweighting techniques with a deweighting variant that removes datasets approximately from the analysis.

Result: The method's implementation in the Hessian formalism is illustrated through a case study on dimuon production in neutrino-induced DIS.

Conclusion: This approach effectively facilitates the investigation of updated theoretical frameworks and their impacts on global analyses.

Abstract: We present a new extension to the toolbox of parton-distribution reweighting methods, which enables a general user to study the impact of an updated theory prediction on the results of a pre-existing parton-distribution global analysis. This new method is a combination of the well-known reweighting method with a new deweighting variant where a dataset is removed from the original analysis in an approximate way. Specific use cases of this method could include e.g. testing the impact of updating the treatment of an observable from N$^n$LO to N$^{n+1}$LO precision in the global analysis, or including previously ignored electroweak, mass, resummation, or higher-twist effects, or even testing the impact of some beyond-standard-model physics. We discuss the implementation of this method in the Hessian formalism, and show its use in a single case study, where we update the treatment of dimuon production in neutrino-induced DIS with the novel semi-inclusive DIS approach.

</details>


### [127] [Chiral matching of dimension-7 baryon-number-violating operators and application to $ΔI=3/2$ nucleon decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08785)
*Xiao-Dong Ma*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The study classifies dim-7 baryon-number-violating operators and investigates their applications in nucleon decay processes, setting constraints on Wilson coefficients and presenting a model for these decays.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding dim-7 baryon-number-violating operators can provide insights into nucleon decay processes and their implications for physics beyond the Standard Model.

Method: The paper classifies all dim-7 operators into Lorentz structures, derives their realizations in chiral perturbation theory, and analyzes various nucleon decay modes systematically.

Result: Stringent constraints on Wilson coefficients are established using experimental bounds, leading to improved limits on the partial lifetimes of three-body decay modes derived from correlations with two-body modes.

Conclusion: The results enhance the understanding of dim-7 contributions to nucleon decays and encourage further experimental investigations into these decay modes.

Abstract: We study chiral matching of dimension-7 (dim-7) baryon-number-violating operators in low-energy effective field theory. We show that all dim-7 operators can be classified into two distinct Lorentz structures and their chirality-flipped counterparts, and we derive their leading-order hadronic realizations in chiral perturbation theory. As an application, we systematically investigate $ΔI=3/2$ nucleon decays, including $n\to \ell^-π^+$, $p\to \ell^- π^+π^+$, and $n\to \ell^- π^+ (π^0, η)$, with $\ell=e,~μ$. Using existing experimental bounds on $n\to \ell^-π^+$, we set stringent constraints on the relevant Wilson coefficients and derive improved lower limits on the partial lifetimes of the six three-body modes from their correlations with the two-body modes.
