Our QLBM is quantitatively validated through simulations of two-dimensional flows past a backward-facing step, a cylinder, and an obstacle with complex geometry, demonstrating both accuracy and extensibility of our boundary algorithm.

</details>


### [190] [Wigner Time: a data-oriented approach to experimental timeline creation for quantum science and technology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06230)
*T. W. Clark,B. Sárközi,A. Dombi,Á. Kurkó,D. Nagy,A. Simon,D. Varga,P. Domokos,A. Vukics*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper introduces Wigner Time, a Python package that simplifies the control of multi-device experiments in atomic physics by representing procedures as data rather than absolute time sequences, enhancing reuse and reducing complexity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current systems for multi-device control require tightly coupled descriptions that make modifications difficult and hinder reuse, which necessitates a more flexible approach.

Method: Wigner Time is developed as a Python package, where experimental procedures are treated as data composed of updates at named points, rather than fixed absolute times.

Result: The application of Wigner Time in two cold-atom setups demonstrated a five-fold improvement in temporal resolution compared to traditional real-time programming.

Conclusion: Wigner Time is a flexible solution for precise timing control that can be adapted to various fields requiring synchronized multi-device operations.

Abstract: Precisely timed, multi-device control in atomic, molecular and optical physics is provided by specialized real-time systems, which impose their own terms on the description of the experiment: a program over a global clock, in which every stage boundary is an absolute time computed from everything preceding it. Such descriptions are tightly coupled -- changing one stage affects all later ones -- and are correspondingly hard to reuse, inspect, or move elsewhere. We introduce Wigner Time, a Python package in which the experimental procedure is instead represented as data: a table of timed updates, assembled by composing functions and referred to named points in the experiment rather than to absolute instants. Hardware enters only at a final conversion step, leaving the description readable and back-end agnostic. Wigner Time has run two cold-atom setups for more than two years, where replacing a hand-written real-time program improved the achievable temporal resolution five-fold; the design applies to any domain requiring precise multi-device timing.

</details>


### [191] [Effects of Trotter Error, Digitization Error, and Initial State Overlap on Tapered Quantum Phase Estimation for Minimum Eigenvalue Computation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06249)
*Elijah Pelofske,Stephan Eidenbenz*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper numerically implements optimal tapered Quantum Phase Estimation for minimum eigenvalue computation, evaluating its performance across various quantum Hamiltonians and algorithm parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enhance the precision of minimum eigenvalue computation using Quantum Phase Estimation by optimizing algorithm parameters, especially in the presence of noise and errors.

Method: Numerically implements full quantum circuits of tapered Quantum Phase Estimation with optimized bandwidth-limited taper and Trotterization for Hamiltonian time-evolution.

Result: Demonstrated that optimal phase sampling rates depend on initial state overlap, with no strong evolution time dependency except at high Trotter error, and noted issues with ringing in approximated unitaries causing non-physical eigenvalue estimates.

Conclusion: The tapering and optimization techniques significantly improve accuracy in eigenvalue computation, but care must be taken with Trotter error and ringing effects.

Abstract: We numerically implement full quantum circuits of optimal tapered Quantum Phase Estimation (in the form of an implementable approximate bandwidth-limited DPSS taper) for the purpose of minimum eigenvalue computation, where the Hamiltonian time-evolution unitary is implemented using standard Trotterization, and the taper state is constructed using an optimized bandwidth-limited taper. We illustrate, on representative quantum Hamiltonians, with tractable exact classical numerical quantum circuit simulations, how tapered QPE performs with respect to optimal phase sampling rate, absolute error, Shannon entropy of the optimal phase distribution, when the tQPE algorithm parameters are changed. Those algorithm parameters are Trotter error, finite sampling, imperfect initial states, total evolution time, and digitization error (number of phase qubits). The reported numerical experiments include up to $\ell=10$ phase qubits of precision with $m=3$ additional phase-register error suppression qubits, on a $4$-qubit Heisenberg quantum magnet model Hamiltonian, using up to 12th-order Trotterization. We numerically show i) the steady-state optimal phase sampling rate is determined by the initial state overlap with the ground-state, ii) with respect to phase sampling probability, or overall eigenvalue sampling error rate, there is no strong evolution time dependence for tQPE unless there is high Trotter error, iii) the approximated time-evolution unitaries in tQPE, like in standard QPE, can result in substantial ringing, which leads to non-physical eigenvalue estimates.

</details>


### [192] [Algorithmic Randomness and Physical Typicality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06268)
*Jeffrey A. Barrett,Eddy Keming Chen,Josiah Lopez-Wild*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores the characterization of physical typicality using algorithmic randomness, specifically applying it within Bohmian mechanics to clarify statistical laws.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the concept of physical typicality and its implications for laws in physics, particularly in Bohmian mechanics.

Method: The authors utilize the theory of algorithmic randomness, particularly focusing on computable measures and Martin-Löf randomness, applied to Bohmian mechanics.

Result: By establishing a typical physical state as Martin-Löf random relative to a computable measure, the paper formulates the distribution postulate as a statistical law, ensuring standard Born statistics in a toy model.

Conclusion: Algorithmic randomness offers a precise framework for understanding statistical laws in physics, exemplified in the context of Bohmian mechanics.

Abstract: Appeals to typicality are common in physics, but it is often unclear what it means for a physical state to be typical relative to a probability measure, and correspondingly unclear what a law that appeals to typicality asserts. Here we consider how one might characterize physical typicality using ideas from the theory of algorithmic randomness. As a concrete example, we show how taking a physical state to be typical relative to a computable measure when it is Martin-Löf random allows one to formulate the distribution postulate in Bohmian mechanics as a statistical constraining law of the theory. Using a toy model, we show how this constraint guarantees the standard Born statistics for computable experimental protocols. Algorithmic Bohmian mechanics (aBM) thus illustrates how algorithmic randomness may be used to provide precise content to a statistical law.

</details>


### [193] [Efficient Quantum Circuits for Electronic Hamiltonian Simulation without Pauli Expansion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06285)
*Tamiya Onodera,Takeshi Sato*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a Lasp-based method for electronic Hamiltonian simulation, optimizing time-evolution circuits while avoiding Pauli expansions and Trotter errors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Traditional methods obscure fermionic structures and limit circuit optimization in electronic Hamiltonian simulation.

Method: The paper introduces time-evolution circuits for second-quantized electronic Hamiltonians using Lasp diagonalization, avoiding Pauli string expansion and systematically reducing CX-gate counts.

Result: The optimization reduces CX-gate counts from 36 to 12 for a triad of Lasp operators, and further reductions to O(n) for sequences of operators, while preventing Trotter errors.

Conclusion: The Lasp-based approach enhances electronic Hamiltonian simulation by optimizing circuit efficiency and preserving high-level structures.

Abstract: Electronic Hamiltonian simulation is commonly formulated by mapping fermionic operators to qubit operators and subsequently expanding the resulting ladder-operator products into Pauli strings. While general, this procedure obscures the higher-level fermionic structure and can hide opportunities for circuit optimization. Building on ladder-string-pair (Lasp) diagonalization originally developed for Hamiltonian simulation of partial differential equations, we construct time-evolution circuits for the second-quantized electronic Hamiltonian without performing a Pauli expansion. For the most general case of complex-valued coefficients, we present a time-evolution circuit for a two-body fermionic Lasp operator that corresponds to 16 Pauli strings in the Pauli-expansion approach but does not suffer from Trotter error at this stage. Expanding the optimization scope from a single operator to a triad of three fermionic Lasp operators sharing the same four spin-orbital indices enables systematic cancellation of CX gates, reducing the CX-gate count from 36 to 12 in the example considered, still without introducing Trotter error at this stage. For $n$ spin orbitals, further expanding the optimization scope to a sequence of $O(n)$ suitably ordered triads enables a cascade of CX-gate reductions across triad boundaries, reducing the CX-gate count from $O(n^2)$ to $O(n)$. The Lasp-based approach also naturally accommodates controlled time evolution and yields further optimizations for real-valued Hamiltonians. These results demonstrate that the Lasp-based approach enables more efficient time-evolution circuits by preserving high-level circuit structures and thereby expanding the scope of optimization, providing a systematic route toward more efficient electronic Hamiltonian simulation.

</details>


### [194] [Quantum Sensing of Non-Repeatable Events Enhanced by In-Sensor Quantum Reservoir Computing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06292)
*Daiki Sasaki,Hideaki Kawaguchi,Yuichiro Matsuzaki*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces NV-based quantum reservoir computing (NV-QRC) for magnetometry, particularly for non-repeatable events, demonstrating its advantage over traditional dynamical decoupling methods in retaining essential information during sensing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The limitation of conventional dynamical decoupling (DD) magnetometry in capturing non-repeatable events drives the need for a novel approach that can handle varying field waveforms through quantum computing techniques.

Method: The authors propose NV-QRC, employing high-density nitrogen-vacancy centers in diamond to implement a quantum reservoir computing framework that leverages many-body dynamics and fluorescence readouts for classifying binary phases from single events.

Result: Benchmarking against fixed DD protocols, NV-QRC effectively retains class-dependent information in scenarios where fixed sequences fail, thus demonstrating superior adaptability to non-repeatable magnetic fields.

Conclusion: The study showcases the potential of NV-QRC as a transformative tool for quantum sensing applications, specifically for scenarios involving non-repeatable events.

Abstract: High-density nitrogen-vacancy (NV) ensembles in diamond enable sensitive magnetometry. Many quantum-sensing protocols rely on reproducible target fields, allowing repeated measurements under different sensing conditions. In dynamical decoupling (DD) magnetometry, sweeping the interval between $π$ pulses across repeated measurements enables estimation of the frequency and amplitude of an unknown alternating field. For non-repeatable events, however, the same field waveform cannot be reproduced for measurements under different settings. Here we propose NV-based in-sensor quantum reservoir computing (NV-QRC) for sensing such events. Field-driven many-body dynamics and simultaneous fluorescence readout from multiple spatial regions provide a classical classifier with multiple features from a single event. For binary phase classification, we benchmark NV-QRC against DD magnetometry using a single pulse sequence fixed in advance. We show that NV-QRC can retain class-dependent information in regimes where the fixed-DD protocol fails to capture it. These results identify non-repeatable-event sensing as a promising application of quantum reservoir computing.

</details>


### [195] [Hierarchical Fourier Approximation for Variational Quantum Distribution Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06307)
*Taha Hoseinpour Asli,Sajjad Hashemian,Ebrahim Ardeshir-Larijani*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores variational quantum distribution learning using Walsh--Fourier approximations, proving learning guarantees and establishing conditions for effective training.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The need for effective quantum distribution learning techniques that leverage Fourier analysis in the context of quantum circuit Born machines.

Method: A hierarchy of Walsh--Fourier approximations is employed on the Boolean cube, with a warm-start map to initialize parameters for successive approximation levels. An end-to-end learning guarantee is derived, alongside an estimator for empirical truncations.

Result: The analysis provides an explicit statistical bound for distributional error related to quantum-state fidelity, showing a factor of $2^{n-1}$ under the total variation distance specialization.

Conclusion: While the framework does not ensure global trainability or remove barren plateaus, it delineates conditions conducive to approximation--estimation--optimization learning strategies.

Abstract: We study variational quantum distribution learning through a hierarchy of Walsh--Fourier approximations on the Boolean cube. At each level, a selected set of target Fourier coefficients defines a spectral truncation, which is projected onto the probability simplex and used as the target of a quantum circuit Born machine. Parameters learned at one level initialize the next through a warm-start map. We prove an end-to-end expected learning guarantee where the approximation term is determined by the omitted Fourier mass, while a normalized unbiased estimator yields an explicit statistical bound for empirical truncations. We then instantiate the abstract discrepancy conditions for total variation distance and relate the resulting distributional error to quantum-state fidelity. The total-variation specialization incurs the explicit factor $2^{n-1}$ under our normalized $\ell_2$ convention and is therefore informative only for sufficiently concentrated Fourier tails. The framework does not establish global trainability or eliminate barren plateaus; rather, it identifies the conditions under which low-to-high spectral training admits a approximation--estimation--optimization analysis.

</details>


### [196] [Quantum Homotopy Perturbation Method to Solve Nonlinear Partial Differential Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06357)
*Jungin E. Kim,Eunsik Choi,Yan Wang*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: A new method, quantum homotopy perturbation method (QHPM), enhances the scalability of solving nonlinear PDEs via quantum computing by maintaining the Hilbert space dimension during linearization and optimizing qubit usage and circuit depth.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper addresses the need for efficient and scalable solutions to nonlinear PDEs, which are prevalent in scientific and engineering applications.

Method: The quantum homotopy perturbation method (QHPM) utilizes homotopy perturbation to linearize PDEs while preserving the Hilbert space dimension, coupled with variational quantum simulations that reduce qubit and circuit complexity.

Result: QHPM effectively demonstrates improved scalability and efficiency in solving various nonlinear PDEs, illustrated through practical applications such as the vorticity transport equation and reduced magnetohydrodynamics equations.

Conclusion: The introduction of new criteria for truncation and circuit depth selection enhances cost-effectiveness, positioning QHPM as a promising method for nonlinear PDE solutions in quantum computing.

Abstract: Solving nonlinear partial differential equations (PDEs) is important in various scientific and engineering applications. Recently, quantum computing was introduced as an alternative computational paradigm for solving nonlinear PDEs. In this paper, a new method called the quantum homotopy perturbation method (QHPM) is proposed to improve the scalability of solving nonlinear PDEs through two aspects. First, the dimension of the Hilbert space remains the same after the nonlinear PDE is linearized through the homotopy perturbation. Second, the solutions are obtained with a variational quantum simulation framework, where the number of qubits is decreased with functional encoding and the depth of parametrized circuits is reduced. The additional contribution of this paper is the introduction of new criteria for selecting the homotopy series truncation order and circuit depth for cost-effective QHPM. The proposed approach is demonstrated with several examples, including the vorticity transport equation and the reduced magnetohydrodynamics equations.

</details>


### [197] [Trapped Ion Quantum Networking and Telecommunications Coexisting on One Fiber](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06387)
*Denton Wu,Mingzhe Han,Zehao Wang,Ana Luiza Ferrari,Mika Zalewski,Yuanheng Xie,Tingjun Chen,Norbert Linke*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This study demonstrates the coexistence of ion-photon entanglement with classical telecom signals on a single fiber, enabling quantum networks on existing infrastructure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To utilize existing fiber optic networks for long-distance quantum memory-based networking, overcoming limitations imposed by using only dark fibers.

Method: The experiment involved distributing ion-photon entanglement using Strontium-88 ions over a deployed 2.8 km fiber loop carrying both Ethernet and 5G signals, with classical control signals co-propagating on this fiber.

Result: Successful demonstration of coexistence of quantum entanglement with classical telecom signals over the same fiber infrastructure, including necessary control signals for the quantum systems.

Conclusion: Memory-based quantum networks can effectively operate over active classical infrastructure, expanding the potential for quantum communications.

Abstract: Research into long-distance quantum memory-based networking to date has exclusively used dark fibers. This avoids the detector background from telecommunications (telecom) traffic, but as a result excludes many fibers deployed in the field. If memory-photon entanglement and telecom signals coexist on one fiber, the entire classical fiber infrastructure becomes available for quantum links. We present the first experimental demonstration of such coexistence. Ion-photon entanglement using 1092 nm photons emitted by a Strontium-88 ion is distributed over a deployed 2.8 km fiber loop which carries Ethernet and 5G traffic. All classical control signals required to coordinate the quantum transmitter and receiver systems co-propagate on the same fiber. These include fiber sensing for polarization stabilization. Our results demonstrate that memory-based quantum networks can be realized on active classical network infrastructure.

</details>


### [198] [Telecom-Integrated Photonic Memory Operating Near the Mechanical Ground State](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06399)
*Jindao Tang,Bo Jing,Liping Zeng,Peiqin Chen,Hengrui Liang,Yifei Zhang,Xinyao Xu,Qizhi Cai,Xueying Zhang,Qiang Zhou,You Wang,Haizhi Song,Guangcan Guo,Guangwei Deng*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a chip-integrated optomechanical memory for telecom-band photons, achieving low phonon occupancy and demonstrating on-demand retrieval capabilities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop scalable quantum networks that require integrated quantum memories compatible with telecom band signals and flexible retrieval mechanisms.

Method: The authors demonstrate an on-chip absorptive optomechanical memory utilizing optomechanically induced transparency (OMIT) near the mechanical ground state, effectively storing and retrieving telecom-band photons.

Result: The memory device operates at 20 mK with a phonon occupancy as low as 0.32 during photon storage, indicating high efficiency and compatibility with few-photon external sources.

Conclusion: The findings support the potential for scalable phonon-based quantum memory devices and highlight the integration of mechanical systems within quantum network architectures.

Abstract: Scalable quantum networks require quantum memories that are chip-integrated, telecom-band compatible, and capable of flexible retrieval. Nanofabricated mechanical resonators meet these criteria. They offer independent tunability of optical and mechanical modes, long-lived phonon states, and design flexibility beyond atomic systems, making them strong candidates for practical integrated quantum memory. Here, we demonstrate an on-chip, absorptive optomechanical memory for telecom-band photons, based on optomechanically induced transparency (OMIT) and operating near the mechanical ground state. The device stores telecom-band photons, demonstrating compatibility with external photon sources at the few-photon level, while enabling on-demand retrieval. By placing the device in a dilution refrigerator at 20 mK and tailoring the control field to suppress optical heating, we achieve a remarkably low phonon occupancy of just 0.32 during the storage process. Our results lay the groundwork for scalable, phonon-based quantum memory devices and open new avenues for integrating mechanical systems into practical quantum network architectures.

</details>


### [199] [After Theft: From Revocation to Neutralization in the Custody of Quantum Clones](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06415)
*Gabriele Gianini,Jianyi Lin,Corrado Mio,Stelvio Cimato,Ernesto Damiani*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper presents encrypted quantum cloning as a method for securely managing quantum states post-compromise, allowing for a unique decryption that neutralizes unauthorized copies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors aim to enhance custodial security by presenting a mechanism that allows legitimate users to neutralize stolen quantum clones without the corresponding decryption key, addressing inadequacies in traditional revocation methods.

Method: The paper introduces a threat model wherein an adversary steals an encrypted clone but lacks the decryption key. It illustrates a post-theft response mechanism whereby a custodian unseals an alternative clone, consuming the sole decryption opportunity.

Result: The introduced mechanism demonstrates a stronger form of post-theft response, termed 'neutralization,' which renders the stolen quantum clone useless, contrasting with classical methods that require external procedural compositions to achieve similar outcomes.

Conclusion: Encrypted quantum cloning serves as a pivotal primitive for post-compromise quantum custody, relevant for Cyber-Humanities preservation architectures, offering significant implications in safeguarding encrypted quantum states.

Abstract: Encrypted quantum cloning enables the creation of multiple encrypted clones of an unknown quantum state while allowing only one effective decryption, with the decryption resource being intrinsically consumed in the process. In this paper, we argue that this property supports a distinctive custodial security primitive for post-compromise response. We consider a threat model in which an adversary steals an encrypted quantum clone but does not yet possess the corresponding decryption key. In such a scenario, a legitimate custodian may unseal a different trusted clone, thereby exhausting the sole available unsealing opportunity and rendering the stolen clone permanently useless. We argue that this mechanism is not adequately described as mere revocation. Rather, it realizes a stronger form of post-theft response, which we call neutralization. We formalize this distinction, locate it within a broader post-theft response space, and introduce a temporal threat model. We compare the mechanism with its closest classical analogue, showing that the classical case can reproduce the policy outcome only through external procedural composition. We finally interpret encrypted quantum cloning as a primitive for post-compromise quantum custody in distributed preservation settings, with prospective relevance for Cyber-Humanities-oriented preservation architectures.

</details>


### [200] [Generation of entanglement statistics with a large-scale integrated photonic-electronic circuit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06471)
*Volkan Gurses,Ali Hajimiri*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a novel approach to continuous-variable quantum processing by integrating optoelectronic systems with a 32-channel coherent receiver, allowing for efficient quantum information transformations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The need for scalable quantum processors that minimize loss during mode transformations motivated this research.

Method: The authors utilized a large-scale integrated photonic-electronic circuit to process squeezed vacuum states, employing radio-frequency electronics to mix analog photocurrents after homodyne detection.

Result: The system demonstrated a two-mode covariance structure with a symplectic eigenvalue of 0.99270, indicating strong entanglement below the separability bound.

Conclusion: This approach paves the way for integrating optical modes into superconducting circuits, facilitating the development of photonic-electronic quantum computers that operate across optical and microwave frequencies.

Abstract: Continuous-variable quantum processors scale with the modes they can transform, and each transformation adds loss. We demonstrate optoelectronic quantum information processing, moving the mixing into radio-frequency electronics after homodyne detection. Squeezed vacuum falls on a large-scale integrated photonic-electronic circuit, a 32-channel coherent receiver array whose radio-frequency network mixes the analog photocurrents. A local-oscillator ramp reconstructs the two-mode covariance at the aperture, which is physical, and its partial transpose has smallest symplectic eigenvalue 0.99270, three standard deviations below the separability bound. Mode transformation becomes a circuit function whose scale follows integrated electronics. With electro-optic transducers in place of the photodiodes, the same receiver would transfer optical modes into superconducting circuits and back, toward a photonic-electronic quantum computer spanning the optical and microwave bands.

</details>


### [201] [Quantum Optics of harmonic generation in the strongly driven Jaynes-Cummings-type system](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06480)
*M Suman,Sili Yi,Maria Chekhova,Misha Ivanov*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper adapts the Jaynes-Cummings model to explore the interaction between cavity quantum electrodynamics and strong field physics, highlighting the generation of quantum-correlated harmonics without approximations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate the interaction between strong light fields and the Jaynes-Cummings quantum system in the context of cavity quantum electrodynamics.

Method: Adapting the Jaynes-Cummings model to study the system driven by a strong low-frequency classical light field, focusing on multi-photon resonances.

Result: Demonstration of the generation of highly non-classical, quantum-correlated harmonics of the classical driver.

Conclusion: The findings contribute to the understanding of strong field physics and quantum optics, paying homage to Joseph Henry Eberly's contributions.

Abstract: We adapt the Jaynes-Cummings model to study the interface of cavity quantum electrodynamics with strong field and attosecond physics. We show how multi-photon resonances in the Jaynes- Cummings system driven by a strong low-frequency classical light field lead to the generation of highly non-classical, quantum-correlated harmonics of the classical driver. Our treatment assumes no approximations, apart from the typical Jaynes-Cummings model assumption of only a few discrete quantum modes of light. The paper is dedicated to Joseph Henry Eberly, whose remarkable research has left indelible mark on both strong field physics and quantum optics.

</details>


### [202] [Low-Depth Random Unitaries without Ancillae](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06528)
*Zhenyu Du,Siyuan Cheng,Xiongfeng Ma*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a novel method for generating random unitaries with optimal depth and no ancillary qubits, offering significant resource savings for quantum protocols.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Minimizing space and circuit depth for generating random unitaries is crucial for efficient quantum computing and its applications.

Method: The authors prove that random unitaries can be generated in optimal depth without ancillae, achieving a depth of $	ilde{O}(k)(	ext{log}n)^{1/	ext{δ}}$ for approximate $k$-designs and $	ilde{O}(k)	ext{log}	ext{log}n$ with all-to-all connectivity, extending this to exact $k$-designs using an exactification lemma.

Result: The proposed method leads to substantial reductions in resource requirements compared to existing exact constructions, enhancing the efficiency of quantum protocols.

Conclusion: This work significantly reduces space-time costs for a variety of quantum applications by enabling optimal-depth random unitary generation without ancillary qubits.

Abstract: Random unitaries are fundamental to quantum information and many-body physics, with widespread applications ranging from quantum learning and metrology to device benchmarking. A central pursuit is to minimize the space and circuit depth required to generate them. However, existing methods for generating low-depth random unitaries rely heavily on an extensive number of ancillary qubits, imposing severe spatial overhead. In this work, we prove that random unitaries can be generated in optimal depth without ancillae. For multiplicative-error approximate $k$-designs on $n$ qubits, our circuits achieve a depth of $\widetilde{O} (k) (\log n)^{1/δ}$ on $δ$-dimensional architectures and $\widetilde{O}(k) \log \log n$ with all-to-all connectivity. Furthermore, by introducing a general exactification lemma, we lift our construction to optimal-depth exact $k$-designs, yielding an exponential resource reduction over state-of-the-art exact constructions. Our results minimize the space-time costs for a wide range of quantum protocols.

</details>


### [203] [Quantum bivariate bicycle codes with weight-8 checks surpassing the BB benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06572)
*Liangdong Lu,Ruipan Yang,Guanmin Guo*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores and establishes new bivariate bicycle (BB) quantum codes with weight-$8$ checks, demonstrating improved performance over existing codes through rigorous algebraic analysis and empirical validation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enhance quantum communication and improve upon existing BB codes by constructing new codes with higher distances and logical qubit capacity.

Method: The authors develop algebraic structures for BB-type codes with weight-$8$ checks, utilizing a validated search pipeline to construct and certify new codes and proving various theoretical results including distance equalities and symmetry group properties.

Result: The study identifies 53 new codes that surpass benchmark distances, including codes with parameters $[[144,6,d
geq 15]]$ and $[[72,14,8]]$, which shows significant performance improvements.

Conclusion: The constructed codes not only exceed existing benchmarks in logical qubit encoding but also offer a better threshold for circuit-level implementations, validating the practical efficiency of the proposed weight-$8$ code structures.

Abstract: Bivariate bicycle (BB) codes of Bravyi \emph{et al.}~\cite{Bravyi2024} are quantum low-density parity-check codes with weight-$6$ checks, exemplified by $[[144,12,12]]$ with $kd^2/n=12$. We develop the algebraic structure theory of BB-type codes with weight-$8$ checks (weight-$4$ generator polynomials) and use it, together with an exactly validated search pipeline, to construct and certify new codes. We prove an exact dimension formula $k=2\dim R/(A,B)$ (forcing even $k$), a $4\ell m$-element symmetry group on generator pairs, an $X/Z$ distance equality $d_X=d_Z$, and a family of subgroup-coset kernel vectors giving rigorous distance upper bounds and a design rule for high-distance constructions; all distances are computed exhaustively by a cross-validated bit-mask verifier. At $n=144$ the pipeline returns a census of $53$ codes whose strongest members surpass the BB benchmark: $[[144,6,d\ge 15]]$ exceeds the benchmark distance $12$ (certified $d\ge 15$), $[[144,10,12]]$ reaches it with weight-$8$ checks, and $[[144,16,10]]$ encodes a third more logical qubits at $kd^2/n=11.11$ ($7.4\%$ below benchmark) while decoding no worse. At $n=72$, $[[72,14,8]]$ attains $kd^2/n=12.44$---more than twice the same-length BB code---and decodes better; a circuit-level memory experiment places our weight-$8$ codes at $\approx 0.1\%$ pseudo-threshold versus $\approx 0.4\%$ for the BB reference under an identical model, quantifying the threshold cost of the heavier checks. All structural statements are verified numerically on the whole census.

</details>


### [204] [Solution-phase fluorination of nanodiamond: near-surface NV$^-$ activation and spin relaxation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06605)
*Szabolcs Czene,Olga Krafcsik,Nikoletta Jegenyés,Dávid Beke,László Péter,Vladimir Verkhovlyuk,Adam Gali*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores two fluorination methods for enhancing the stability of nitrogen-vacancy centers in nanodiamonds, achieving a high NV$^-$ fraction and evaluating their effects on spin relaxation times.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enhance the stability and application of fluorescent nanodiamonds (FND) in quantum technology by optimizing the surface chemistry of NV$^-$ centers.

Method: Two fluorination methods were employed: one using xenon difluoride for direct C-F bond formation and the other using the Balz-Schiemann reaction to replace surface amino groups with fluorine. The resulting structures were characterized using various spectroscopic techniques.

Result: Both methods achieved a high NV$^-$ fraction (~90% on average), with near-total conversion in fluorine-rich areas, and demonstrated significant spin-lattice relaxation times (733μs and 712μs for XeF$_2$ and Balz-Schiemann methods, respectively), although less than the 1173μs of the unmodified material.

Conclusion: While fluorination improves NV$^-$ activation, it also increases susceptibility to surface noise for near-surface centers, highlighting a trade-off between charge-state stability and spin lifetime.

Abstract: Nanodiamonds hosting luminescent point defects, known as fluorescent nanodiamonds (FND), are a leading platform for quantum technology. The nitrogen-vacancy (NV) centre is the most intensively studied of these; its negatively charged state (NV$^-$) can serve as a qubit at room temperature, and its stability is governed by surface functional groups. We present two solution-phase fluorination routes for stabilising NV$^-$: direct C-F bond formation by decarboxylation with xenon difluoride via a radical mechanism, and the Balz-Schiemann reaction, which replaces surface amino groups with fluorine. The two routes were compared by infrared, X-ray photoelectron, energy-dispersive X-ray, Raman and photoluminescence spectroscopy. Both gave a high NV$^-$ fraction, up to $\sim$90% on average and approaching 100% in fluorine-rich regions, which to our knowledge is among the highest reported for surface-terminated nanodiamonds of this size and, in particular, for fluorine termination. Frequency-domain relaxometry shows that the fluorinated particles retain a long spin-lattice relaxation time, $733\pm56$ and $712\pm20$ $μ$s for the XeF$_2$ and Balz-Schiemann routes, several times the values reported for commercial HPHT nanodiamonds, although shorter than the $1173\pm123$ $μ$s of the as-received material. Charge-state stability and spin lifetime therefore do not improve together: fluorination activates near-surface NV$^-$ centres, which are the most exposed to surface noise but also the ones that dominate relaxometric sensing.

</details>


### [205] [Spectral bounds for the partial transpose](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06617)
*Wei-Jie Jiang,Jing-Tao Qiu,Xiao-Dong Yu*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces the Ky Fan $k$-negativity, a hierarchical generalization of negativity, providing spectral bounds for partial transpose and its applications in entanglement certification.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the finer structure of negativity by moving beyond the total weight of negative eigenvalues, which remains largely unexamined.

Method: Introduction of Ky Fan $k$-negativity; development of a spectral graph optimization approach to derive absolute bounds; complete resolution of the $k=1$ case, including an analysis of its underlying graph structure.

Result: The derivation of exact bounds for the Ky Fan $k$-negativity through a cubic equation, alongside both analytical and numerical analyses under fixed-purity constraints.

Conclusion: The Ky Fan $k$-negativity effectively certifies genuine multilevel entanglement and converts the $p_3$-PPT condition into a computable lower bound on negativity.

Abstract: Among the various entanglement measures, the negativity stands out not only for its clear physical meaning but also for being directly computable from the spectrum of the partial transpose. However, the negativity captures only the total weight of the negative eigenvalues, whereas the finer structure of the negative spectrum remains largely unexplored. In this work, we fill this gap by introducing a hierarchical generalization of the negativity, the Ky Fan $k$-negativity, and developing a unified analytical framework for deriving spectral bounds on the partial transpose. To obtain the absolute bounds, we reduce the maximization problem to a spectral graph optimization, whose solution yields the exact bound for every $k$ through a single cubic equation. We further investigate these spectral bounds both analytically and numerically under a fixed-purity constraint. In particular, we solve the $k=1$ case completely and uncover a simple underlying graph structure. As two direct applications, we show that the Ky Fan $k$-negativity robustly certifies genuine multilevel entanglement and that it converts the $p_3$-PPT condition into a quantitative lower bound on the negativity.

</details>


### [206] [Anomaly fluctuation theorem for quantum coherence dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06632)
*Kun Zhang,Hai-Long Shi,Xiao-Hui Wang,Wen-Li Yang*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces an integral fluctuation theorem for quantum coherence dynamics, providing new statistical constraints and insights using Kirkwood-Dirac quasiprobabilities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the limited understanding of the dynamics governing quantum coherence, a critical resource in quantum information science.

Method: An exact integral fluctuation theorem is derived using Kirkwood-Dirac quasiprobability trajectories, applicable to arbitrary initial states.

Result: The theorem reveals a deviation termed the anomaly, which quantifies relationships between residual coherence and coherence generated from dephased states, with real and imaginary parts yielding distinct statistical bounds.

Conclusion: The findings establish new general statistical constraints on coherence dynamics, underscoring the utility of fluctuation theorems in quantum resource studies.

Abstract: Quantum coherence is a central resource in quantum information science, yet general frameworks and constraints governing its dynamics remain limited. Although quantum coherence lacks a general monotonicity law, we establish an exact integral fluctuation theorem (FT) for coherence dynamics, formulated in terms of Kirkwood-Dirac (KD) quasiprobability trajectories and valid for arbitrary initial states and dynamics. This integral FT deviates from the standard unit-valued form, and the complex deviation, termed the anomaly, quantifies a weighted overlap between the residual final-state coherence and the coherence generated from the dephased input. The real part of the anomaly yields bounds on coherence change, while its imaginary part constrains the second-order moment of the stochastic coherence change weighted by the imaginary parts of the KD quasiprobabilities. Our results establish general statistical constraints on coherence dynamics, demonstrating the utility and broad applicability of FTs for studying quantum-resource dynamics.

</details>


### [207] [Entanglement Growth as Transport Across Schmidt Scales](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06643)
*Shi-Xin Zhang,Shuo Liu,Yu-Qin Chen*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces new concepts to analyze quantum entanglement growth, presenting coordinates that reveal the dynamics of entanglement across different scales, particularly in the context of disorder in spin chains.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper addresses the limitation of summarizing quantum entanglement growth with a single entropy figure, which obscures details about correlations in the Schmidt spectrum.

Method: The authors define Schmidt-scale concentration and dominant Schmidt scale to analyze quenched random-field spin chain dynamics. These concepts are used to characterize the transport of entanglement across Schmidt ranks under varying disorder conditions.

Result: The study finds distinct behaviors in the transport of entanglement: at weak disorder, the dominant scale moves quickly to higher ranks, while at strong disorder, transport is suppressed even as entropy increases logarithmically. A hierarchy of dynamics is established which includes initial entropy production and delays in dominant transport.

Conclusion: Schmidt-scale coordinates serve as effective tools for probing intricate entanglement structures, providing insights into entanglement production and reorganization that go beyond traditional entropy measurements.

Abstract: Quantum entanglement growth is commonly summarized by a single entropy, obscuring where correlations reside in the exponentially large Schmidt spectrum and how they form. Here, we introduce Schmidt-scale concentration and dominant Schmidt scale, two coordinates that locate the probability maximum across logarithmic windows in ordered Schmidt-rank space. Applied to quenches of a random-field spin chain, these coordinates distinguish rapid transport of the dominant scale to higher Schmidt rank at weak disorder from strongly suppressed transport despite continued logarithmic entropy growth at strong disorder. The disorder-averaged dynamics exhibit an ordered hierarchy: entropy production peaks first, spectral roughness and exact nonlocal magic peak next, and dominant-Schmidt-scale transport becomes typical only after a substantial delay. Moreover, a solvable head--tail model and controlled numerical experiments reveal the physical origin of this hierarchy: the spectral path determines the order of events, local dynamics on active exchange bonds set their early timing, and intra-subsystem many-body dressing further delays dominant-Schmidt-scale transport. These results establish the Schmidt-scale coordinates as powerful dynamical probes for uncovering fine-grained entanglement structures distinguishing entanglement production, entanglement-spectrum reorganization, and dominant-Schmidt-scale transport beyond entropy alone.

</details>


### [208] [Efficient Representation of multicategorical local Hilbert spaces: nonlinear Restricted Boltzmann Machines to Kolmogorov-Arnold Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06672)
*Abhiroop Lahiri*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper proposes a novel approach to represent multi-categorical quantum systems using neural networks, specifically optimizing parameter efficiency and trainability while capturing critical behaviors in quantum models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the limitations of existing NQS representations for systems with multiple local states, which suffer from high parameter counts and optimization challenges.

Method: The proposed method employs nonlinear functions of multi-valued spin variables instead of the conventional one-hot encoded MLP architecture, using both Restricted Boltzmann Machines and Kolmogorov-Arnold Networks.

Result: This approach reduces the parameter count compared to one-hot encoding and enhances trainability, while accurately reproducing the critical behavior of quantum Potts systems.

Conclusion: The framework effectively models multi-categorical quantum states, capturing essential quantum phenomena while optimizing computational resources.

Abstract: Neural network quantum states (NQS) for representing spin-$\frac{1}{2}$ systems are typically built from Multi-Layer Perceptrons (MLPs) with binary visible variables, in which the input spins are first combined through linear affine maps before more expressive nonlinear transformations are applied. The standard way for representing multi-categorical systems, namely spin-1 and spin-2, or the q-state quantum Potts systems with more than two local degrees of freedom, is a unary or one-hot encoded MLP architecture. Following a few similar studies, I observe that while the one-hot construction is the mathematically faithful encoding for systems with more than two local states such as spin-$S$ models with $S > 1/2$ or $q$-state Potts models, its parameter count grows with the number of local states, and the resulting optimization landscape can hinder convergence. I find numerically that allowing $\log Ψ$ to be a nonlinear function of the raw multi-valued spin variable is a natural categorical generalization: it preserves the labelling freedom of the local basis and reproduces the one-hot model with strictly fewer parameters, often with improved trainability. I first benchmark this idea on shallow Restricted Boltzmann Machines (RBMs), equipping them with several distinct nonlinear connections, for spin-1, 2, and 3 Heisenberg chains. I then turn to Kolmogorov-Arnold Networks (KANs), where each edge carries a learnable univariate nonlinearity, and show that they provide a strictly more expressive realization of the same principle. Finally, I demonstrate that this framework captures the critical behaviour of the quantum Potts Hamiltonian, recovering its phase transition.

</details>


### [209] [Contact order governs the onset of entanglement cascades](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06675)
*Sugumi Kanno,Jiro Soda*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper establishes a link between entangling rates and multipartite entanglement through the contact order of physical trajectories, introducing a geometric approach to understand the emergence of entanglement in quantum systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the relationship between entangling rates and multipartite entanglement, especially when entangling channels are forbidden, by introducing a method based on geometric concepts in pure-state dynamics.

Method: The authors derive a time-ordered recursion that calculates the contact order $m_	ext{star}$, which characterizes the first non-reproducible Taylor order of physical trajectories relative to the $S|E$ product manifold, focusing on how this relates to entanglement formation over time.

Result: The method demonstrates that genuine $(n+1)$-partite entanglement corresponds to the contact order $m_	ext{star}$, with results from both symmetry-protected three-qubit and three-mode bosonic models showcasing emergent entanglement despite separability in certain subsystems.

Conclusion: This work provides a geometric framework for understanding entanglement cascades, emphasizing that new subsystems can achieve entanglement only following interactions defined by the established contact order.

Abstract: Entangling rates describe a direct entangling channel, but give no information when that channel is forbidden. For finite-dimensional analytic pure-state dynamics, we show that the onset of genuine $(n+1)$-partite entanglement is determined by the contact order $m_\star$ between the physical trajectory and the $S|E$ product manifold. It is the first Taylor order that cannot be reproduced by any product curve, or equivalently the first nonvanishing order of the Fubini--Study distance from the product manifold. We derive a time-ordered recursion that removes curvature-induced kinematic terms and computes $m_\star$ from the Taylor coefficients of $H(t)$. This provides a geometric description of an entanglement cascade, in which new subsystems can join multipartite entanglement only after one or more interaction steps. A symmetry-protected three-qubit model realizes $m_\star=2$, and a three-mode bosonic example shows that genuine tripartite entanglement can arise even when the two newly formed reduced pairs remain separable.

</details>


### [210] [Error Exponents of Probabilistic Quantum Resource Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06688)
*Xian Shi*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a framework for analyzing conditional error exponents in quantum resource distillation, showcasing advantages of postselection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a better understanding of probabilistic resource distillation in quantum mechanics and find ways to improve error rates in resource processing.

Method: The authors establish a framework using one-shot bounds and relate distillation to postselected quantum hypothesis testing for various resource theories.

Result: They derive general bounds, explicit closed-form formulas for conditional error exponents, and identify improvements in error decay rates due to postselection.

Conclusion: Postselection in quantum resource distillation is beneficial, offering operational advantages and providing a new tool for analyzing probabilistic tasks.

Abstract: In this manuscript, we establish a unified framework for analyzing the conditional error exponents of probabilistic resource distillation under approximately resource-nongenerating instruments. For generic quantum resource theories satisfying suitable structural conditions, we derive general one-shot bounds on the conditional distillation error exponents. By relating probabilistic distillation to postselected composite quantum hypothesis testing, we further obtain analytical characterizations of the conditional error exponents for entanglement, coherence, and magic distillation at finite blocklength, and derive their asymptotic expressions in the zero-rate regime. For several representative families of states in entanglement and magic resource theories, these characterizations reduce to explicit closed-form formulas. Comparing them with the corresponding deterministic distillation exponents, we identify regimes in which postselection yields a strict improvement in the exponential decay rate of the conditional error. Our results reveal an operational advantage of postselection in quantum resource distillation and establish postselected composite hypothesis testing as a general tool for characterizing probabilistic resource-processing tasks.

</details>


### [211] [Thermodynamic Performance of a Measurement-Driven Quantum Engine with a Two-Parameter $(p,q)$-Deformed Harmonic Oscillator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06695)
*Tunde Joseph Osunmusanmi,Berihu Teklu*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper proposes a measurement-driven quantum engine using a deformed harmonic oscillator to optimize work output through quantum measurement backaction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how quantum measurement processes can enhance the efficiency and work output of quantum engines utilizing nontraditional oscillators.

Method: The study uses a quantum engine cycle involving a Gibbs state, adiabatic changes, Gaussian measurements, thermalization, and a consistent first-law approach to analyze contributions from measurement heat and adiabatic work.

Result: Numerical analysis indicates that the use of a $(p,q)$-deformed harmonic oscillator can lead to enhanced energy extraction compared to undeformed systems under specific operational conditions.

Conclusion: The findings indicate that while the reported efficiency is lower and excludes measurement costs, deformation in the system effectively increases the performance of measurement-driven quantum engines.

Abstract: We address a measurement-driven, single-bath quantum engine that generates usable work using quantum measurement backaction. The working medium is a two-parameter $(p,q)$-deformed harmonic oscillator, whose nonlinear spectrum modifies the energy gaps sampled by a nonselective Gaussian measurement and the work exchanged during quasistatic adiabatic strokes. The cycle starts from a Gibbs state and consists of an adiabatic change of $(ω,p,q)$, measurement of the final effective position quadrature, a reverse adiabatic stroke, and thermalization with the original bath. Using a consistent first-law convention, we derive the measurement heat, adiabatic work contributions, thermalization heat, and reduced working-medium efficiency. We also express the measurement stroke via a transition matrix and identify passivity/unitality conditions that ensure nonnegative measurement heat. Numerical scans show that, within the finite-basis, spectral-ordering, and engine-operation checks used in this work, deformation can enhance measurement-induced energy input and extracted work relative to the undeformed oscillator. The physical engine regime is selected by $\QM>0$, $\Wcyc<0$, $\QT<0$, and $0<η<1$. The reported efficiency is reduced and excludes measurement-apparatus costs.

</details>


### [212] [Scale-Free Localization Morphing in Bilocally Coupled Hatano-Nelson Chains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06799)
*Chong Wang,Linhu Li*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper explores the critical non-Hermitian skin effect in Hatano--Nelson chains, revealing critical behavior in strong coupling scenarios where local hybridization leads to fragmented low-energy states and supports scale-free skin states.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding anomalous critical non-Hermitian effects in tightly coupled systems can shed light on new physical phenomena in non-Hermitian quantum mechanics.

Method: The study employs a model of two Hatano--Nelson chains with local coupling, analyzing the effects of strong inter-chain coupling and local hybridization.

Result: The analysis demonstrates that strong local hybridization generates impurity modes and fragments the Hilbert space, yielding scale-free skin states and a specific count of low-energy states.

Conclusion: Local coupling is shown to drive critical non-Hermitian behavior, suggesting a novel mechanism for such effects outside traditional weak-coupling frameworks.

Abstract: We investigate an anomalous critical non-Hermitian skin effect (CNHSE) in two Hatano--Nelson chains coupled locally at two bulk sites separated by an inclusive distance $d$. Whereas conventional CNHSE is commonly associated with weakly coupled subsystems, the critical behavior in our system persists in the strong inter-chain-coupling regime. Strong local hybridization generates high-energy impurity modes and effectively fragments the low-energy Hilbert space into geometry-selected sectors, one of which supports scale-free skin states. Independently, the count of states selected in the outer sector follows $2(L-d)$ for the two-chain system. These results establish local-coupling-induced fragmentation as a route to critical non-Hermitian behavior beyond the weak-coupling paradigm.

</details>


### [213] [Hilbert space geometry change by a nonadiabatic electromagnetic field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06800)
*Ivan Georgiev Koprinkov*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper reveals that the Hilbert space geometry of a quantum system changes due to interaction with a nonadiabatic electromagnetic field, shifting states from orthogonal to non-orthogonal.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how the geometric structure of quantum systems is altered when subjected to varying electromagnetic fields, particularly in nonadiabatic conditions.

Method: Investigated the evolution of quantum states and their orthogonality through matrix elements under three scenarios: closed system, adiabatic field, and nonadiabatic field.

Result: In nonadiabatic fields, quantum states transition from orthogonality to non-orthogonality, which can be captured by a time-dependent Fubini-Study metric of the Hilbert space.

Conclusion: The findings suggest a parallel between the dynamical metrics of Hilbert space in quantum mechanics and those of spacetime in general relativity.

Abstract: A change in the geometry of the Hilbert space of a quantum system interacting with a nonadiabatic electromagnetic field is found. To identify its physical origin, the evolution of orthogonality to non-orthogonality of the states of the quantum system has been investigated based on the matrix elements of the quantum states in three main physical cases: a quantum system in zero external field (closed quantum system), a quantum system in slowly changing (adiabatic) field, and a quantum system in rapidly changing (nonadiabatic) field. The quantum states are orthogonal in the first two cases and change to non-orthogonal in a nonadiabatic field, following continuously the parameters of the field. A time-dependent Fubini-Study metric of the Hilbert space can be determined based on the matrix elements of the quantum states in the nonadiabatic case. Such a dynamical metric of the Hilbert space in quantum mechanics resembles the dynamical metric of spacetime in general relativity.

</details>


### [214] [Spectral Twisting in a Common Bosonic Reservoir: Fragility of Two-Qubit Dark-State Protection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06802)
*Fabio Borrelli,Giovanni Miano,Carlo Forestiere*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper investigates the effects of spectral twisting in two-qubit systems coupled to a correlated bosonic reservoir, focusing on how it influences leakage and dark-state protection over different frequencies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how frequency-dependent characteristics of the spectral-density matrix affect the dynamics and stability of coupled qubit systems in quantum information processes.

Method: The authors characterize the spectral-density matrix, quantify spectral twisting through the Fubini--Study speed, and apply nonperturbative hierarchical-equations-of-motion calculations to analyze leakage from dark states.

Result: Spectral twisting leads to frequency-dependent leakage from locally dark states, shifts the optimal protected state, and accelerates the decay of entanglement (Werner-state concurrence), showing a quadratic relationship between leakage and twisting parameters.

Conclusion: The findings offer insights for engineering dark states in structured reservoirs, with potential applications in noise-resistant quantum technologies and enhanced decoherence management.

Abstract: The interaction of two qubits with a common bosonic reservoir is characterized by the spectral-density matrix $\mathbf{J}(ω)$, whose diagonal entries $J_{11}(ω)$ and $J_{22}(ω)$ describe the local spectra, while the off-diagonal entries encode cross-correlations. For a maximally correlated reservoir, $\mathbf{J}(ω)$ has rank one and therefore a \textit{locally} dark coupling direction at each frequency. If $J_{22}(ω)/J_{11}(ω)$ varies with frequency, however, the bright and dark directions rotate and $\ker\mathbf{J}(ω)$ is frequency dependent. We call this \textit{spectral twisting} and quantify it through the Fubini--Study speed $τ(ω)$ of the bright spectral projector. We investigate how spectral twisting affects coupled two-qubit dynamics and dark-state protection. We quantify protection loss by the survival \textit{leakage} $P_{\mathrm{leak}}(t)$, which can become finite for states that are dark only locally in frequency. By comparing rotating-wave dynamics and untwisted asymmetric reservoirs, together with analyzing qubit detuning, we distinguish twisting from coupling asymmetry, thermal absorption, counter-rotating processes, and Hamiltonian symmetry breaking. For mismatched Drude--Lorentz spectra, our nonperturbative hierarchical-equations-of-motion calculations show that twisting induces leakage from the singlet, which is locally dark at the spectral crossing $ω_\times$ defined by $J_{11}(ω_\times)=J_{22}(ω_\times)$. Twisting also shifts the optimally protected state and accelerates the decay of Werner-state concurrence. At fixed observation time, we find the quadratic weak-twisting scaling $P_{\mathrm{leak}}(t)\propto[ω_\timesτ(ω_\times)]^2$. These results may guide dark-state engineering in structured reservoirs, with implications for correlated-noise spectroscopy and decoherence-free encodings.

</details>


### [215] [Quantum-Enhanced Learning Framework for Intelligent and AI-Native 6G Wireless Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06814)
*Muhammad Bilal Akram Dastagir,Rakesh Saini,Omer Tariq,Saif Al-Kuwari,Shahid Mumtaz,Anwer Al-Dulaimi,Ahmed Farouk*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents Quantumer, a hybrid TinyML-quantum framework for efficient intrusion detection in resource-constrained edge networks, utilizing a lightweight transformer architecture alongside a four-qubit variational circuit for quantum-assisted representation learning.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The convergence of 6G wireless systems and TinyML highlights the need for effective on-device intelligence in edge networks, which face challenges such as ultra-low latency and constrained resources.

Method: Quantumer integrates multi-scale dilated convolutions and scaled dot-product attention in a lightweight transformer model, employing a two-stage transfer learning approach: Quantum Pre-Training followed by Classical Fine-Tuning. A four-qubit variational circuit, QuantiblentLayer, is used for offline embedding preparation.

Result: Quantumer-Q demonstrates competitive performance with 105.86K parameters and low memory requirements (0.4038 MB), achieving a deployment latency of 16.8413 ms on a Raspberry Pi 4.

Conclusion: The study validates the effectiveness of quantum-assisted representation learning in improving detection performance while maintaining energy efficiency for edge-deployable intrusion detection systems.

Abstract: The recent convergence of 6G wireless systems and Tiny Machine Learning (TinyML) has driven the need for on-device intelligence in edge networks, where ultra-low latency, stringent energy budgets, and tight compute constraints demand novel architectures. Lightweight deep models efficiently extract local patterns but fail to capture global dependencies, while attention mechanisms do so at the expense of energy and computational cost. To bridge this gap, we introduce Quantumer, a hybrid TinyML--quantum framework that integrates multi-scale dilated convolutions and scaled dot-product attention within a lightweight transformer architecture, employing a two-stage transfer learning pipeline from Quantum Pre-Training (Quantumer-Q) to Classical Fine-Tuning (Quantumer-C). We also present QuantiblentLayer, a four-qubit variational circuit that maps compact traffic representations into measurement-based Hilbert-space features using trainable rotations and cyclic entangling operations. The circuit is used only during offline pre-training as a nonlinear embedding teacher and is removed before Quantumer-C deployment, leaving a fully classical inference model without runtime quantum execution. By transferring these quantum-assisted embeddings into an energy-efficient, lightweight transformer, Quantumer achieves strong detection performance with minimal compute and memory overhead on resource-constrained edge devices. The intrusion detection system (IDS) is used as a case study and evaluated on the Edge-IIoTset, TON IoT, and WUSTL-IIoT-2021 datasets. Quantumer-Q achieves competitive compact-model performance with 105.86K parameters, 0.4038 MB memory usage, 0.5525 MB model size, and 5.5646 MFLOPs; the INT8 Raspberry Pi 4 deployment obtains 16.8413 ms latency with a 0.6493 MB footprint. These results support training-time quantum-assisted representation learning for compact edge-deployable IDS.

</details>


### [216] [Trace-class spectra of irreducible Gaussian quantum Markov semigroups](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06957)
*Franco Fagnola,Zheng Li*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper analyzes the spectrum of the predual generator of a Gaussian quantum Markov semigroup across different drift regimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the spectral properties of irreducible Gaussian quantum Markov semigroups specifically in trace-class operators over a finite-mode bosonic Fock space.

Method: The analysis employs characteristic functions, Gaussian diffusion semigroups, and bounded eigenoperators of the dual semigroup to determine the spectrum and point spectrum under different drift conditions.

Result: Identified spectral characteristics: for stable drift, a unique invariant state exists, with spectra in the closed left half-plane; for strictly positive drift eigenvalues, the point spectrum is empty; for periodic drift, an explicit formula shows horizontal or parabolic spectral configurations.

Conclusion: The nonperiodic critical drift case remains unsolved, presenting potential future research directions.

Abstract: We determine the spectrum of the predual generator of an irreducible Gaussian quantum Markov semigroup on the trace-class operators over a finite-mode bosonic Fock space in the stable, strictly unstable, and periodic critical drift regimes. For stable drift, irreducibility is equivalent to the existence of a unique faithful normal invariant state. The spectrum and approximate point spectrum are the closed left half-plane, whereas the point spectrum is the open left half-plane together with zero. Thus the polynomial eigenvalues generated by the drift matrix do not exhaust the trace-class point spectrum. If the drift has an eigenvalue with strictly positive real part, the spectrum is again the closed left half-plane, but the point spectrum is empty and the open left half-plane together with zero belongs to the residual spectrum. For periodic critical drift, we obtain an explicit spectral formula that includes the displacement parameter and yields horizontal half-lines or parabolic regions. The proofs use characteristic functions, Gaussian diffusion semigroups, and bounded eigenoperators of the dual semigroup. The nonperiodic critical drift case remains open.

</details>


### [217] [Coherence-Mediated Boundary Control of the Liouvillian Gap in Nonreciprocal Open Quantum Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06981)
*K. L. Li,M. H. Yu,X. Z. Zhang*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This study examines a single-particle Lindblad lattice featuring nonreciprocal incoherent hopping and a tunable coherent boundary link, influencing the Liouvillian gap through population-coherence-population feedback, with implications for various lattice geometries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how coherent boundary links affect the dynamics of open quantum systems, particularly in terms of population coherence and relaxation behaviors.

Method: Analyzing a single-particle Lindblad lattice with fixed incoherent jump rates and a tunable coherent link, employing exact diagonalization and reduced models to derive hydrodynamic equations and relaxation scales.

Result: The coherent boundary link modifies the Liouvillian gap by influencing the population-coherence dynamics, leading to effects that depend on the lattice geometry and coherences, with notable differences in relaxation scales across one and two dimensions.

Conclusion: The findings reveal the critical role of coherence in controlling the gaps and relaxation rates in open quantum systems, with both exact analytical benchmarks and numerical simulations supporting the theories proposed.

Abstract: We study a single-particle Lindblad lattice in which nonreciprocal incoherent hopping is combined with a tunable coherent boundary link. The link changes the Hamiltonian boundary condition while the incoherent jump rates are kept fixed, so it does not directly alter the diagonal population generator. Its effect on the Liouvillian gap comes from population-coherence-population feedback: the Hamiltonian couples populations to damped coherences, and eliminating those coherences produces a frequency-dependent self-energy for the slow population branch. For the periodic reference system, where translation symmetry is imposed on both the Hamiltonian and the dissipator, we derive an exact fixed-momentum reduction, a scalar secular equation for the population-connected branch, and the associated hydrodynamic drift and coherence-corrected diffusion. For open finite lattices, exact diagonalization and sparse near-zero eigensolvers show that the coherent boundary link can enhance or suppress the gap in one dimension and produce geometry-dependent responses in two dimensions. We also show that an observed relaxation scale can differ from $1/Δ_{\mathcal L}$ when the gap family has weak visibility in the chosen perturbation and observable. The periodic construction gives exact analytical benchmarks, while finite-size simulations in one and two dimensions demonstrate how the same coherence-mediated mechanism controls open-boundary gaps and observable relaxation.

</details>


### [218] [A Single Fixed Shallow Circuit for Classical Shadows of Arbitrary n-Qubit States](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07032)
*Yu Wang,Xiuwu Zhu*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a fixed shallow quantum analyzer that streamlines classical shadow techniques by using a single measurement setting, which simplifies the extraction of quantum properties while maintaining efficient outcomes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the control and calibration complexities associated with randomized measurement settings in classical shadows, enabling more efficient quantum state reconstruction.

Method: The proposed analyzer combines an n-qubit fiducial register with a parallel Bell readout, producing a compact record of quantum information while employing a fixed measurement setting instead of the conventional multiple local-Pauli settings.

Result: The proposed method retains minimum outcomes for state reconstruction, achieves dimension-independent variance properties, and demonstrates bounded variances for specific states, significantly reducing control costs in measurement settings.

Conclusion: The analyzer allows for compiling randomness and control complexity into a fixed structure, leading to efficient quantum measurement processes with reduced depth and qubit overhead.

Abstract: Classical shadows extract quantum properties from reusable classical records, typically obtained through randomized measurement settings. Although individual settings may be shallow, switching among them introduces control, calibration, and reconfiguration costs beyond conventional metrics. Here we construct, for every $n$, a single fixed shallow quantum analyzer whose Born outcomes replace externally sampled settings as labels for reusable shadow snapshots. The analyzer combines a freshly prepared $n$-qubit fiducial register $A$ with parallel Bell readout of $A$ and the unknown system $S$, producing one $2n$-bit record per copy. The same circuit realizes a rank-one minimal informationally complete measurement: one fixed setting replaces the $3^n$ local-Pauli settings conventionally used for complete reconstruction while retaining the minimum $d^2$ outcomes with $d=2^n$. Its Pauli-diagonal frame admits an analytic inverse. For fixed Hermitian observables, the Haar-averaged conditional variance has a dimension-independent coefficient, whereas the state-uniform coefficient is dimension dependent. For $n\ge3$, worst-state Pauli variances remain bounded in the axial sector and scale as $Θ(d)$ in the mixed sector. Fiducial preparation before system contact uses $n-1$ arbitrary two-qubit gates, no work qubits beyond $A$, and logarithmic depth under all-to-all connectivity. The unknown system undergoes one parallel system-ancilla entangling layer followed by local Hadamards and readout. Measurement-assisted preparation achieves $O(1)$ adaptive quantum depth using $n+1$ extra qubits in the heralded route, or $O(n\log n)$ auxiliaries for deterministic completion. These tradeoffs show that part of the measurement-setting randomness and control complexity normally supplied shot by shot can instead be compiled into a fixed reusable analyzer.

</details>


### [219] [Capability-Gated Conformance Testing of Quantum Error-Correction Decoder Libraries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07035)
*Jiachen Shen,Hui Zhong*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper examines the effectiveness of quantum error correction decoders using various checks to evaluate their performance and conformance, revealing significant gaps in reliability among existing libraries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess what additional checks can ensure the reliability and correctness of quantum error correction decoders beyond traditional measurements.

Method: The study introduces conformance checks that evaluate decoder behavior in terms of syndrome explanations and correction weight comparisons under different representations, using configurations from multiple libraries.

Result: Results show a 0.0% direct yield for bounded-distance correctness despite hypotheses holding true in 62.1% of cases; various configurations exhibited substantial inconsistency and sensitivity to presentation.

Conclusion: This analysis highlights critical weaknesses in existing decoders and the importance of thorough verification processes, as established evaluations fail to identify certain corruption scenarios.

Abstract: A quantum error correction decoder is a library other people's results depend on, judged in one dominant way. Sample errors, decode, and count wrong logical observables. We ask what else can be checked there. Our conformance contract needs no oracle. One check asks that a returned correction explain the syndrome in the caller's index space. The other hands a decoder one instance under two presentations differing only in bookkeeping, where two feasible corrections of different weight prove the heavier is not minimum-weight. Verdicts are gated on what each library declares, so a firing contradicts a published guarantee. Nine configurations from five public libraries give three results. Documentation answers 4 of 54 capability questions. Bounded-distance correctness, the property callers most depend on, has a direct declaration yield of 0.0%, though its hypotheses hold in 62.1% of cases. Presentation sensitivity is real but shallow. One solver moved to a 26% heavier correction under a different numbering, which reaches the logical class at most once in twenty thousand shots. Established evaluation misses corruptions that preserve logical parity, while one summation over the caller's weights catches every one we injected. All 639 certificates ship as bundles a standalone verifier re-derives from first principles.

</details>


### [220] [2-Fold Forrelation is in QAC$^0$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07060)
*Francisca Vasconcelos*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This work demonstrates that 2-fold Forrelation with a specific promise gap can be efficiently solved using polynomial-size QAC$^0$ circuits, establishing a separation between QAC$^0$ and AC$^0$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The research aims to explore the computational capabilities of QAC$^0$ circuits in the context of promise problems like Forrelation, particularly in scenarios with a low promise gap.

Method: The authors utilize polynomial-size QAC$^0$ circuits designed to accept explicit input rather than relying on oracle-based methods, focusing on the 2-fold Forrelation problem.

Result: The study establishes that such circuits can solve 2-fold Forrelation with an inverse-polylogarithmic promise gap with bounded error, indicating their efficiency over AC$^0$ circuits under certain conditions.

Conclusion: This work reveals a clear computational boundary between QAC$^0$ and AC$^0$ at constant gap levels in the context of promise problems.

Abstract: We show that 2-fold Forrelation with inverse-polylogarithmic promise gap can be solved, with bounded error, by polynomial-size QAC$^0$ circuits. Unlike the standard oracle-based Forrelation algorithm, our circuits receive the input explicitly, in the same form as the AC$^0$ circuits against which Forrelation is known to be hard. At constant gap, this yields a natural promise-problem separation between QAC$^0$ and AC$^0$.

</details>


### [221] [Single-tone drive-enhanced CROT gate for bosonic quantum error correction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07076)
*Shushen Qin,Long D. H. My,Adrian Copetudo,Amon M. Kasper,Yvonne Y. Gao,Hui Khoon Ng*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper presents a novel implementation of the two-mode controlled-rotation (CROT) gate in a circuit-QED architecture, enhancing error correction for rotation-symmetric bosonic codes while retaining desirable properties without relying on specific code features.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Bosonic error correction is essential for robust quantum information processing, and effective implementation of the CROT gate is a key challenge due to the limitations of existing methods that often compromise gate properties.

Method: The authors introduce a direct method for implementing the CROT gate by driving a transmon coupled to two microwave cavities, enhancing effective nonlinearities to achieve the required two-mode interaction while using a single drive frequency.
