In this work, we use numerical emulations of a neutral-atom quantum processing unit to assess the feasibility of measuring the dynamical structure factor in a broader class of Ising-like spin systems. Beyond the standard one-dimensional transverse-field Ising chain, we benchmark the protocol in chains with dimerized interactions and in the two-dimensional transverse-field Ising model, where spectral properties are difficult to access with classical numerical methods at large scales. We further analyze the robustness of the protocol under realistic experimental conditions, including finite simulation times, pulse modulation, positional disorder, and laser noise. Our results show that neutral-atom quantum simulators can provide a practical route to probing dynamical response functions in regimes where classical simulations become increasingly demanding, paving the way toward experimental implementation.

</details>


### [254] [Analog-to-digital conversion in the quantum regime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07643)
*Thomas Pousset,Matteo Schiavon,Guillaume Ricard,Elie Awwad,Romain Alléaume,Nicolas Fabre*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents an analysis of digital signal processing in quantum optics, focusing on double homodyne detection and introducing a generalized dimensioning rule based on quantum fluctuations and electronic noise.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The study addresses the gap in understanding the digitization chain in double homodyne detection in quantum optics, which affects coherent detection and signal processing.

Method: The authors analyze the continuous mode description of double homodyne detection, considering various factors such as electronic noise, bandwidth, and sampling rate to establish a new dimensioning rule that extends the Nyquist-Shannon criterion.

Result: Numerically, it is shown that an optimal signal-to-noise ratio is achieved with a specific relationship between electronic bandwidth and sampling rate, which helps in reducing noise amplification.

Conclusion: This work provides a concrete design criterion for coherent detection in quantum optics, enhancing the understanding of signal processing implications across various stages.

Abstract: Digital signal processing has become essential in quantum optics, particularly for continuous-variable (CV) information encoding, enabling coherent detection schemes and mitigation of linear impairments. While linear signal processing in double homodyne detection has been studied under the assumption that all post-measurement processing can be backpropagated in the optical domain, a rigorous treatment of the digitization chain, spanning optical, electronic, and sampling stages, is still lacking. In this work, we consider the continuous mode description of double homodyne detection to fully capture the maximum mode-matching coefficient achievable as a function of electronic noise, signal bandwidth, receiver electronics bandwidth, and sampling rate, that is the entry point of digital signal processing. We establish a dimensioning rule that generalizes the Nyquist-Shannon criterion to account for quantum fluctuations of the measured signal, not merely its bandwidth. Analyzing the case of additive white electronic noise, we find numerically that optimal signal-to-noise ratio is achieved when the electronic bandwidth is large enough to pass the signal yet narrower than the sampling rate in order to mitigate noise amplification, yielding a concrete design criterion for coherent detection.

</details>


### [255] [Geometric inflation of deviations challenges neural quantum states in dynamics of quantum Ising models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07645)
*Wladislaw Krinitsin,Jonas B. Rigo,Mohammad Abedi,Markus Schmitt*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper investigates challenges in using neural quantum states (NQS) for simulating non-equilibrium dynamics in quantum systems, focusing on the one-dimensional quantum Ising model. It reveals that geometric factors, rather than parameter count, significantly affect simulation accuracy, suggesting improvements in TDVP algorithms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To tackle the challenges faced in accurately simulating non-equilibrium dynamics in strongly correlated quantum systems using neural quantum states (NQS).

Method: Analyzed the quench dynamics of a one-dimensional quantum Ising model and employed supervised state reconstruction to obtain tighter bounds on parameter counts. Identified issues related to the geometric dynamics of the quantum geometric tensor (QGT) affecting simulation accuracy.

Result: Determined that small deviations in parameters, through the rotation of the QGT's kernel, have significant effects on NQS dynamics, challenging the previously held assumptions about parameter representation limits.

Conclusion: Identified QGT-null-space rotation as a critical factor in the sensitivity of NQS dynamics, providing insights for enhancing time-dependent variational principle (TDVP) algorithms.

Abstract: Neural quantum states (NQS) have emerged as a powerful framework for simulating non-equilibrium dynamics in strongly correlated quantum systems, offering scalable variational representations of highly entangled states. Yet, accurate NQS simulations have been found to be surprisingly challenging in some physical regimes of limited complexity. Here, we address paradigmatic quench dynamics of a one-dimensional quantum Ising model as a controlled benchmark. Through supervised state reconstruction we establish substantially tighter empirical upper bounds on the required parameter count than previous estimates, ruling out representational limitations as the key obstruction. Instead, we uncover a geometric inflation of small deviations as a hitherto overlooked challenge for accurate solutions of the infinitesimal time-dependent variational principle (TDVP): the dynamical rotation of the kernel of the quantum geometric tensor (QGT) can suddenly lend physical significance to previously irrelevant parameter deviations. The stability of matrix product state solutions of the same TDVP suggests that the non-linearity of the neural network ansatz is the origin of the sensitivity. These results identify QGT-null-space rotation as a geometric diagnostic of sensitive NQS dynamics and as a concrete target for improving TDVP algorithms

</details>


### [256] [Microwave-Free $^{13}$C Hyperpolarization of Diamond Particles Enabled by Magic Angle Spinning and NV Centers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07653)
*Rémi Blinder,Guzel Musabirova,Daehee Kim,Anshuman Nayak,Jason Twamley,Viateschlav N. Agafonov,Raiker Witter,Jörg Matysik,Fedor Jelezko*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This study demonstrates efficient nuclear hyperpolarization of $^{13}{m C}$ in diamond particles using optically pumped NV centers instead of microwave-driven techniques, achieving significant polarization enhancements and steady-state levels at room temperature.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore alternative methods for nuclear hyperpolarization that circumvent the limitations of microwave-driven DNP, utilizing optically pumped NV centers in diamond as a medium for enhanced nuclear polarization.

Method: The method integrates optical illumination with magic angle spinning (MAS) under magnetic fields of 7.1 T and 9.4 T, avoiding the need for microwave irradiation. The polarization was measured while varying MAS frequency between 0 to 6 kHz.

Result: Maximal light-induced polarization enhancements of 280-fold for the 20 % isotopically enriched sample and 411-fold for natural abundance were achieved, reaching steady-state $^{13}{m C}$ polarization levels above 0.1 % under continuous illumination.

Conclusion: The findings represent significant progress toward leveraging diamond particles for nuclear polarization transfer to external nuclei, opening avenues for improved sensitivity and resolution in room temperature NMR applications.

Abstract: Nuclear hyperpolarization from optically pumped color centers in solids offers an alternative to conventional microwave-driven dynamic nuclear polarization (DNP). Diamond can host the nitrogen vacancy (NV) center, whose ground spin state can be readily polarized by light at room temperature, making diamond a candidate platform for nuclear hyperpolarization. We report $^{13}{\rm C}$ nuclear hyperpolarization in randomly oriented diamond particles with sizes ranging from 0.2 to 2 $μ$m, both at natural $^{13}{\rm C}$ abundance (1.1 %) and at 20 % isotopic enrichment, at magnetic fields of 7.1 T and 9.4 T. The protocol combines optical illumination with magic angle spinning (MAS) and does not require microwave irradiation. By investigating the nuclear polarization as a function of the MAS frequency between 0 and 6 kHz at the magnetic field of 7.1 T, we find maximum light-induced polarization enhancements of $280$-fold for the isotopically enriched sample and $411$-fold for the natural abundance sample. Under continuous illumination, steady-state absolute $^{13}{\rm C}$ polarization levels above 0.1 % are reached. A model involving optical pumping of NV centers and spin dynamics near level anticrossings (LACs) in three-spin clusters formed by NV, a substitutional nitrogen (P1) and $^{13}{\rm C}$ is used to describe these findings. The protocol strongly mitigates the effect of the anisotropy of the NV spin Hamiltonian, allowing more than $99.9\%$ of NV orientations to participate in the polarization transfer process. These results represent a first step toward transferring nuclear polarization from diamond particles to external nuclei, with potential applications in sensitive and high-resolution NMR at room temperature.

</details>


### [257] [Dynamical Crossover of the Quantum Fisher Information in the Spin-Boson Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07674)
*D. Parlato,G. Di Bello,F. Pavan,G. De Filippis,C. A. Perroni*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The study explores the quantum Fisher information in a two-level system with a bosonic environment, showing how coupling and temperature affect the estimation of the qubit gap, revealing distinct behaviors in Ohmic and non-Ohmic regimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how the environment influences the estimation of a qubit gap in a two-level system via quantum Fisher information.

Method: Combines analytical solutions with matrix-product-state simulations across different temperatures and coupling regimes to analyze quantum Fisher information.

Result: Demonstrates that long-term quantum Fisher information behaves differently in Ohmic vs non-Ohmic regimes and that thermal fluctuations alter long-time growth patterns, establishing a crossover behavior in certain conditions.

Conclusion: Establishes a link between the bath's low-frequency structure and quantum metrology, highlighting the effects of thermal fluctuations and energy relaxation on quantum Fisher information.

Abstract: We investigate the dynamical quantum Fisher information of a two-level system coupled to a bosonic environment, focusing on the estimation of the qubit gap. We combine analytical calculations with numerically controlled matrix-product-state simulations. In the exactly solvable pure-dephasing Ohmic regime at zero temperature, the long-time quantum Fisher information displays a coupling-dependent algebraic behavior, leading to a dynamical crossover: it grows without bound at weak coupling, approaches a finite asymptotic value at the crossover coupling, and vanishes at strong coupling. At finite temperature, thermal fluctuations suppress the long-time growth and generate a finite-time maximum, whose dependence on the dephasing coupling retains a clear signa- ture of the zero-temperature crossover. We show that this crossover is absent at zero temperature in non-Ohmic baths: the long-time quantum Fisher information vanishes in the sub-Ohmic and diverges in the super-Ohmic regimes. At non-zero temperature instead, the crossover appears for a super-Ohmic quadratic bath and the crossover coupling becomes temperature-dependent. Moreover, in the zero-temperature Ohmic regime, we introduce an additional amplitude-damping system-bath coupling that induces energy relaxation. This relaxation channel replaces the unbounded long-time growth with a finite asymptotic quantum Fisher information associated with the reduced interacting ground state, while a signature of the pure-dephasing crossover persists in the early-time dynamics. These results establish a direct connection between the low-frequency structure of the bath and the asymptotic metrological behavior of dynamical gap sensing, and show how thermal fluctuations and energy relaxation regularize the ideal pure-dephasing dynamical crossover of the quantum Fisher information.

</details>


### [258] [Subsystem self-correction of the GKP qubit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07702)
*Brian Chung Hang Cheung,Lasse Bjørn Kristensen,Frederik Nathan,Michael Kastoryano*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores the self-correcting properties of the Gottesman-Kitaev-Preskill (GKP) code, presenting a detailed derivation of its error-correction mechanisms and demonstrating its advantages over traditional spin-based methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The pursuit of passive quantum error correction is crucial for developing resilient quantum memories.

Method: The authors derive the self-correcting behavior of the GKP Hamiltonian using subsystem code decomposition and analyze the effects of coupling through physical quadratures, focusing on the detailed-balance jump operators and Gibbs weights.

Result: The GKP code exhibits an Arrhenius type scaling of logical lifetime, with boundary error suppression and an effective string tension in modular phase space, allowing for better logical sector stability.

Conclusion: The GKP code presents a promising avenue for self-correcting quantum memories, offering significant advantages over existing spin-based quantum memory approaches.

Abstract: Passive quantum error correction, also known as self-correction, is a holy grail in quantum information science. Recent theoretical advances suggest that the Gottesman-Kitaev-Preskill (GKP) code can exhibit self-correction properties, positioning it as a candidate for the realization of self-correcting quantum memories. In this article, we provide a self-contained derivation of the self-correcting behavior of the ideal GKP Hamiltonian, manifested in the Arrhenius type scaling of the logical lifetime, from a quantum-information perspective based on the subsystem code decomposition. When coupling through the physical quadrature $q$ and $p$, the detailed-balance jump operators decompose into a dominant part acting only on the gauge subsystem and a boundary term that acts non-trivially on the logical qubit. The boundary term is then exponentially suppressed by the Gibbs weights near the edge of the modular cells. In contrast to spin-based quantum memories such as the two-dimensional surface code, the GKP Hamiltonian realizes an effective string tension in modular phase space, whereby the energy cost increases as an error approaches the boundary of a logical sector.

</details>


### [259] [Single- and Two-Qubit Gates Driven by an Integrated Photonic Laser](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07708)
*Chris Caron,Zhenyu Wei,Andrei Isichenko,David Heim,Meiting Song,Nick Montifiore,Kaikai Liu,Josiah Dill,Daniel J. Blumenthal,Robert J. Niffenegger*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper demonstrates that integrated visible-wavelength Brillouin lasers can effectively drive coherent quantum operations in trapped ion quantum computing, achieving high fidelity in single- and two-qubit gates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the scalability and robustness challenges of trapped ion quantum computing by utilizing integrated laser technologies.

Method: An integrated Brillouin laser was used to drive coherent single- and two-qubit gates on $^{88}$Sr$^+$ optical qubits, allowing for measurement of fidelity and entanglement.

Result: Achieved single-qubit fidelity of 99.61% per Clifford gate and two-qubit entanglement fidelity of 92.35%, with significant coherence times.

Conclusion: Integrated visible-wavelength narrow-linewidth lasers meet the performance requirements for scalable trapped ion quantum logic, enabling future integration with quantum processors.

Abstract: Trapped ions are a leading technology for quantum computing, but their reliance on bespoke tabletop laser and optical systems remains a major obstacle to scaling and robustness. Integrated silicon nitride lasers, compatible with future monolithic integration with surface electrode ion traps, have recently demonstrated frequency-selective qubit state preparation and measurement as well as interrogation of an optical clock transition. However, coherent qubit gates driven by integrated laser sources have not yet been demonstrated because coherent quantum logic imposes substantially more stringent performance requirements on the laser. Here, we use a visible-wavelength integrated Brillouin laser stabilized to an integrated coil resonator to drive coherent single- and two-qubit gates with $^{88}$Sr$^+$ optical qubits. We measure an average single-qubit fidelity of 99.61% $\pm$ 0.03% per Clifford gate with randomized benchmarking and use a two-qubit Mølmer-Sørensen interaction to generate an entangled Bell state with a fidelity of 92.35% $\pm$ 1.50%. The qubit exhibits a bare Ramsey coherence time of 660 $\pm$ 9 $μ$s, extended to 1.750 $\pm$ 0.033 ms by spin echo. These results demonstrate that integrated visible-wavelength narrow-linewidth photonic lasers can meet the phase-noise requirements for coherent trapped ion quantum logic, providing a path for scalable optical systems integrated within trapped ion quantum processors.

</details>


### [260] [Propaq: A Python package for Heisenberg Propagation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07730)
*Hrishikesh Belagali,Ryan LaRose*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: Propaq is a Python package for efficient simulation of quantum circuits using Heisenberg propagation techniques, offering compatibility with popular quantum software and various advanced features.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a performant and flexible tool for simulating quantum circuits using Heisenberg propagation, addressing the limitations of existing packages.

Method: Propaq implements Heisenberg propagation in any basis, supports hybrid Schrödinger-Heisenberg simulation, and allows for custom truncation and noise handling, with extensive logging capabilities.

Result: Propaq is compatible with major quantum platforms like Qiskit and Cirq, and matches or outperforms leading packages in performance for Heisenberg propagation.

Conclusion: The package is a valuable resource for researchers in quantum simulations, offering advanced features and ease of integration with existing software.

Abstract: We introduce Propaq, a performant and flexible Python package implementing Heisenberg propagation techniques for classically simulating quantum circuits. Propaq is compatible with major quantum software packages like Qiskit and Cirq, and matches or outperforms similar state-of-the-art packages for Heisenberg propagation. Beyond this, Propaq has multiple distinguishing features for simulation and research. These include Heisenberg propagation in any basis, hybrid Schrödinger-Heisenberg simulation, custom truncation and noisy simulation methods, and detailed logging information for analyzing simulation accuracy, runtime, and memory usage. We describe these features, how to use Propaq for research, and its backend implementation.

</details>


### [261] [QROB: Quantifying Realization Overhead in Quantum Compilation via Reverse Construction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07776)
*Jintao Li,Kaiqi Li,Rui Wang,Yilun Zhao,Kaixuan Huang,Ying Wang,Jialin Zhang,Zheng-An Wang,Xiaoming Sun,Heng Fan*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: QROB is a scalable methodology for quantum compilation, providing references that enhance the efficiency of various quantum scheduling tasks, showing marked improvement over existing tools.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The need for a robust evaluation framework in quantum compilation that aligns idealized interaction with hardware constraints while identifying realization overheads.

Method: QROB generates compilation instances backwards from realizable configurations, facilitating compiler-independent references for various quantum scheduling tasks.

Result: QROB highlighted significant gaps in existing compilers, showing greater cost efficiency, improved router performance, and better physical fidelity in hardware.

Conclusion: By addressing compilation gaps, QROB contributes to enhanced realization efficiency in quantum circuits, indicating its potential as a standard in quantum compilation methodologies.

Abstract: Quantum compilation reconciles a program's idealized interaction topology with hardware locality constraints, yet evaluations at scale lack calibrated references for realization overhead. We present QROB, a scalable reverse-construction methodology that generates compilation instances backward from directly realizable configurations, retaining the inverse paths as feasible, compiler-independent references. QROB provides a common evaluation substrate for NISQ SWAP routing and fault-tolerant lattice-surgery scheduling, while extending its reference-preserving principle to capacity-constrained quantum memory-access scheduling.
  Across systems ranging from 9 to 156 qubits, evaluations highlight QROB's utility as both a diagnostic benchmark and a data source. First, for compiler characterization, QROB reveals substantial realization gaps in existing tools, with NISQ compilers incurring up to 24.1x the reference SWAP cost and fault-tolerant compilers requiring up to 7.0x the reference makespan. Second, as a supervision source for data-driven compilation, a router trained on QROB references outperforms Qiskit SABRE on 84.8% of real-world application circuits. Finally, on real hardware, QROB reference realizations achieve a median mirror-circuit survival rate 1.65x that of full Qiskit O3 compilations across three 156-qubit IBM Heron-r2 processors, demonstrating that closing algorithmic compilation gaps translates directly into physical fidelity gains.

</details>


### [262] [Realization of decoherence-induced averaged symmetry-protected topological phases on quantum processors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07831)
*Ruizhe Shen,Xue-Jia Yu,Ching Hua Lee*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper explores decoherence-induced averaged symmetry-protected topological (ASPT) order in mixed states using programmable quantum processors, demonstrating the feasibility of manipulating topological quantum matter under decoherence.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate how symmetry-protected topological phases can emerge in open quantum systems where decoherence leads to mixed states, rather than pure states.

Method: The authors implement engineered sublattice-selective Pauli-$Z$ dephasing via ancilla-assisted quantum circuits in a one-dimensional cluster SPT setup, measuring observable changes in symmetry and entanglement spectra.

Result: They observe a decay in symmetry charge, while the non-trivial nature of the ASPT structure is confirmed through two-replica Renyi-2 string correlators, indicating that the topological order survives mixed-state conditions.

Conclusion: The findings suggest that structured decoherence can be engineered as a resource for exploring and realizing mixed-state topological quantum matter, paving the way for future studies in quantum computing and condensed matter physics.

Abstract: Symmetry-protected topological (SPT) phases are conventionally formulated for pure states protected by exact symmetries. In open quantum systems, however, decoherence generates mixed-state ensembles in which average symmetry can instead emerge only after averaging over microscopic trajectories. In this work, we realize decoherence-induced averaged SPT (ASPT) order on programmable quantum processors. Starting from a one-dimensional cluster SPT, we engineer sublattice-selective Pauli-$Z$ dephasing through ancilla-assisted quantum circuits. We observe the resulting symmetry conversion through the decay of the symmetry charge. Moreover, a two-replica Renyi-2 string correlator measured through destructive SWAP readout remains nontrivial, revealing the ASPT structure encoded at the density-matrix level. We further show that the engineered dephasing redistributes spectral weight among reduced-stabilizer sectors while preserving the characteristic twofold pairing of the half-chain entanglement spectrum. These results establish a gate-based route to engineering and probing ASPT order and demonstrate structured decoherence as a programmable resource for realizing mixed-state topological quantum matter.

</details>


### [263] [Restricted typicality in non-equilibrium quantum many-body systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07832)
*Konrad Pawlik,Piotr Sierant,Jakub Zakrzewski*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper addresses the challenge of characterizing quantum many-body systems, revealing how to recover nonlinear properties of quantum states through a maximum-entropy approach, demonstrating global restricted typicality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The need to accurately characterize the complex behaviors of quantum many-body systems without exponentially scaling computational demands.

Method: Utilizing the maximum-entropy Scrooge ensemble based on slow modes to recover nonlinear characteristics of the quantum state for systems with timescale separation.

Result: Successful reconstruction of complex properties like half-chain entanglement entropy and participation entropy, demonstrating a typical pure state behavior in dynamically restricted submanifolds of Hilbert space.

Conclusion: The concept of global restricted typicality enhances understanding of quantum states in hydrodynamically constrained systems.

Abstract: Characterizing the time evolution of generic quantum many-body systems is a fundamental challenge, as representing the exact state requires exponentially scaling computational resources. While hydrodynamics and statistical mechanics successfully simplify this task by predicting the expectation values of local observables, these macroscopic frameworks provide no information about nonlinear characteristics of the quantum state. In this work, we demonstrate that for systems exhibiting a timescale separation, with dynamics governed by the transport of conserved charges, this lost information can be systematically recovered. By constraining the maximum-entropy Scrooge ensemble solely by the system's slow modes, we accurately reconstruct complex, nonlinear quantum properties of the global time-evolved state, including the half-chain entanglement entropy and the participation entropy in the computational basis. Our results generalize the paradigm of canonical typicality, revealing that a hydrodynamically bottlenecked system behaves as a typical pure state within a dynamically restricted submanifold of the Hilbert space - a phenomenon we term global restricted typicality.

</details>


### [264] [Snakes on a Hyper-Plane: Dynamical and Ontological Locality in Everettian Quantum Mechanics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07860)
*Travis Norsen*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper argues that while Everettian Quantum Mechanics (EQM) can be locally dynamical within the Spacetime State Realism framework, its inclusion of non-local beables challenges the theory's compatibility with relativistic causality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To examine the apparently emerging consensus on EQM's compatibility with relativity, particularly regarding dynamical locality and non-locality.

Method: Analysis of the implications of non-local beables within the framework of SSR and how they affect local interventions and causality.

Result: The introduction of non-local beables complicates the existing understanding of dynamical locality and raises doubts about the compatibility of SSR EQM with relativistic causality.

Conclusion: SSR EQM may need to be reconsidered concerning its alignment with relativistic causality due to the complications introduced by non-local beables.

Abstract: An apparently emerging consensus holds that Everettian Quantum Mechanics (EQM), in the Spacetime State Realism (SSR) version of Wallace and Timpson, involves ontological non-locality (i.e., non-separability) but is fully compatible with the dynamical notion of locality usually taken to be required by relativity. The claim of dynamical locality is based on the fact that the local beables postulated by the theory (namely, Reduced Density Matrices, RDMs, associated with localized regions) are not affected by interventions outside their past light cones. However, the theory's non-separability means that it posits non-local beables (namely RDMs for non-localized regions) in addition to the local ones. We argue here that the involvement of non-local beables introduces complications for extant formulations of dynamical locality and at least suggests, contrary to the emerging consensus, that SSR EQM should perhaps not be considered compatible with relativistic causality after all.

</details>


### [265] [Quantifying Margenau--Hill Nonclassicality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07869)
*Sudip Chakrabarty*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This study develops a moment-based framework for quantifying nonclassicality in quantum systems through the Margenau--Hill quasiprobability distribution, introducing the logarithmic MHQ negativity and deriving lower bounds based on moments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify nonclassicality in quantum systems without reconstructing the full quasiprobability distribution.

Method: Introduces logarithmic MHQ negativity and derives lower bounds using low-order moments, particularly focusing on the fourth-moment bound.

Result: Derives a tight upper bound for MHQ negativity for qubits and shows relevant moments can be estimated via multicopy representations.

Conclusion: The developed framework enables the extraction of MHQ negativity from limited observational data using various estimation techniques.

Abstract: Quasiprobability distributions offer a useful way of describing nonclassical features of quantum systems through their departure from classical probability theory. In this work, we investigate the quantification of nonclassicality associated with the Margenau--Hill quasiprobability (MHQ) distribution using its moments, without requiring reconstruction of the full distribution. First we introduce the logarithmic MHQ negativity as a quantifier of nonclassicality, and then derive a hierarchy of rigorous lower bounds in terms of low-order moments. Within the resulting hierarchy, the fourth-moment bound is the strongest among the bounds based on even moments. For qubits, we further derive a tight upper bound on the exact MHQ negativity and identify the corresponding extremal state. We also obtain an optimal positivity threshold based on the fourth moment for arbitrary pairs of qubit observables. Finally, we show that the relevant moments admit exact multicopy representations, providing a route to their estimation using interferometry or classical shadow techniques. Our results establish a moment-based framework for extracting quantitative information about MHQ negativity from a finite set of low-order observables.

</details>


### [266] [Measurement-induced remote activation of nonclassicality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07875)
*Sudip Chakrabarty*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates that local measurements by a correlated partner can activate nonclassical states in a classical party, focusing on two-qubit systems and the role of local noise.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is to explore how nonclassical states can be activated remotely to leverage quantum advantages, particularly through measurement-induced steering.

Method: The researchers analyze nonclassicality via quasiprobability distributions, employing closed forms for measurements on two-qubit systems and investigating the effects of local noise in detail.

Result: The study finds that local noise can enhance nonclassical effects under specific conditions, while providing closed-form expressions for the maximal activation effect and proving optimal measurement strategies.

Conclusion: The findings establish measurement-induced steering as a valuable operational resource for activating nonclassical states, with implications for quantum technologies.

Abstract: Nonclassicality, defined through nonpositivity of quasiprobability distributions such as the Wigner and Kirkwood-Dirac distributions, underlies key quantum advantages. We show that it can be remotely activated: local measurements by a correlated partner alone can drive a party whose reduced state is classical into a genuinely nonclassical conditional state. For two-qubit systems we obtain closed forms for the maximal single-branch effect and for its probability-weighted average under the best two-outcome projective measurement, the latter proven optimal over all measurements when the shared state carries no local bias. Local noise on the steered party, evaluated relative to its own noise-updated reference basis, can enhance the effect when non-unital and misaligned with that party's axis, whereas no unital channel ever helps. This nonclassicality is shown to unlock, for the same reference observable, anomalous weak values unavailable to the unsteered state under arbitrary postselection. An exactly solvable hybrid qubit-oscillator model shows the same mechanism generates Wigner negativity. These results identify measurement-induced steering as an operational resource for remotely activating nonclassicality.

</details>


### [267] [Riemannian optimization for linear optical problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07890)
*Pablo V. Parellada*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a closed analytical formula for the Riemannian gradient of functions related to multiport interferometer configurations, enhancing optimization processes in linear optics for quantum science.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The need to optimize multiport interferometers for applications in quantum science, particularly in state preparation with enhanced fidelity and success probability, drives this research.

Method: Deriving a closed analytical formula for the Riemannian gradient and employing Riemannian optimization algorithms like gradient descent and BFGS for optimizing interferometer setups.

Result: The proposed optimizer improves success probabilities for linear optical state preparations and demonstrates significant speed advantages compared to existing methods.

Conclusion: The findings enable more efficient solutions for complex linear optical problems, promising advancements in quantum applications with increased modes and photons.

Abstract: Many applications of linear optics in quantum science involve searching for some optimal configuration of a multiport interferometer. For example, to find heralded state preparations, one can optimize the interferometer to maximize the fidelity and success probability of the state preparation. In this paper, we derive a closed analytical formula for the Riemannian gradient of any differentiable function defined over the interferometer unitaries. We use this gradient in Riemannian optimization algorithms, such as gradient descent or BFGS, to find the optimal setup of the interferometer. As an interesting use case, we search for linear optical state preparations, improving some of the success probabilities reported in the literature for NOON or photon catalysis preparations. Finally, we show that our optimizer is orders of magnitude faster than other methods in the literature, opening the door for solving linear optical problems involving more modes and photons.

</details>


### [268] [Few-Body Decay Dynamics in Colloidal CsPbI3 Quantum-Dot Clusters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07892)
*Emma Daggett,Christian M. Lange,Nicholas Favate,Ankit Kundu,Ishita Agarwal,Arya D. Keni,Adhyyan S. Mansukhani,Christina W. Li,Libai Huang,Jonathan D. Hood*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This study investigates the radiative dynamics of CsPbI3 perovskite quantum dot clusters, revealing emitter-number-dependent decay behaviors and establishing a platform for exploring collective optical phenomena.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the radiative dynamics in perovskite quantum dots and their clusters, transitioning from single emitters to collective behavior.

Method: Characterization of CsPbI3 quantum dots through photon autocorrelation, blinking statistics, and emission brightness, analyzing clusters up to ten dots.

Result: Clusters show biexponential decay with two lifetimes; short lifetime decreases with more emitters, and long-lived component persists, indicating complex interactions.

Conclusion: Self-assembled perovskite quantum dot clusters serve as a valuable system for studying the emergence of collective optical behavior.

Abstract: Colloidal perovskite quantum dots combine bright emission with the ability to self- assemble into closely spaced structures, enabling radiative dynamics to be studied from isolated emitters to few-dot clusters. Here, we characterize CsPbI3 perovskite quan- tum dots from isolated emitters to self-assembled clusters containing up to ten dots. We estimate the number of emitters in each cluster using a combination of photon autocorrelation, blinking statistics, and emission brightness. Compared with isolated dots, clusters containing two or more emitters exhibit biexponential decay, with a short lifetime that decreases with emitter number and an additional long-lived component. Photon-correlation Fourier spectroscopy shows that the single-dot emission remains far from the lifetime-limited regime. Nevertheless, we observe emitter-number-dependent decay dynamics even in this low-coherence regime. Together, these measurements es- tablish self-assembled perovskite quantum-dot clusters as a platform for studying how collective optical behavior emerges between the single-emitter and ensemble limits

</details>


### [269] [A Novel Steganography Scheme Using Quantum Hilbert Transform](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07894)
*Nitin Jha,Abhishek Parakh*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a novel quantum steganography scheme using a Quantum Hilbert Transform for embedding hidden messages in quantum systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the application of phase-domain information hiding in quantum systems, which has been under-researched.

Method: A finite-dimensional Quantum Hilbert Transform is introduced as a unitary phase operator, enabling the embedding of classical bits in quantum states as phase perturbations. Recovery of the hidden message is achieved using binary state discrimination and classical error correction.

Result: A QHT-based quantum steganography scheme is proposed that successfully embeds and recovers hidden messages using advanced quantum operations.

Conclusion: The proposed scheme demonstrates the feasibility of phase-domain steganography in quantum systems, opening new avenues for secure communication.

Abstract: The main goal of steganography is to transmit hidden messages in legitimate-looking communication messages. Phase-domain information hiding, however, has not been fully explored for quantum systems. This work introduces a finite-dimensional Quantum Hilbert Transform (QHT) as a unitary phase operator based on the Quantum Fourier Transform. Using this construction, we develop a QHT-based quantum steganography scheme that embeds classical bits as weak signed phase perturbations of quantum cover states. Bob recovers the hidden message through binary state discrimination, block aggregation, and classical error-correcting decoding.

</details>


### [270] [Quantum Wavefunction Augmentation via Variational Autoencoders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07972)
*Sonaldeep Halder,Chayan Patra,Rahul Maitra*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces Q-WAVE, a hybrid method for quantum diagonalization that enhances accuracy in strongly correlated regimes using variational autoencoders and sampled determinants.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The accuracy of sample-based quantum diagonalization (SQD) diminishes in strongly correlated regimes, motivating the search for methods that can handle larger determinant spaces effectively.

Method: Q-WAVE combines determinants from SqDRIFT Krylov circuits and configuration interaction singles and doubles with a variational autoencoder to iteratively expand the basis of sampled determinants towards the variational ground state.

Result: Q-WAVE achieves sub-millihartree accuracy in quantum simulations for water and nitrogen dimer molecules, and demonstrates scalability on larger systems like a 52-qubit ethylene and a 60-qubit chromium dimer system.

Conclusion: Q-WAVE significantly enhances the accuracy of quantum diagonalization methods in strongly correlated regimes and proves effective across various molecular systems.

Abstract: Sample-based quantum diagonalization (SQD) has emerged as a promising route for quantum-centric supercomputing, relying on classical diagonalization of the molecular Hamiltonian within a hardware-sampled determinant subspace. However, its accuracy degrades in strongly correlated regimes where the relevant determinant space exceeds what finite-shot sampling can capture. In this work, we introduce Quantum Wavefunction Augmentation via Variational Autoencoders (Q-WAVE), a hybrid method that combines determinants sampled via SqDRIFT Krylov circuits and configuration interaction singles and doubles (CISD) determinants with generative machine learning. Using a custom $β$-annealed variational autoencoder (VAE) model, Q-WAVE iteratively expands this basis toward the variational ground state. The VAE learns the wavefunction's primary support structure from the combined hardware and CISD seed in a continuous latent space, generating new dominant determinants beyond any fixed excitation hierarchy. The resulting compact wavefunction exceeds what can be extracted from raw hardware samples alone. We demonstrate sub-millihartree accuracy compared to full configuration interaction for $\text{H}_2\text{O}$ and $\text{N}_2$ dissociation. Finally, we establish Q-WAVE's scalability on a 52-qubit ethylene system (achieving sub-millihartree accuracy versus CCSD(T)) and a highly correlated 60-qubit $\text{Cr}_2$ stress test that attains chemical accuracy upon a final perturbative correction.

</details>


### [271] [Learned Diffractive Optics for Quantum-Optimal Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08014)
*Matthew J. Filipovich,Alexander Duplinskii,A. I. Lvovsky*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper explores how learned diffractive optics can optimize measurements for visual sensing to achieve quantum limits on photon-limited sensing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enhance visual sensing capabilities under quantum mechanics constraints, especially for complex light fields from distant objects.

Method: Utilizes learned diffractive optics in state discrimination and parameter estimation of coherent and incoherent light fields with limited photons, optimizing for each task's performance metric.

Result: The diffractive optical neural networks significantly exceed standard measurement methods and approach quantum limits for given photon budgets and in the asymptotic case.

Conclusion: The study demonstrates that optimized optical measurements can effectively leverage quantum mechanics for enhanced sensing in visual applications.

Abstract: Quantum mechanics sets the ultimate bounds on photon-limited sensing, yet practical measurements attaining these bounds are known only in special cases. This is particularly the case for visual sensing problems, where the goal is to infer features of a distant object based on the spatial structure of the light field it emits or reflects. Because of the potentially complex structure of such objects and fields, constructing optimal measurements on them is a challenging task. Here, we apply learned diffractive optics to state discrimination and parameter estimation of coherent and diffraction-limited incoherent light fields under a restricted photon budget. Optimized directly on each task's figure of merit, without prior knowledge of the optimal measurement, the physically realizable diffractive optical neural networks substantially outperform standard measurements and approach the quantum limits for a given number of photons as well as in the asymptotic limit.

</details>


### [272] [Sklar's Theorem and Quantum State Reconstruction from All-Context Dependence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08035)
*Yi-Yu Lin*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper applies Sklar's theorem to Born statistics from local quantum measurements, revealing how dependence structures inform non-product two-qubit states.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how dependence structures in statistics relate to quantum states and their measurements.

Method: Utilizing Sklar's theorem, the authors examine local binary measurement contexts and their impact on determining two-qubit states, addressing ambiguities through scalar optimization and correlations.

Result: The dependence nuclei clarify state determination up to a discrete ambiguity, aligning with the concept of quantum discord.

Conclusion: The findings bridge statistical frameworks with quantum mechanics, highlighting the role of measurement contexts in understanding quantum states.

Abstract: Sklar's theorem separates the marginals of a joint distribution from its dependence structure. We apply this viewpoint to Born statistics generated by local quantum measurements. For non-product two-qubit states, the dependence nuclei over all local binary projective measurement contexts determine the state up to at most a discrete double-spin-flip ambiguity. The corresponding scalar optimization, combined with the quantum total correlation, reproduces global quantum discord.

</details>


### [273] [Quantum magnonics with engineered dissipative and coherent interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08050)
*Debsuvra Mukhopadhyay,Jayakrishnan M. P. Nair,Girish S. Agarwal*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This Perspective highlights the advance of cavity magnonics from traditional spin-photon interactions to advanced applications in quantum state engineering, emphasizing dissipative dynamics, Floquet engineering, and their implications for devices.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper aims to explore how advancements in cavity magnonics can enable new technologies and applications by controlling interactions and dynamics at the quantum level.

Method: The authors review the recent developments in cavity magnonics, focusing on concepts like dissipative coupling, Floquet dynamics, and the creation of novel quantum states.

Result: The paper indicates that new techniques allow for long-lived collective modes, enhanced spectral responses, and novel magnon states, including squeezed and entangled states.

Conclusion: The findings suggest that cavity magnonics has evolved into a significant platform for various applications in sensing, spin transport, and hybrid quantum technologies, surpassing traditional polariton physics.

Abstract: Cavity magnonics is moving beyond the conventional paradigm of coherent spin--photon hybridization toward the controlled engineering of dissipative and coherent interactions, Floquet dynamics, and quantum fluctuations. This Perspective examines how these ingredients reshape collective dynamics and open new avenues for quantum state engineering, transport, and sensing. We discuss the role of dissipative coupling and anti-$\mathcal{PT}$ symmetry in enabling long-lived collective modes and enhanced spectral response, and highlight Floquet engineering as a means of engineering nonreciprocal transport and phase-controlled interactions. We then review recent progress in quantum magnonics, ranging from squeezed and entangled magnon states, including the squeezing of thermal magnons, to prospective schemes for preparing magnonic Schrödinger-cat states. We also discuss how parametric driving and active gain strongly modify hybrid susceptibilities and amplify magnon--photon response. These advances expand the scope of cavity magnonics beyond conventional polariton physics, establishing it as a versatile platform for sensing, transduction, spin transport, and hybrid quantum technologies.

</details>


### [274] [From Bits to Qubits: The Theory and Practice of Quantum Data Encoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08058)
*Xiao-Ming Zhang,Arthur G. Rattew,Bujiao Wu,Georgios Styliaris,Xiaoming Sun,Bálint Koczor,Xiao Yuan*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The review analyzes quantum data encoding, covering concepts, algorithms, and practical aspects, while emphasizing its importance in achieving quantum advantage.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the critical bottleneck in realizing practical quantum advantages through efficient encoding of classical data into quantum systems.

Method: The review traces historical developments and surveys various encoding models (quantum state preparation, unitary synthesis, etc.) with a focus on their circuit complexities and practical implications.

Result: It identifies key tradeoffs in circuit size, depth, and resources, stressing the effectiveness of structured data representation in quantum algorithms.

Conclusion: The review serves as a resource for researchers and newcomers, highlighting the importance of quantum data encoding for future quantum computing advancements.

Abstract: Encoding classical data into quantum systems is a foundational step in the execution of nearly all quantum algorithms, and a critical bottleneck in realizing practical quantum advantage. This review provides a comprehensive account of the concepts, algorithms, and practical considerations associated with quantum data encoding. We trace the development from its early conceptual foundations to recent advances, considering commonly used access models, such as quantum state preparation, unitary synthesis, QRAM and block encoding. We survey the circuit size, depth, space-time tradeoffs, as well as non-Clifford resources required for fault-tolerant implementation. We also discuss the roles of different access models in quantum algorithms. Special attention is given to structured data, such as sparse data, Boolean functions and data represented by tensor networks. This review bridges theory and applications, serving both as a pedagogical guide for newcomers and as a reference for active researchers. We also highlight the pivotal role of quantum data encoding in quantum computing and provide insights into future directions that will enable quantum advantage.

</details>


### [275] [Bottom-Up Design of Quantum Optical Experiments Using Discrete Generative Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08073)
*Isaac L. Huidobro-Meezs,Simón Paiva-Ortega,Rodrigo A. Vargas-Hernández*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper introduces Grinch, a generative framework for designing quantum optical circuits by learning from fidelity-based rewards without pre-existing datasets, successfully generating high-fidelity graphs for various quantum states and gates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The need for efficient design of quantum optical experiments through optimization of circuit topologies and parameters.

Method: Grinch utilizes a bottom-up, reward-driven approach to directly sample optical graphs based on rewards related to fidelity of quantum states, avoiding reliance on existing datasets.

Result: Successfully generated multiple high-fidelity optical graphs for multipartite states, cluster states, and nonlocal gates, identifying solutions for states that cannot be perfectly realized and addressing hardware constraints.

Conclusion: The findings demonstrate a novel reward-driven method for quantum optical inverse design, enabling exploration of varied topologies while considering connectivity limitations.

Abstract: Designing quantum optical experiments requires searching over discrete circuit topologies and continuous parameters, often with multiple realizations of the same target state. Graph-based methods commonly address this problem by optimizing a dense graph and pruning it toward a single circuit. We introduce \texttt{Grinch}, a bottom-up reward-driven generative framework that learns to sample optical graphs directly from fidelity-based rewards without relying on a pre-existing training dataset. We demonstrate the framework on multipartite entangled states, graph and cluster states, and nonlocal photonic-gate targets, obtaining multiple high-fidelity optical graphs. We identify asymptotic solutions for states that cannot be generated exactly by graphs, as well as nonlocal Toffoli gate constructions requiring two ancillas. Our work presents hardware-constraint objectives, which lead to alternative constructions of CNOT and Toffoli gates when connections between ancilla nodes are restricted. These findings illustrate a reward-driven approach to quantum optical inverse design, exploring alternative circuit topologies while directly incorporating connectivity constraints into the search process.

</details>


### [276] [Spectral Sign Implication for Quantum Logic](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08099)
*Kenji Tokuo*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper explores spectral sign implication in quantum logic via operator theory, connecting it to classical material implication and quantum discrimination methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the relationship between quantum logic and classical implications through spectral properties of operators derived from projections.

Method: Analyzes the difference between consequent and antecedent projections to define spectral sign implication, ensuring it meets specific logical conditions and compares to standard implications.

Result: The spectral sign implication adheres to relevant implicative conditions, uniquely characterized within specific Borel constructions, and shows alignment with optimal projective tests in quantum discrimination.

Conclusion: The findings integrate quantum implication concepts with operator theory, expanding the understanding of binary quantum discrimination and logical frameworks.

Abstract: We study the spectral sign implication for quantum logic, defined by the nonnegative spectral projection of the operator obtained by subtracting the antecedent projection from the consequent projection. The construction agrees with classical material implication on commuting projections and compares arbitrary pairs of projections through the spectral structure of their difference. It satisfies Hardegree's four minimal implicative conditions, his law of contraposition, and a falsity condition. It differs from the standard polynomial implications in that its value need not belong to the ortholattice generated by its arguments. Within a uniform class of Borel constructions for pairs of projections, the operations satisfying entailment, contraposition, and the falsity condition correspond exactly to measurable choices of spectral branch. In the continuous subclass, these three conditions determine the spectral sign implication uniquely. In finite dimensions, the operation is also the largest among the acceptance projections of optimal projective tests for Helstrom discrimination between subspace states with priors proportional to rank. These results connect quantum implication with the operator theory of two projections and binary quantum discrimination.

</details>


### [277] [Quantum algorithm for PageRank computation through multistep quantum resonant transitions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08140)
*Chuqing Wang,Hefeng Wang,Hua Xiang*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a quantum algorithm that efficiently computes the PageRank vector using multistep quantum resonant transition (mQRT).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To leverage quantum computing for efficiently obtaining the PageRank vector of large-scale networks, improving upon classical methods.

Method: The algorithm encodes the PageRank vector in the ground state of a Hamiltonian related to the Google matrix, employing a series of Hamiltonians derived from subgraphs to transition to the problem Hamiltonian.

Result: The algorithm requires only one ancillary qubit and has a runtime proportional to the number of steps taken through intermediate Hamiltonians to achieve the ground state of the problem Hamiltonian.

Conclusion: This approach provides an efficient quantum solution for calculating PageRank in large networks, potentially enhancing performance compared to classical algorithms.

Abstract: We present a quantum algorithm for obtaining a quantum state that encodes the PageRank vector of the Google matrix through multistep quantum resonant transition (mQRT). In the algorithm, the PageRank vector is encoded in the ground state of a problem Hamiltonian associated with the Google matrix. By dividing the web graph corresponding to the Google matrix into a series of subgraphs with nested structure, we construct a sequence of Hamiltonians based on the subgraphs to form a Hamiltonian evolution path from a simple initial Hamiltonian to the problem Hamiltonian. The ground state of the problem Hamiltonian is obtained by going through ground states of the intermediate Hamiltonians via QRT step by step. This algorithm requires only one ancillary qubit, and the runtime of the algorithm is proportional to the number of steps. It provides a new way for efficiently obtaining the quantum state of the PageRank vector of large-scale networks.

</details>


### [278] [Quantum-state texture measure and texture transformation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08163)
*Yufan Lin,Yu Guo,Fei He,Shuanping Du*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends the theory of quantum-state texture by examining state convertibility under texture-free operations, solving transformation problems for qubits, proposing new texture measures, and providing a resource-theoretic framework.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enhance understanding and characterization of quantum states, particularly in relation to gate characterization in quantum circuits.

Method: Investigation of state convertibility under texture-free operations, solving deterministic transformation problems for qubits, and proposing new texture measures.

Result: Introduced new classes of texture measures and established a comprehensive resource-theoretic framework for quantum-state texture.

Conclusion: The developed framework and measures provide deeper insights into state irregularities and transformations in quantum mechanics.

Abstract: Quantum-state texture quantifies structural irregularities of a state in a selected basis and has emerged as a valuable resource for gate characterization in universal circuits. Consequently, a class of contractive distance based texture measures and a framework for constructing convex-roof extended texture measure have been proposed. Here, we extend this theory in several directions. We examine the convertibility of a pure state to any state under texture-free operations, and fully solve the deterministic transformation problem between any two states in the qubit case. We then propose two classes of texture measures: convex-function-based measures and texture cost defined via minimal cost of texture contained in pure states. Moreover, we show that every texture monotone gives rise to a non-negative function which admits the same properties as the function used in the convex-roof extension---thus providing a converse to that construction. Our work thereby offers a relatively comprehensive resource-theoretic formulation of quantum-state texture.

</details>


### [279] [Saving resources through repeat-until-success positive-operator-valued-measure measurements in quantum computation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08168)
*Hefeng Wang,Sixia Yu,Hua Xiang*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper introduces a quantum computation technique using POVM measurements during computation steps, involving an ancillary qubit to enhance efficiency by reducing qubit and unitary operation requirements.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve quantum computation efficiency by integrating measurements during the computation process rather than deferring them until the end.

Method: Coupling an ancillary qubit with working qubits and applying a unitary operation followed by a projective measurement on the ancillary qubit to implement POVM measurements in a repeat-until-success manner.

Result: The proposed approach enables a polynomial reduction in the number of qubits and unitary operations needed compared to methods deferring measurements to the end.

Conclusion: Introducing intermediate measurements enhances efficiency and simplifies quantum computations, with practical implementation strategies proposed.

Abstract: We present a quantum computation approach in which computation is guided by positive-operator-valuedmeasure (POVM) measurements following a given computation path in multisteps. In this approach, one ancillary qubit is coupled to a register of working qubits, and a POVM measurement is implemented effectively on the working qubits by applying a unitary operation on the whole system followed by a projective measurement performed on the ancillary qubit. Each step of the computation is a repeat-until-success procedure such that the desired state of the step is obtained deterministically on the working qubits via POVM measurements. The principle of deferred measurement states that measurements can always be moved from an intermediate stage of a quantum circuit to the end of the circuit without affecting the efficiency of the computation.We demonstrate that in our approach, by introducing intermediate measurements on the ancillary qubit in the computation process, both the number of qubits and unitary operations can be reduced polynomially, compared to the case where the intermediate measurements are deferred to the end of the computation. We also provide a method for implementation of the approach.

</details>


### [280] [Classical Active-Space Hybrid Quantum Subspace Expansion (CASH-QSE): Quantum Corrections without Remeasuring the Classically Calculable Energy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08170)
*Artur F. Izmaylov*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: CASH-QSE is a hybrid quantum-classical approach to efficiently calculate wave functions, boosting accuracy while minimizing measurement costs compared to VQE.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enhance energy estimation in quantum computations by mitigating measurement errors from classical-quantum wave function components.

Method: CASH-QSE combines classical reference energies with quantum states constructed to be orthogonal, streamlining energy calculations without overlap measurements.

Result: CASH-QSE achieves chemical accuracy in energy estimation and reduces measurement complexity, outperforming ADAPT-VQE in certain scenarios.

Conclusion: This method provides an efficient and accurate framework for quantum chemistry problems, particularly in challenging correlation regimes.

Abstract: The wave function prepared by a variational quantum eigensolver (VQE) can contain a substantial classically tractable component, yet its energy contribution is sampled on the quantum device. Representing the wave function as a combination of a classical reference and separately prepared quantum states allows the reference energy to be evaluated without quantum sampling. However, near-linear dependence among these components can amplify measurement errors and destabilize the resulting energy estimate. We introduce Classical Active-Space Hybrid Quantum Subspace Expansion (CASH-QSE), which retains a complete-active-space self-consistent field (CASSCF) reference classically and uses occupation structure to construct quantum states exactly orthogonal to the reference and to one another. The energy follows from an ordinary Hermitian eigenvalue problem without overlap measurements, while the same occupation constraints simplify the measured operators. Using full configuration interaction to guide component selection, we test CASH-QSE along H$_2$O and N$_2$ bond-stretching coordinates spanning weakly to strongly correlated regimes. The benchmarks use STO--3G for both molecules and a restricted cc-pVDZ orbital space for H$_2$O. CASH-QSE reaches chemical accuracy while limiting the largest complete measurement circuits to a few hundred all-to-all logical controlled-NOT gates. In favorable cases, it also reduces the idealized final-energy sampling cost by several orders of magnitude relative to VQE with an adaptive derivative-assembled pseudo-Trotter ansatz (ADAPT-VQE).

</details>


### [281] [Transversal Fanout for Fault Tolerant Distributed Quantum Computing: Analysis and Application](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08233)
*Seng W. Loke*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a resource-efficient method for implementing logical fanout operations in distributed quantum computing by using transversal operations on error-correcting code blocks, reducing non-local communication requirements.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Logical fanout is crucial for distributed quantum computation but is hindered by high communication costs between encoded blocks.

Method: The authors utilize the structure of encoded blocks and transversal logical operations to construct distributed fanout circuits that minimize non-local operations while maintaining logical functionality.

Result: The constructed method effectively reduces physical gate, entanglement, circuit-depth, and ancilla requirements through the use of Bivariate Bicycle (BB)-code blocks, with an additional study on the GCZ global gate implementation.

Conclusion: This systematic approach enhances the feasibility of large logical fanout operations across distributed quantum processors using error correction.

Abstract: We study a resource-efficient approach for implementing logical fanout operations in fault-tolerant distributed quantum computing using transversal operations on quantum error-correcting code blocks. Logical fanout, comprising multiple controlled-NOT operations from a common control qubit to target qubits located at remote nodes, is an important primitive for distributed quantum computation but can require substantial non-local communication when implemented directly between encoded blocks. We exploit the structure of encoded blocks and the availability of transversal logical operations to construct distributed fanout circuits that reduce the required non-local operations while preserving the logical action of the fanout operation. The construction is developed for encoded quantum information and illustrated using Bivariate Bicycle (BB)-code blocks. We analyze the resulting physical gate, entanglement, circuit-depth, and ancilla requirements. The approach provides a systematic method for implementing large logical fanout operations across distributed error-corrected quantum processors. Also, we study a distributed implementation of the global gate GCZ involving logical qubits (encoded using BB-code blocks), exploiting the concurrency in transversal distributed fanouts.

</details>


### [282] [Instantaneous-Frame Theory of Strongly Driven Parametric Gates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08348)
*Kentaro Kubo,Shinichi Inoue,Jushin Tei,Yinghao Ho,Yasunobu Nakamura,Hayato Goto*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a new theoretical framework for describing parametric two-qubit gates in superconducting quantum processors, focusing on instantaneous eigenstates instead of traditional idle eigenstates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the limitations of conventional theories for large-amplitude fast gate operations in superconducting quantum processors.

Method: The authors propose a theory based on coherent rotations between instantaneous eigenstates and apply the non-Abelian Berry connection to describe transitions and effective couplings.

Result: The new theory matches exact numerical simulations across different tunable-coupler architectures, demonstrating accuracy even in regimes where idle-frame descriptions fail.

Conclusion: The study presents a novel instantaneous-frame theory for parametric gates, offering a more accurate description of driven quantum dynamics and reducing reliance on complex simulations.

Abstract: Parametric two-qubit gates using tunable couplers are a promising approach to scalable superconducting quantum processors. However, conventional theories formulated in the idle eigenbasis break down in the large-amplitude regime required for fast gate operations. Here, we show that parametric gates are more naturally and accurately described as coherent rotations between instantaneous eigenstates rather than idle eigenstates. This approach may appear counterintuitive, since instantaneous eigenbases are usually associated with adiabatic dynamics. However, they also form a natural moving frame for driven dynamics, where the non-Abelian Berry connection governs transitions and provides a compact description of the exchange and effective $ZZ$ couplings. We show that this theory achieves quantitative agreement with exact numerical simulations across representative tunable-coupler architectures, including double-transmon, capacitively shunted double-transmon, and single-transmon couplers. The description remains accurate even in regimes where idle-frame descriptions fail, while substantially reducing the reliance on computationally demanding exact simulations. These results establish an instantaneous-frame theory of parametric gates beyond conventional idle-frame models and provide a new perspective on driven quantum dynamics.

</details>


### [283] [Scaling-Enhanced Rapid Readout of a Qubit Ensemble Assisted by High-Frequency Detector Modes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08374)
*Hiroki Nakabayashi,Yuichiro Matsuzaki*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper investigates how readout time in collective quantum measurements scales with the number of qubits, establishing a benchmark and showing how high-frequency modes can enhance performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the scaling behavior of readout times in collective quantum measurements.

Method: The study compares readout times with different types of detectors, assessing one with standard characteristics and another with unbounded high-frequency modes.

Result: A benchmark of $N^{-1/2}$ is established, with detectors having unbounded high-frequency modes achieving a better scaling of $N^{-1/(2-ν)}$, where $ν$ reflects the spectral properties of the detector.

Conclusion: High-frequency modes in detectors offer a significant advantage in collective quantum readout, with their spectral characteristics influencing the efficiency of the readout process.

Abstract: Quantum measurements of large qubit ensembles are often performed indirectly by coupling the ensemble to a detector and subsequently measuring the detector. Despite the importance of rapid collective readout, it remains unclear what determines how the readout time scales with the number of qubits $N$. Here, we first establish a benchmark $N^{-1/2}$ for a broad class of detectors without ultraviolet high-frequency modes. We then show that the detector with unbounded high-frequency modes can surpass this benchmark and yield a characteristic time scaling $N^{-1/(2-ν)}$ for $0 < ν< 2$, where $ν$ characterizes the spectral structure of the detector. Our results establish high-frequency detector modes as a resource for achieving a scaling advantage in collective quantum readout, with the detector spectrum directly controlling the scaling exponent of the readout time.

</details>


### [284] [Non-Markovian dynamics of a qubit on the zigzag edge of photonic graphene](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08382)
*Enrico Di Benedetto*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The study analyzes the dynamics of a qubit in a photonic honeycomb lattice, revealing distinct long-time behavior compared to bulk graphene.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding qubit dynamics in specific lattice structures can enhance quantum information applications.

Method: The authors use a resolvent operator approach and exact lattice Green's functions to study the spectrum of bound states and qubit population scaling laws.

Result: The analysis shows that the zero-energy flat band suppresses certain long-time effects, leading to damped Rabi oscillations rather than branch-cut-induced tails.

Conclusion: The findings highlight the unique characteristics of qubit interactions at the zigzag edge of a honeycomb lattice, with implications for experimental setups.

Abstract: We present an analytical study of the dynamics of a qubit coupled to the zigzag edge of a photonic honeycomb lattice. Leveraging a resolvent operator approach and exact lattice Green's functions, we map the spectrum of qubit-photon bound states and complex poles to uncover the long-time scaling laws of the qubit population. Unlike a qubit coupled to the bulk of graphene, the presence of a zero-energy flat band connecting the Dirac points suppresses long-time branch-cut-induced tails, instead driving damped Rabi oscillations. Potential experimental platforms are briefly discussed.

</details>


### [285] [Dynamics of entanglement in non-Hermitian system of nonreciprocal coupling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08388)
*Jiguo Wu,Shaojie Yang,Xiong Liu,Zhibo Jiang,Zhiyou Zhang,Rongchun Ge*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores non-Hermitian quantum systems, focusing on the interaction of exceptional points (EPs) and their role in enhancing entanglement generation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The recent experimental progress in non-Hermitian systems highlights the need to investigate the interactions and orders of exceptional points for applications in quantum control.

Method: The research employs a non-reciprocally coupled cavity system designed to realize both second and third orders of EPs, enabled by a two-dimensional NH gauged laser array.

Result: A novel quantum continuous entanglement phase transition is identified, demonstrating how multi-EP interactions affect entanglement dynamics and allow for accelerated generation.

Conclusion: The study reveals significant aspects of quantum non-Hermitian physics at higher orders of EPs, paving the way for further research into higher-order degeneracy and dynamic phase transitions in quantum systems.

Abstract: The exploration of the effect of non-Hermitian (NH) in quantum systems is gaining renewed momentum as a result of the recent progress in experiments. It has been indicated that the ability to engineer the orders of exceptional points (EPs) can be fundamental to its applications in quantum control, such as accelerating entanglement generation; however, an initial survey of the interactions between different orders of EPs is still missing. In this work, we try to partially address this issue by employing a non-reciprocally coupled cavity system which can be experimentally realizable with a two-dimensional reconfigurable NH gauged laser array. With deliberate design, our systems display both second and third orders of EPs. The sign of a novel, purely quantum continuous entanglement phase transition is observed, which reveals how multi-EP interactions alter entanglement dynamics across different regimes while maintaining accelerated generation. Our findings reveil the genuinely quantum NH physics of higher-order EPs, which has opened up a new avenue for future investigations of the higher-order degeneracy application and the dynamic phase transitions of quantum systems.

</details>


### [286] [A theoretical study of subcarrier-wave quantum key distribution system integration with an optical transport network utilizing dense wavelength division multiplexing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08389)
*Fedor Kiselev,Natalia Veselkova,Roman Goncharov,Vladimir Egorov*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper analyzes subcarrier-wave quantum key distribution (SCW QKD) in dense wavelength division multiplexing (DWDM) optical networks, focusing on the effects of noise and channel efficiency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To optimize SCW QKD performance in DWDM optical transport networks by understanding the effects of various noise factors and channel isolation.

Method: The paper calculates quantum bit error rates and secure key generation rates for SCW QKD under different DWDM parameters and quantum channel allocations, specifically assessing spontaneous Raman scattering and four-wave mixing issues.

Result: It is determined that using a quantum channel allocation at 1310 nm yields a lower quantum bit error rate relative to allocations at C-band, despite greater fiber attenuation at 1310 nm.

Conclusion: Allocating quantum channels at 1310 nm improves the performance of SCW-QKD by minimizing nonlinear noise, fostering more secure key generation.

Abstract: In this paper we study the performance of subcarrier-wave quantum key distribution (SCW QKD) in the presence of classical channels of optical transport network utilizing dense wavelength division multiplexing (DWDM). We consider the impact of spontaneous Raman scattering noise as well as the four-wave mixing and channel isolation efficiency. We calculate quantum bit error rate as well as the secure key generation rate of SCW-QKD protocol for different parameters of DWDM system and quantum channel allocations. Our calculations show, that quantum channel allocation at the wavelength of 1310 nm is preferable to allocation at C-band due to lower nonlinear noise, despite fiber's larger attenuation at this wavelength.

</details>


### [287] [Performance of the subcarrier-wave quantum key distribution in the presence of spontaneous Raman scattering noise generated by classical DWDM channels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08406)
*Fedor Kiselev,Roman Goncharov,Natalia Veselkova,Eduard Samsonov,Alexei D. Kiselev,Vladimir Egorov*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper analyzes subcarrier-wave quantum key distribution (SCW QKD) performance under spontaneous Raman scattering (SpRS) noise in dense wavelength division multiplexing (DWDM) networks, comparing continuous wave and pulsed regimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the impact of SpRS noise on SCW QKD performance within DWDM networks and identify optimal configurations for secure key generation.
